Most of the literature on groupoids only deals with the so-called Green–Renault twisted actions, with the abelian twist, see
[
57
,
58
]
.
We need to consider here twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
á la Busby–Smith (
[
12
]
), where the twist is not necessarily abelian.
This type of twisted action appeared in
[
17
]
, where they are interpreted as certain functors between
2
-categories.

If
A
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra we denote by
U\mathcal{M}(A)
the unitary group in
\mathcal{M}(A)
, and if
\mathcal{A}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle we write
U\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}):=\{U\mathcal{M}(A_{x})\}_{x\in X}\subseteq\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})
for the corresponding group bundle.
A twisted groupoid action on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
should be viewed as a ‘continuous twisted functor’
\alpha
from
\mathcal{G}
to the category of
*
-isomorphisms between the fibres of
\mathcal{A}
and the twist
u
is a ‘strictly continuous 2-cocycle in
U\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})
’.
A more precise interpretation is given in
[
17
]
where one views twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
as ‘continuous morphisms’ (or weak functors) from
\mathcal{G}
to the
2
-category of
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-algebras whose morphisms are nondegenerate homomorphisms
A\to\mathcal{M}(B)
, and
2
-morphisms are certain unitary multiplier intertwiners between these homomorphisms.
We now make precise exactly what we need.

Definition 4.6
.

A
twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle

\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
is a pair
(\alpha,u)
where

(1)

\alpha=\{\alpha_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
is a family of
*
-isomorphisms
\alpha_{g}:A_{s(g)}\to A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, such that the map
s^{*}\mathcal{A}\ni(a,g)\mapsto(\alpha_{g}(a),g)\in r^{*}\mathcal{A}
is continuous, where
r^{*}\mathcal{A}:=\{(a,g)\in\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{G}:a\in A_{r(g)}\}
and
s^{*}\mathcal{A}:=\{(a,g)\in\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{G}:a\in A_{s(g)}\}
are the pullback bundles;

(2)

u=\{u(g,h)\}_{(g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(2)}}
is a section of the pullback group bundle
r^{(2)*}U\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})=\{(w,(g,h))\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})\times\mathcal{G}^{(2)}:w\in U\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})\},
such that for every continuous section
a
of the pullback bundle
r^{*}\mathcal{A}^{(2)}:=\{((a,g),(b,h))\in r^{*}\mathcal{A}\times r^{*}\mathcal{A}:s(g)=r(h)\}
the formula
au(g,h):=a(g,h)u(g,h)
for
g,h\in\mathcal{G}
defines a continuous section of
r^{*}\mathcal{A}^{(2)}
.

In addition, we require this pair to satisfy the following algebraic conditions

(1)

\alpha_{x}=\id_{A_{x}}
,
u(x,x)=1_{\mathcal{M}(A_{x})}
for all
x\in X
;

(2)

\Ad_{u(g,h)}\circ\alpha_{gh}=\alpha_{g}\circ\alpha_{h}
for all
(g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(2)}
;

(3)

\overline{\alpha}_{f}(u(g,h))u(f,gh)=u(f,g)u(fg,h)
, for all
(f,g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(3)}
;

where
\overline{\alpha}_{f}:\mathcal{M}(A_{s(f)})\to\mathcal{M}(A_{r(f)})
is the unique homomorphic extension of
\alpha_{f}
for
f\in\mathcal{G}
.

Remark 4.7
.

Relations
(1)
–
(3)
have some standard consequences that we will sometimes use without warning.
For instance,
\alpha_{g}^{-1}=\Ad_{u(g^{-1},g)^{*}}\circ\alpha_{g^{-1}}
,
u(g,s(g))=u(r(g),g)=1_{\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})}
and
\alpha_{g}(u(g^{-1},g))=u(g,g^{-1})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

We first note that a twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
induces a twisted inverse semigroup action of
\Bis(\mathcal{G})
in the sense of Sieben
[
63
]
.
This is a special case of the inverse semigroup twisted actions of Buss–Exel
[
14
, Section 5]
, which were also considered in
[
42
, Section 2]
.
The latter actions are more general and cover twists given by groupoid circle extensions, see
[
14
, Theorem 7.2]
or
[
42
, Theorem 2.16]
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
(\alpha,u)
be a twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
.
For any open
U\subseteq X
put
A_{U}:=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{U})
.
Then for any
U,V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, the maps
\alpha_{U}:A_{s(U)}\to A_{r(U)}
and sections
\omega(U,V)\in U\mathcal{M}(A_{r(UV)})=U\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(UV)})
given by the formulas

\alpha_{U}(a)(r(g)):=\alpha_{g}(a(s(g))),\qquad\omega(U,V)(r(g)):=u(g,h),\qquad g\in U,h\in V,r(h)=s(g),

form a twisted inverse semigroup action of
\Bis(\mathcal{G})
on
A=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
, in the sense of
[
63
]
and hence also of
[
14
]
.
In particular, it gives rise to a saturated inverse semigroup Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}_{\sem}^{(\alpha,u)}=\{(a,U):a\in A_{r(U)},\ U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})\}
, where writing
a\delta_{U}
for
(a,U)\in\mathcal{A}_{\sem}
we put

(a\delta_{U})\cdot(b\delta_{V}):=\alpha_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)b)\omega(U,V)\delta_{UV},\quad(a\delta_{U})^{*}=\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega(U^{*},U)^{*}\delta_{U^{*}},

for all
a\in A_{r(U)}
,
b\in A_{r(V)}
,
U,V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
.

Proof.

By
(1)
and
(2)
in Definition
4.6
, the maps
\alpha_{U}
are well-defined isometries, and
\omega(U,V)
are well defined elements of
U\mathcal{M}(A_{UV})
.
Thus we need to check that for any
U,V,W\in S
:

(1)

A_{s(UV)}=A_{s(V)}\cap\alpha_{V}^{-1}(A_{s(U)})
and
\alpha_{U}\circ\alpha_{V}=Ad_{\omega(U,V)}\alpha_{UV}
on this ideal;

(2)

\alpha_{U}\big(a\omega(V,W)\big)\omega(U,VW)=\alpha_{U}(a)\omega(U,V)\omega(UV,W)
for
a\in A_{s(V)}\cap A_{r(VW)}
;

(3)

\omega(U,V)=1_{\mathcal{M}(A_{r(UV)})}
if either
U\subseteq X
or
V\subseteq Y
.

Let
a\in A_{s(UV)}
, and
g\in U
,
h\in V
with
s(g)=r(h)
.
By Definition
4.6
(2)
we get

\begin{split}Ad_{\omega(U,V)}\alpha_{UV}(a)(r(g))&=u(g,h)\alpha_{gh}(a(s(h)))u(g,h)^{*}=\alpha_{g}(\alpha_{h}(a(s(h))))\\
&=\alpha_{g}(\alpha_{V}(a)(s(g)))=\alpha_{U}(\alpha_{V}(a))(r(g)).\end{split}

This implies
(1)
, as the above calculation implies that
A_{r(UV)}=\alpha_{U}(A_{r(V)}\cap A_{s(U)})=\alpha_{U}(A_{r(V)})\cap A_{r(U)}
, which by taking inverses is equivalent to
A_{s(UV)}=A_{s(V)}\cap\alpha_{V}^{-1}(A_{s(U)})
.

To show
(2)
let
a\in A_{s(V)}\cap A_{r(VW)}
and
f\in U
,
g\in V
,
h\in W
with
(f,g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(3)}
.
Using Definition
4.6
(3)
we get

\begin{split}[\alpha_{U}\big(a\omega(V,W)\big)\omega(U,VW)](r(f))&=\alpha_{f}\Big(a(s(f))u(g,h)\Big)u(f,gh)\\
&=\alpha_{f}\Big(a(s(f))\Big)u(f,g)u(fg,h)\\
&=\big(\alpha_{U}(a)\omega(U,V)\omega(UV,W)\big)(r(f)).\end{split}

Property
(3)
follows from Definition
4.6
(1)
,
see also Remark
4.7
.

Every twisted inverse semigroup action naturally yields a saturated Fell bundle over the inverse semigroup, see
[
14
, page 250]
, which in the present context is as described in the assertion.
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

Let
(\alpha,u)
be a twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
.
Write the pullback bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}:=r^{*}\mathcal{A}
, as
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}=\bigsqcup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}A_{g}
where
A_{g}:=A_{r(g)}
for
g\in\mathcal{G}
.
Then
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}
is a Fell bundle over the groupoid
\mathcal{G}
with multiplication and involution given by

\displaystyle A_{g}\times A_{h}\ni(a,b)\longmapsto a\cdot\alpha_{g}(b)u(g,h)\in A_{gh},

\displaystyle A_{g}\ni a\longmapsto u(g^{-1},g)^{*}\alpha_{g^{-1}}(a^{*})=\alpha_{g}^{-1}(a^{*})u(g^{-1},g)^{*}\in A_{g^{-1}}.

Proof.

One could check the conditions in
[
15
, Definition 2.6]
, which is a tedious verification. To avoid that we appeal to Lemma
4.8
and Theorem
4.4
. Namely, by Theorem
4.4
there is a unique Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}
over
\mathcal{G}
, such that
\mathcal{A}_{\sem}^{(\alpha,u)}
is induced by
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}
.
By the description in Remark
4.5
, the bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}
as a Banach bundle can be naturally identified with the pullback bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}:=r^{*}\mathcal{A}
, and then the multiplication and involution have to be as in the statement.
∎

Note that we have in particular
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}|_{X}=\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 4.10
.

If
(\alpha,u)
is a twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
, we define the
full
and the
reduced crossed product
for
\alpha
as the cross-sectional
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-algebras
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
and
C_{\red}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
for the Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}
described in Corollary
4.9
.

More specifically, the full crossed product
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
is the maximal
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-completion of the
*
-algebra
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
where
for
a,b\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, we have

(a*b)(g)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{s(g)}}a(gh^{-1})\alpha_{gh^{-1}}(b(h))u(gh^{-1},h),\quad\quad a^{*}(g)=u(g,g^{-1})^{*}\alpha_{g}(a(g^{-1})^{*}).

The
regular representation

\Lambda
of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
on
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})=\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{A})=\{\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}A_{r(g)}\}_{x\in X}
is determined by the representations
\Lambda_{x}
on the Hilbert
A_{x}
-modules
\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{A})_{x}=\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}A_{r(g)}
,
x\in X
, where
[\xi\cdot a_{x}](g)=\xi(g)\alpha_{g}(a_{x})
,
\left\langle\xi,\zeta\right\rangle_{A_{x}}=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\alpha_{h}^{-1}(\xi(h)^{*}\zeta(h))
, for
a_{x}\in A_{x}
,
\xi,\zeta\in\bigoplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}A_{r(h)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
and

(4.1)

\big(\Lambda_{x}(a)\xi\big)(g)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}a(gh^{-1})\alpha_{gh^{-1}}(\xi(h))u(gh^{-1},h),\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}),\ g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}.

Example 4.11
.

When
\mathcal{G}=G
is a discrete group then a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
is the same as a twisted group action on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\mathcal{A}=A
in the sense of Busby–Smith
[
12
]
; this has been considered in many other papers, for example
[
51
,
6
,
7
,
8
]
, and the algebras
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
and
C_{\red}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
are the usual full and reduced crossed products, respectively, usually also denoted by
A\rtimes_{(\alpha,u)}G
and
A\rtimes_{(\alpha,u),r}G
in the literature.

Example 4.12
.

Twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
on a trivial bundle
\mathcal{A}=\{\mathbb{C}\}_{x\in X}
correspond to continuous groupoid cocycles, i.e. for any such action
(\alpha,u)
we necessarily have
\alpha=\{\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{C}}\}_{x\in X}
, and
u
is a
continuous normalised
2
-cocycle
on
\mathcal{G}
, that is,
u:\mathcal{G}^{(2)}\to\mathbb{T}
is a continuous map satisfying
u\big(r(g),g\big)=1=u\big(g,s(g)\big)
and
u(f,g)u(fg,h)=u(g,h)u(f,gh)
for every composable triple
(f,g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(3)}
.
The algebras
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
and
C_{\red}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
are then the usual full and reduced twisted groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras, usually also written as
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
and
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{G},u)
, see
[
56
]
. In particular, the (left) regular representation (
4.1
) for
x\in X
specialises in this case to a homomorphism
\lambda^{u}_{x}\colon\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x}))
, which on the canonical basis
\{\delta_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\subseteq\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})
is given by

(4.2)

\lambda^{u}_{x}(f)\delta_{h}=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{r(h)}}f(g)u(g,h)\delta_{gh}.

We use the superscript
u
above in order to differentiate
\lambda^{u}
from the ordinary (left) regular representation
\lambda
, which coincides with
\lambda^{u}
if
u=1
is the trivial
2
-cocycle.

Example 4.13
.

Consider a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of a groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
The lifted action
(\alpha^{(n)},u^{(n)})
on
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A})
is given by the isomorphisms
\alpha_{g}^{(n)}
from
\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{s(g)})
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{r(g)})
and unitaries
u(g,h)^{(n)}=u(g,h)\otimes I_{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)}))\cong\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{r(g)}))
.
The algebraic and topological conditions appearing in Definition
4.6
are clearly satisfied by the lifted action.
Further checks show that we have the isomorphisms
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}))\cong\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A})^{(\alpha^{(n)},u^{(n)})})
and
\mathbb{M}_{n}(C_{\red}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}))\cong C_{\red}^{*}(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A})^{(\alpha^{(n)},u^{(n)})})
.
Defining a matrix lift of an abstract Fell bundle is more complicated.
The question relates to the norm on the matrices over fibres, and it can addressed via the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-condition, but we will not need it in this paper.

4.1.
Representations of crossed products

Let us fix a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
, and recall the inverse semigroup Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}_{\sem}^{(\alpha,u)}
from Lemma
4.8
.
For every
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, and
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
we may identify
a\delta_{U}\in\mathcal{A}_{\sem}^{(\alpha,u)}
with an element of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
given by

(a\delta_{U})(g):=[g\in U]a(r(g)),\qquad g\in\mathcal{G}.

The product and involutions in
\mathcal{A}_{\sem}^{(\alpha,u)}
and
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
are compatible, and every element in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
is of the form
\sum_{U\in F}a_{U}\delta_{U}
where
F\subseteq\Bis(\mathcal{G})
is finite and
a_{U}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
,
U\in F
.
This extends to an isomorphism between the algebras
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
and
C_{\red}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
and the full and reduced algebras associated to the inverse semigroup Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}_{\sem}^{(\alpha,u)}
, respectively, see
[
18
, Theorem 5.5]
and
[
15
, Theorem 2.15, Theorem 4.11]
.
In particular, the relation

\psi(\sum_{U\in F}a_{U}\delta_{U})=\sum_{U\in F}\psi_{U}(a_{U}\delta_{U})

gives a bijective correspondence between representations
\psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to B
in a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
B
and families
\{\psi_{U}\}_{U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})}
of linear maps
\psi_{U}:A_{r(U)}\to B
such that for all
a\in A_{r(U)}
,
b\in A_{r(V)}
,
U,V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, we have

(SR1)

\psi_{U}(a\delta_{U})\psi_{V}(b\delta_{V})=\psi_{UV}(\alpha_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a_{U})a_{V})\omega(U,V)\delta_{UV})
;

(SR2)

\psi_{U}(a\delta_{U})^{*}=\psi_{U^{*}}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega(U^{*},U)\delta_{U^{*}})
.

If
H
is a Hilbert space then the relation

\psi(\sum_{U\in F}a_{U}\delta_{U})=\sum_{U\in F}\pi(a_{U})v_{U}

gives a bijective correspondence between representations
\psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to\mathbb{B}(H)
and
covariant representations of
(\alpha,u)
on
H
, i.e. pairs
(\pi,v)
where
\pi:A\to\mathbb{B}(H)
is a representation (recall that
A
denotes the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra associated with the bundle
\mathcal{A}
) and
\{v_{U}\}_{U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})}\subseteq\mathbb{B}(H)
is a family of partial isometries satisfying:

(CR1)

v_{U}\pi(a)v_{U}^{*}=\pi(\alpha_{U}(a))
for all
a\in A_{s(U)}
,
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
;

(CR2)

the ranges of
v_{U}
and
v_{U}^{*}
are
\overline{\pi(A_{r(U)})H}
and
\overline{\pi(A_{s(U)})H}
, for all
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
;

(CR3)

\pi(a)v_{U}v_{V}=\pi(a\omega(U,V))v_{UV}
for all
a\in A_{r(UV)}
,
U,V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
.

5.
Equivariant correspondences

Throughout this section
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
will be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X:=\mathcal{G}^{(0)}
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}

(where
\mathcal{G}
is an étale locally compact Hausdorff groupoid).
In the untwisted case, the following notion was called an
equivariant representation
of
\mathcal{G}
by Le Gall in
[
46
, Definition 4.6]
.
Bédos and Conti
[
7
,
8
]
follow this naming, while
Echterhoff, Kaliszewski, Quigg and Raeburn
[
25
, Definition 2.5]
call such an object a
compatible right-Hilbert module
(in the context of group actions).
Deaconu
[
23
, Definition 5.10]
calls it a
groupoid action on a
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
.
Here we extend this notion to twisted actions, and call it an equivariant correspondence.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
be twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, respectively. An
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})

equivariant correspondence
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
endowed with an
equivariant representation
of
\mathcal{G}
, meaning a family
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
satisfying

(1)

for each
g\in\mathcal{G}
the map
L_{g}:E_{s(g)}\to E_{r(g)}
is an invertible isometry,
L_{x}=\mathrm{id}_{E_{x}}
for
x\in X
, and

L_{g}(L_{h}(\xi))=u_{\alpha}(g,h)L_{gh}(\xi)u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*},\qquad\xi\in E_{s(h)},\,(g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(2)};

(2)

for
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
\xi,\zeta\in E_{s(g)}
,
a\in A_{s(g)}
,
b\in B_{s(g)}
we have

\qquad L_{g}(a\xi)=\alpha_{g}(a)(L_{g}\xi),\qquad L_{g}(\xi b)=L_{g}(\xi)\beta_{g}(b),\qquad\beta_{g}(\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B_{s(g)}})=\langle L_{g}\xi,L_{g}\zeta\rangle_{B_{r(g)}};

(3)

for each
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
the section
\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\langle\xi(r(g)),L_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}\in r^{*}\mathcal{B}
is continuous.

Remark 5.2
.

The relations in Definition
5.1
imply that
L_{g}^{-1}(\cdot)=u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*}L_{g^{-1}}(\cdot)u_{\beta}(g^{-1},g)
,
L_{g}^{-1}(\alpha_{g}(a)\xi)=aL_{g}^{-1}(\xi)
,
L_{g}^{-1}(\xi\beta_{g}(b))=L_{g}^{-1}(\xi)b
and
\beta_{g}^{-1}(\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B_{s(g)}})=\langle L_{g}^{-1}\xi,L_{g}^{-1}\zeta\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}
for all
\xi,\zeta\in E_{s(g)}
,
a\in A_{s(g)}
,
b\in B_{s(g)}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Condition
(3)
in Definition
5.1
, assuming
(2)
, is equivalent to continuity of

(5.1)

s^{*}\mathcal{E}\supseteq E_{s(g)}\ni\xi\longmapsto L_{g}(\xi)\in E_{r(g)}\in r^{*}\mathcal{E}.

Proof.

Clearly, continuity of (
5.1
) implies
(3)
in Definition
5.1
.
Conversely, assume
(3)
and the last relation in
(2)
in Definition
5.1
.
The latter implies that all maps
L_{g}:E_{s(g)}\to E_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, are isometries and for all
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
, the section

\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\beta_{g}(\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B_{s(g)}})=\langle L_{g}\xi,L_{g}\zeta\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}\in B_{r(g)}\subseteq r^{*}\mathcal{B}

is continuous (because
\beta
and the inner product are ‘continuous’).
Let
(\xi_{i})_{i\in I}
be a net of elements of
s^{*}\mathcal{E}
convergent to
\xi_{0}\in s^{*}\mathcal{E}
.
This means that
g_{i}:=p(\xi_{i})\to g:=p(\xi_{0})
in
\mathcal{G}
and for every
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
we have
\|\xi_{i}-\xi(g_{i})\|\to\|\xi_{0}-\xi(g)\|
.
Choose
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
and
\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})
such that
\xi(g)=\xi_{0}
and
\zeta(g)=L_{g}(\xi_{0})
.
Then

\|L_{g_{i}}(\xi_{i})-\zeta(g_{i})\|\leq\|\xi_{i}-\xi(g_{i})\|+\|L_{g_{i}}(\xi(g_{i}))-\zeta(g_{i})\|

where the first summand tends to zero because
\xi_{0}=\xi(g)
.
To see the same for
\|L_{g_{i}}(\xi(g_{i}))-\zeta(g_{i})\|
note that its square is the norm of the following element of
B_{g_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
:

\langle L_{g_{i}}(\xi(g_{i})),L_{g_{i}}(\xi(g_{i}))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}-\langle L_{g_{i}}(\xi(g_{i})),\zeta(g_{i}),\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}-\langle\zeta(g_{i}),L_{g_{i}}(\xi(g_{i}))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}+\langle\zeta(g_{i}),\zeta(g_{i})\rangle_{B_{r(g)}},

which tends to

\langle\zeta(g),\zeta(g)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}-\langle\zeta(g),\zeta(g)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}-\langle\zeta(g),\zeta(g)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}+\langle\zeta(g),\zeta(g)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}=0

by continuity of all the maps involved.
This proves that
L_{g_{i}}(\xi_{i})\to L_{g}(\xi_{0})
in
r^{*}\mathcal{E}
.
∎

For a twisted groupoid action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
on
\mathcal{A}
we may think of a pair
(\overline{\alpha},u_{\alpha})
, where
\overline{\alpha}:=\{\overline{\alpha}_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
is a family of standard strict extensions of the
*
-isomorphisms
\alpha=\{\alpha_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
, as a ‘strictly continuous’ twisted action on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})
.
Then any equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
extends to a ‘strictly continuous’ equivariant representation between the extended actions as follows.

Proposition 5.4
.

Any
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
extends to a ‘strictly continuous’
(\overline{\alpha},u_{\alpha})
-
(\overline{\beta},u_{\beta})
equivariant representation
\overline{L}=\{\overline{L}_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E})
, in the sense that each isometry
L_{g}
extends uniquely to an isometry
\overline{L}_{g}:\mathcal{M}(E_{s(g)})\to\mathcal{M}(E_{r(g)})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, such that
\overline{L}=\{\overline{L}_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
satisfies the obvious analogues of conditions
(1)
and
(2)
in Definition
5.1
, and the following analogue of
(3)
:

(3’)

the section
\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\langle\xi(r(g)),a(r(g))\overline{L}_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}\in B_{r(g)}\subseteq r^{*}\mathcal{B}
is continuous for all strictly continuous sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
and any
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

For each
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
(L_{g},\alpha_{g},\beta_{g})
is an isomorphism from the
A_{s(g)}
-
B_{s(g)}
-bimodule
E_{s(g)}
onto the
A_{r(g)}
-
B_{r(g)}
-bimodule
E_{r(g)}
.
Hence by Lemma
1.6
,
L_{g}
extends uniquely to a linear map
\overline{L}_{g}:\mathcal{M}(E_{s(g)})\to\mathcal{M}(E_{r(g)})
such that

\overline{L}_{g}(a\xi)=\overline{\alpha}_{g}(a)(\overline{L}_{g}\xi),\qquad\overline{L}_{g}(\xi b)=\overline{L}_{g}(\xi)\overline{\beta}_{g}(b),

for all
a\in\mathcal{M}(A_{s(g)})
,
b\in\mathcal{M}(B_{s(g)})
,
\xi\in\mathcal{M}(E_{s(g)})
.
Moreover,
\overline{L}_{g}
is necessarily an invertible isometry, and it is strictly continuous.
Strict continuity readily implies that the remaining conditions in Definition
5.1
(1)
,
(2)
remain valid when
L
is replaced by
\overline{L}
.
To show
(3’)
note that any
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
may be written as the product
a=bc
for
b,c\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
.
Then for
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
we have

\langle\xi(r(g)),a\overline{L}_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}=\langle b^{*}(r(g))\xi(r(g)),L_{g}(\alpha_{g^{-1}}(c(r(g)))\zeta(s(g)))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}\in B_{r(g)}

and the section
\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\langle\xi(r(g)),a(r(g))\overline{L}_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}\in r^{*}\mathcal{B}
is continuous because
b^{*}\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
and for each open bisection
U\subseteq\mathcal{G}
the map
s(U)\ni s(g)\mapsto\alpha_{g^{-1}}(a(r(g)))\zeta(s(g))
is a continuous section of
\mathcal{E}|_{s(U)}
.
∎

Example 5.5
.

For a twisted group action
(A,G,\alpha,u)
, that is, when
X=\{e\}
and
\mathcal{G}=G
is a discrete group, the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle is a single
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
E
over
A
and
(\alpha,u)
-
(\alpha,u)
equivariant representations of
G
on
E
, as in Definition
5.1
, generalise equivariant representations considered in
[
7
]
to the non-unital case.

Example 5.6
.

When the bundle
\mathcal{A}=\{\mathbb{C}\}_{x\in X}
is trivial, so that a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
on
\mathcal{A}
reduces to a continuous groupoid
2
-cocycle
u
(i.e.
\alpha
is trivial, see Example
4.12
), then
(\alpha,u)
-
(\alpha,u)
equivariant correspondences are precisely
continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundles
, that is, continuous bundles
\mathcal{H}=\{H_{x}\}_{x\in X}
of Hilbert spaces together with a continuous groupoid homomorphism
L
from
\mathcal{G}
to the unitary groupoid of
\mathcal{H}
, meaning that
L_{g}\colon H_{s(g)}\!\to H_{r(g)}
are unitary isomorphisms satisfying
L_{g}L_{h}=L_{gh}
for all
(g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{2}
, and such that for continuous sections
\xi,\eta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{H})
, the map

g\longmapsto\langle\xi(r(g))\,|\,\mathopen{}L_{g}(\eta(s(g)))\rangle

is continuous on
\mathcal{G}
.

In particular, we have the
left
and
right regular continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundles

(\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}^{x})\}_{x\in X},\lambda)
and
(\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},\rho)
,
where the topology is induced by the functions from
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
, and for each
g\in\mathcal{G}
the unitaries

\lambda_{g}\colon\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}^{s(g)})\!\to\!\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}),\qquad\rho_{g}\colon\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{s(g)})\!\to\!\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}),

are given by

\lambda_{g}(\delta_{h}):=\delta_{gh},\qquad\rho_{g}(\delta_{h}):=\delta_{hg^{-1}}.

The inverse operation in
\mathcal{G}
induces a unitary equivalence between these bundles.
The relationship between the equivariant regular representations of
\mathcal{G}
introduced here and the usual regular representations of the associated
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra, as discussed in Example
4.12
, does not appear to be fully understood. This issue is related to the next remark.

Remark 5.7
.

Equivariant correspondences in Example
5.6
do not depend on the cocycle
u
.
This is compatible with
[
8
, Remark 3.2]
.
We also note that our notion of equivariant representation only deals with the “continuous part” of the representation theory of
\mathcal{G}
.
This idea was already considered by Paterson in
[
52
]
, while in
[
55
]
general measurable Hilbert bundles are also taken into account by Ramsay and Walter in order to define Fourier–Stieltjes algebras of general locally compact groupoids.

Example 5.8
.

Consider a
2
-cocycle
u\in Z^{2}(G,\mathbb{T})
on a group
G
and the trivial action
\id
of
G
on
A=\mathbb{C}
, so that
(\id,u)
is a twisted action of
G
on
\mathbb{C}
.
To the cocycle
u
we can assign its left
u
-representation
\lambda^{u}\colon G\to\mathcal{L}(\ell^{2}(G))
by
\lambda^{u}_{g}(\delta_{h}):=u(g,h)\delta_{gh}
(which is a special case of (
4.2
)).
This is a unitary (projective)
u
-representation in the sense that
\lambda^{u}_{g}\lambda^{u}_{h}=u(g,h)\lambda^{u}_{gh}
for all
g,h\in G
.
Viewing
\ell^{2}(G)
as a correspondence from
\mathcal{K}(\ell^{2}(G))
to
\mathbb{C}
, we may view
(\ell^{2}(G),\lambda^{u})
as an equivariant correspondence from the (untwisted)
G
-action
(\mathcal{K}(\ell^{2}(G)),\Ad\lambda^{u})
to the twisted action
(\mathbb{C},\id,u)
.
This is an
equivariant Morita equivalence
.
Indeed, at least in the separable case every twisted group action is Morita equivalent to an ordinary action by the Packer–Raeburn stabilisation trick
[
51
]
.
A version of this also holds for twisted groupoid actions, see
[
17
, Theorem 5.3]
.

The following canonical constructions apply to equivariant correspondences:

Example 5.9
.

Any twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}
can be viewed as the
(\alpha,u)
-
(\alpha,u)
-equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on the trivial bundle of
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondences
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{A}
where
L=\alpha
.
We call it
the trivial equivariant representation
of
(\alpha,u)
.

Example 5.10

(Tensoring with Hilbert bundles)
.

We may tensor any
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
with any continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundle
(\mathcal{H},\ell)=(\{H_{x}\}_{x\in X},\{\ell_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}})
to get another
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation
L\otimes\ell
on
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{H}
.
Namely, for each
x\in X
the external tensor product
E_{x}\otimes H_{x}
is naturally a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A_{x}\otimes\mathbb{C}\cong A_{x}
to
B_{x}\otimes\mathbb{C}\cong B_{x}
, see
[
45
]
.
We equip
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{H}:=\{E_{x}\otimes H_{x}\}_{x\in X}
with the unique topology such that the sections
X\ni x\to\xi(x)\otimes h(x)\in E_{x}\otimes H_{x}
are continuous for all
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
and
h\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})
.
Then the maps

L_{g}\otimes\ell_{g}(\xi\otimes h):=L_{g}(\xi)\otimes\ell_{g}(h),\qquad\xi\in E_{s(g)},h\in H_{s(g)},\ g\in\mathcal{G},

satisfy the conditions in Definition
5.1
.
Tensoring
(\mathcal{E},L)
with left or right regular Hilbert bundles, viewed as a
\mathcal{G}
-bundle, see Example
5.6
, we get equivariant correspondences
equivalent to a
regularised
version of
L
, see Definition
6.8
and Example
6.12
below.

Example 5.11

(Amplifications of equivariant correspondences)
.

Let
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
be twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, respectively.
Let
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})

equivariant representation
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
.
Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then
L^{(n)}=\{L_{g}^{(n)}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
, with
L_{g}^{(n)}:\mathbb{M}_{n}(E_{s(g)})\to\mathbb{M}_{n}(E_{r(g)})
being the entry-wise matrix lifting of
L_{g}
becomes an
(\alpha^{(n)},u_{\alpha}^{(n)})
-
(\beta^{(n)},u_{\beta}^{(n)})

equivariant representation
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{E})
from
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A})
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{B})
(see Examples
3.6
and
4.13
).
Once again checking the algebraic and topological conditions is straightforward; moreover the strict extensions
\overline{L_{g}^{{}_{(n)}}}
constructed in Proposition
5.4
are the expected matrix liftings of
\overline{L}_{g}
.

Example 5.12

(Tensor product of equivariant correspondences)
.

Let
L
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
and
K
a
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-
(\gamma,u_{\gamma})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{F}
where
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
,
(\beta,u_{\beta})
and
(\gamma,u_{\gamma})
are twisted actions on
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathcal{C}
respectively.
In such a situation we will say that
L
and
K
are ‘composable’.
We have an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\gamma,u_{\gamma})
equivariant representation
L\otimes K
on the tensor product
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{F}
,
discussed in Example
3.10
, determined by

(L_{g}\otimes K_{g})(\xi\otimes\zeta):=(L_{g}\xi)\otimes(K_{g}\zeta),\qquad\xi\in E_{s(g)},\ \zeta\in F_{s(g)},\ g\in\mathcal{G}.

Indeed, let us first note that
L_{g}\otimes K_{g}
extends to an isometry from
E_{s(g)}\otimes_{B_{s(g)}}F_{s(g)}
to
E_{r(g)}\otimes_{B_{r(g)}}F_{r(g)}
because it satisfies the last relation from
(2)
in Definition
5.1
on elementary tensors:

\begin{split}\gamma_{g}(\langle\xi_{1}\otimes\zeta_{1},\xi_{2}\otimes\zeta_{2}\rangle_{C_{s(g)}})&=\gamma_{g}(\langle\zeta_{1},\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{B_{s(g)}}\zeta_{2}\rangle_{C_{s(g)}})\\
&=\langle K_{g}\zeta_{1},K_{g}(\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{B_{s(g)}}\zeta_{2})\rangle_{C_{r(g)}}\\
&=\langle K_{g}\zeta_{1},\beta_{g}(\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{B_{s(g)}})K_{g}\zeta_{2}\rangle_{C_{r(g)}}\\
&=\langle K_{g}\zeta_{1},\langle L_{g}\xi_{1},L_{g}\xi_{2}\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}K_{g}\zeta_{2}\rangle_{C_{r(g)}}\\
&=\langle(L_{g}\xi_{1})\otimes(K_{g}\zeta_{1}),(L_{g}\xi_{2})\otimes(K_{g}\zeta_{2})\rangle_{C_{r(g)}}\\
&=\langle(L_{g}\otimes K_{g})\xi_{1}\otimes\zeta_{1},(L_{g}\otimes K_{g})\xi_{2}\otimes\zeta_{2}\rangle_{C_{r(g)}}.\end{split}

The remaining relations in
(2)
in Definition
5.1
are immediate, and the relation in
(1)
holds because we consider balanced tensor products.
Condition
(3)
holds because the section of
r^{*}\mathcal{C}
sending
g\in\mathcal{G}
to
\langle(L_{g}\otimes K_{g})\xi_{1}\otimes\zeta_{1},\xi_{2}\otimes\zeta_{2}\rangle_{A_{r(g)}}=\langle L_{g}\zeta_{1},\langle K_{g}\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}\zeta_{2}\rangle_{C_{r(g)}}
is continuous for every
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
,
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{F})
.

Moreover, it is straightforward to see that the extension
\overline{L_{g}\otimes K_{g}}:\mathcal{M}(E_{s(g)}\otimes_{B_{s(g)}}F_{s(g)})\to\mathcal{M}(E_{r(g)}\otimes_{B_{r(g)}}F_{r(g)})
of
L_{g}\otimes K_{g}
, as described in Proposition
5.4
, is given on elementary tensors defined in Lemma
1.12
by

\overline{L_{g}\otimes K_{g}}(\xi\otimes\zeta)=\overline{L}_{g}(\xi)\otimes\overline{K}_{g}(\zeta),\qquad\xi\in\mathcal{M}(E_{r(g)}),\,\zeta\in\mathcal{M}(F_{r(g)}).

Example 5.13

(Direct sums of of equivariant correspondences)
.

For any family of
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representations
\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of the groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles
\{\mathcal{E}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
the direct sum
\oplus_{i\in I}L_{i}:=\{\oplus_{i\in I}(L_{i})_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
is a well-defined
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on the direct sum
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\oplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i}
.
Also we have a natural embedding
\oplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}_{i})\subseteq\mathcal{M}(\oplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i})
, which is equality when
I
is finite, and the extended action
\overline{\oplus_{i\in I}L_{i}}
restricted to
\oplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}_{i})
coincides with
\{\oplus_{i\in I}(\overline{L_{i}})_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
.

5.1.
Representations induced by equivariant correspondences

Equivariant representations allow us to transfer representations of the crossed product for
(\beta,u_{\beta})
to the crossed product for
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
.
Firstly, let us notice that any such equivariant representation
(\mathcal{E},L)
can be interpreted in terms of inverse semigroups as follows.
For every open
V\subseteq X
we put
E_{V}:=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E}|_{V})
.
For every
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
we have an isometry
L_{U}:E_{s(U)}\to E_{r(U)}
where

L_{U}(\xi)(r(g))=L_{g}(\xi(s(g))),\qquad g\in U,\ \xi\in E_{s(U)},

see Lemma
5.3
.
Conditions
(1)
and
(2)
in Definition
5.1
imply that

L_{U}(L_{V}(\xi))=\omega_{\alpha}(U,V)L_{UV}(\xi)\omega_{\beta}(U,V)^{*}

for
\xi\in E_{s(UV)}
, and for
\xi,\zeta\in E_{s(U)}
,
a\in A_{s(U)}
and
b\in B_{s(U)}
we have

L_{U}(a\xi)=\alpha_{U}(a)L_{U}(\xi),\qquad L_{U}(\xi b)=L_{U}(\xi)\beta_{U}(b),\qquad\beta_{U}(\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B_{s(U)}})=\langle L_{U}(\xi),L_{U}(\zeta)\rangle_{B_{r(U)}}.

In particular, for any
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
and
a\in A_{r(U)}
we have a naturally defined element
aL_{U}(\xi)\in E_{r(U)}
, which agrees with
L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\xi)
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
and let
\psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
be a representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
on a right Hilbert
C
-module
\mathcal{H}
.
Let
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}\mathcal{H}
be the tensor product of
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondences where the left action of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
on
\mathcal{H}
is given by
\psi
.
Then we get a representation
L\dashind(\psi):\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}\mathcal{H})
determined by the formula

L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\,\xi\otimes\zeta:=aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes\psi(\delta_{U})\zeta,\qquad\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E}),\zeta\in\mathcal{H},a\in A_{r(U)},U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G}),

where
aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes\psi(\delta_{U})\zeta:=\lim_{i}L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\xi)\otimes\psi(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})\zeta
for an approximate unit
\{e_{i}^{r(U)}\}_{i}
in
B_{r(U)}
.
This limit exists and does not depend on the choice of
\{e_{i}\}_{i}
.

Proof.

If
H
were a Hilbert space (equivalently
C=\mathbb{C}
), then we could disintegrate
\psi
into a covariant representation
(\pi,v)
, as described in Subsection
4.1
, and then put
L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\,\xi\otimes\zeta=aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes v_{U}\zeta
where the corresponding partial isometry
v_{U}\in\mathbb{B}(H)
is given by the strong limit of
\psi(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})
.
Here we work with a general Hilbert module
\mathcal{H}
(with a view towards one of our versions of the Fell absorption principle — see Theorem
6.15
below) where such a strong limit does not necessarily exist, so we need to struggle with approximate units.

Let us first note that the limit
\lim_{i}L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\xi)\otimes\psi(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})\zeta
exists and does not depend on the choice of the approximate unit.
Indeed, we may write
aL_{U}(\xi)=\kappa b
where
\kappa\in E_{r(U)}
and
b\in B_{r(U)}
, and then
\lim_{i}aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes\psi(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})\zeta=\lim_{i}\kappa\otimes\psi(be_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})\zeta=\kappa\otimes\psi(b\delta_{U})\zeta
.
Let
\xi_{j}\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\zeta_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
.
A simple calculation shows that for
j,k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
we have

\langle L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\,\xi_{j}\otimes\zeta_{j},L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\,\xi_{k}\otimes\zeta_{k}\rangle_{C}=\langle\zeta_{j},\psi(\langle\xi_{j},\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*}a)\xi_{k}\rangle)\zeta_{k}\rangle_{C}.

Since
\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\langle\zeta_{j},\psi(\langle\xi_{j},\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*}a)\xi_{k}\rangle)\zeta_{k}\rangle_{C}\leq\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\langle\zeta_{j},\psi(\langle\xi_{j},\|a^{*}a\|\xi_{k}\rangle_{C})\zeta_{k}\rangle
it follows that

\Bigg\|L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\otimes\zeta_{i}\right)\Bigg\|^{2}\leq\|a\|^{2}\Bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\otimes\zeta_{i}\Bigg\|^{2}.

Hence
L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})
is a well defined contractive map on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}\mathcal{H}
.
As in Remark
5.2
we get
L_{U}^{-1}(\xi)=\omega_{\alpha}(U^{*},U)^{*}L_{U^{*}}(\xi)\omega_{\beta}(U^{*},U)
for
\xi\in E_{r(U)}
.
Thus for
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in E=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
we have

\begin{split}\langle aL_{U}(\xi),\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B}&=\lim_{i}\langle aL_{U}(\xi),e_{i}^{r(U)}\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B}=\lim_{i}\langle L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\xi),L_{U}(L_{U}^{-1}(e_{i}^{r(U)}\xi^{\prime}))\rangle_{B_{r(U)}}\\
&=\lim_{i}\beta_{U}\Big(\langle\xi,\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})L_{U}^{-1}(e_{i}^{r(U)}\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{B_{s(U)}}\Big)\\
&=\beta_{U}\Big(\langle\xi,\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega_{\alpha}(U^{*},U)^{*}L_{U^{*}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{B_{s(U)}}\omega_{\beta}(U^{*},U)\Big).\end{split}

Hence

\begin{split}\lim_{i}(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})^{*}\langle aL_{U}(\xi),\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B}&=\lim_{i}\beta_{U}^{-1}(e_{i}^{r(U)})\omega_{\beta}(U^{*},U)^{*}\delta_{U^{*}}\langle aL_{U}(\xi),\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B}\\
&=\beta_{U}^{-1}(\langle aL_{U}(\xi),\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B})\omega_{\beta}(U^{*},U)^{*}\delta_{U^{*}}\\
&=\langle\xi,\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega_{\alpha}(U^{*},U)^{*}L_{U^{*}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{B_{s(U)}}\delta_{U^{*}}.\end{split}

Using this for
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
we get

\begin{split}\langle aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes\psi(\delta_{U})\zeta,\xi^{\prime}\otimes\zeta^{\prime}\rangle_{C}&=\lim_{i}\langle\psi(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})\zeta,\psi(\langle aL_{U}(\xi),\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B})\zeta^{\prime}\rangle_{C}\\
&=\lim_{i}\langle\zeta,\psi((e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})^{*}\langle aL_{U}(\xi),\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B})\zeta^{\prime}\rangle_{C}\\
&=\langle\zeta,\psi(\langle\xi,\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega_{\alpha}(U^{*},U)^{*}L_{U^{*}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{B_{s(U)}}\delta_{U^{*}})\zeta^{\prime}\rangle_{C}\\
&=\langle\xi\otimes\zeta,\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega_{\alpha}(U^{*},U)^{*}L_{U^{*}}(\xi^{\prime})\otimes\psi(\delta_{U^{*}})\zeta^{\prime}\rangle_{C}.\end{split}

This shows that
L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\in\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}\mathcal{H})
and

L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})^{*}=L\dashind(\psi)(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a^{*})\omega_{\alpha}(U^{*},U)^{*}\delta_{U^{*}})=L\dashind(\psi)((a\delta_{U})^{*}).

Now if
b\in A_{r(V)}
for
V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, then

\begin{split}L\dashind(\psi)&(a\delta_{U})[L\dashind(\psi)(b\delta_{V})\xi\otimes\zeta]=\lim_{i}\lim_{j}aL_{U}(bL_{V}(\xi))\otimes\psi(e_{i}^{r(U)}\delta_{U})\psi(e_{j}^{r(V)}\delta_{V})\zeta\\
&=\lim_{i}\lim_{j}\alpha_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)b)L_{U}(L_{V}(\xi))\otimes\psi\Big(\beta_{U}\big(\beta_{U}^{-1}(e_{i}^{r(U)})e_{j}^{r(V)}\big)\omega_{\beta}(U,V)\delta_{UV}\Big)\zeta\\
&=\lim_{i}\alpha_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)b)\omega_{\alpha}(U,V)L_{UV}(\xi)\otimes\psi\Big(\omega_{\beta}(U,V)^{*}e_{i}^{r(UV)}\omega_{\beta}(U,V)\delta_{UV}\Big)\zeta\\
&=L\dashind(\psi)[(a\delta_{U})\cdot(b\delta_{V})](\xi\otimes\zeta).\end{split}

Thus
L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{U})\cdot L\dashind(\psi)(b\delta_{V})=L\dashind(\psi)((a\delta_{U})\cdot(b\delta_{V}))
,
and the assertion now follows, cf. Subsection
4.1
.
∎

Example 5.15
.

For group actions on unital
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-algebras as in
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
]
, the above proposition takes the following form, see
[
6
, Lemma 4.9]
for its progenitor.
Let
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
be two twisted actions of a group
G
on unital
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-algebras
A
and
B
, let
L
be an equivariant representation on an
A
-
B
-
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-correspondence
E
.
Given a representation
\psi\colon B\rtimes_{(\beta,u_{\beta})}G\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
on a Hilbert
C
-module
\mathcal{H}
, there is a representation
L\dashind(\psi)\colon A\rtimes_{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}G\to\mathcal{L}(E\otimes_{\psi}\mathcal{H})
which on the generators
a\delta_{g}\in A\rtimes_{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}G
,
a\in A
,
g\in G
, is given by the formula

L\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{g})(\xi\otimes\zeta)=aL_{g}(\xi)\otimes\psi(\delta_{g})\zeta,\quad\xi\in E,\eta\in\mathcal{H}.

6.
Induced regular representations and absorption principles

In this section we will discuss induced/regular representations of bundle
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras related to twisted groupoid actions.
Once again we first discuss the case of general Fell bundles.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be a Fell bundle over
\mathcal{G}
.
We begin this section by introducing various forms of “regular induced representations” for
\mathcal{A}
using the
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
-
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{X})
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}))
, and a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle from
\mathcal{A}|_{X}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
to another
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{x}\}_{x\in X}
.
These do not seem to have appeared explicitly in the literature.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}=\{E_{x}\}_{x\in X}
be a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle from
\mathcal{A}|_{X}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
to
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{x}\}_{x\in X}
.
The
regular representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
induced by
\mathcal{E}
is given by the left action
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}:=\Lambda\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})}
of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
on the
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
-
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}))\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{X})}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
(see Example
3.10
).

Remark 6.2
.

By construction
\ker\Lambda\subseteq\ker\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
and we have
\ker\Lambda=\ker\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
whenever
\mathcal{E}
is
faithful
, i.e. the left action of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{X})
on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
is faithful (this holds when there is a dense set of points
X_{0}\subseteq X
such that the
A_{x}
-
B_{x}
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
E_{x}
is faithful for all
x\in X_{0}
).
Hence
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
factors through a representation
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}_{\red}
of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A})
and
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}_{\red}
is faithful if
\mathcal{E}
is so.

Remark 6.3
.

We give an alternative description of
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
as follows.
For each
x\in X
, we have a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{G}_{x}})\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}
from
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
to
B_{x}
.
As a right Hilbert module it is equal to the direct sum
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x})
of Hilbert
B_{x}
-modules
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}:=\{A_{g}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}
.
The multiplication of
\mathcal{A}
yields bounded linear maps
A_{gh^{-1}}\otimes_{A_{x}}A_{h}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}\to A_{g}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}
,
g,h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
, that we write again as ‘multiplication’ maps whenever
s(g)=s(h)
.
Then the formula

(6.1)

[\Lambda_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}(a)\xi](g):=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}a(gh^{-1})\cdot\xi(h),\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}),\ \xi\in\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}),\ g\in\mathcal{G}_{x},

makes sense and gives the
*
-homomorphism
\Lambda_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}))
induced from
\Lambda_{x}
by
E_{x}
.
We equip the bundle
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E}):=\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x})\}_{x\in X}
with the topology generated by declaring sections of the form
x\mapsto\oplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi(g)
to be continuous for all
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
, i.e. requesting that
\mathcal{G}\ni g\mapsto\xi(g)\in E_{s(g)}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
is continuous.
Then we get an isomorphism of Hilbert
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
-modules

\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}))\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{X})}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E}))

that maps
a\otimes\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}))\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{X})}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
to
\widehat{a\otimes\xi}\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E}))
where
\widehat{a\otimes\xi}(x)=a(x)\otimes\xi(x)
.
Under this isomorphism the
induced regular representation
is
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E})))
, where

(\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}(a)\xi)(x)=\Lambda_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}(a)\xi(x)=\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}a(gh^{-1})\cdot\xi(h),

a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}),\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E}))
,
x\in X
.

We will now specify the context to Fell bundles coming from twisted groupoid actions.
Assume then now that
\mathcal{A}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle over
X
which carries a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Induced regular representations of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
can be described as follows.
To any
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
we associate the
regularised
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle

\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})=\{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}\}_{x\in X}
whose fibres are direct sums

\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}=\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x}

of
A_{x}
-
B_{x}
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondences
E_{x}
.
We equip
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
with the unique topology making it a Banach bundle and such that
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
contains
x\mapsto\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi(g)
for
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
.
Then
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
is an
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle.
Alternatively, the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
is determined by the
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
to
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
given by the completion of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
with structure determined by the operations

\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}(x):=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\langle\xi(h),\zeta(h)\rangle_{B_{x}},\qquad a\cdot\xi\cdot b(g):=a(s(g))\xi(g)b(s(g))

for
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
,
a\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
,
b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
,
x\in X
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
(\alpha,u)
be a twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}
and let
\mathcal{E}
be an
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle for another
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{B}
over
X
.
The induced regular representation
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
is naturally unitarily equivalent to a representation
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})))
, where

(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(a)\xi)(x)=\pi_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}(a)\xi(x),\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}),\ \xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})),\ x\in X,

and
\pi_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to\mathcal{L}(\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x})
is a representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
on the Hilbert
B_{x}
-module
\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x}
, given by

[\pi_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}(a)\xi](g):=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\alpha_{g}^{-1}\left(a(gh^{-1})u(gh^{-1},h)\right)\xi(h),\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}),\ \xi\in\bigoplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x},\ g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}.

In particular,
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}
descends to a representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
, which is faithful if
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}
is faithful on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

We use Remark
6.3
to treat
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
as a representation on the bundle
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E}):=\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x})\}_{x\in X}
where
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}:=\{A_{g}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}
for
x\in X
. For each
g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
, we have an isomorphism
A_{g}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x}\cong E_{x}
of
B_{x}
-Hilbert modules given by
a\otimes\xi\mapsto\alpha_{g}^{-1}(a)\xi
.
These isomorphisms give a unitary isomorphism
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{A_{x}}E_{x})\cong\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x}
under which (
6.1
) translates to the formula for
\pi_{x}^{\mathcal{E}}(a)
as in the assertion.
These unitaries yield an isomorphism
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}|_{X}}\mathcal{E})\cong\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
of Hilbert module bundles that intertwines
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
.
∎

Example 6.5

(Regular representation revisited)
.

Suppose that
(\alpha,u)
is a twisted
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
.
Then the bundle
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
can be viewed as a faithful
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{A}
.
Hence, by Proposition
6.4
, it induces a representation
\pi^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})))
, where
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}):=\{\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}A_{x}\}_{x\in X}
, which factors through a faithful representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
.
In fact, the formula

W(\xi)(x)=\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\alpha_{g}(\xi(g)),\qquad\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})),\ x\in X,

defines a Hilbert
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
-module unitary
W:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}))\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}))
that intertwines
\pi^{\mathcal{A}}
and the regular representation
\Lambda:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})))
from (
4.1
).

Example 6.6
.

For the trivial bundle
\mathcal{A}=\{\mathbb{C}\}_{x\in X}
we are treating a twisted groupoid
(\mathcal{G},u)
as in Example
4.12
.
In this case, the induced regular representation
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\colon C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})))
associated with the
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence
\mathcal{E}
is given by:

(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(f)\xi)(g)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}f(gh^{-1})\xi(h)u(gh^{-1},h)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}}f(h)\xi(h^{-1}g)u(h,h^{-1}g)

for
f\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
,
\xi\in\oplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x}=\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x},E_{x})
and
g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
.
Here each
E_{x}
is just a Hilbert module over
B_{x}
because
A_{x}=\mathbb{C}
.
Of course, this is a special case of Proposition
6.4
where the action
\alpha
is trivial.
The above representation generalises the ordinary regular representation
\lambda^{u}
from (
4.2
) by adding the coefficient bundle
\mathcal{E}
.
For an equivariant representation
L
on
\mathcal{E}
, its associated regularised equivariant representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}
acts on
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
by the formula (
6.2
).
There is no further simplification here, except that each
E_{x}
is just a Hilbert
B_{x}
-module.
Further assuming that also
\mathcal{B}=\{\mathbb{C}\}_{x\in X}
is the one-dimensional trivial bundle, then equivariant representations are described by continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundles, that is, continuous Hilbert bundles
\mathcal{H}=\{H_{x}\}_{x\in X}
together with a continuous family of unitaries
L_{g}\colon H_{s(g)}\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{r(g)}
satisfying
L_{g}L_{h}=L_{gh}
for all
(g,h)\in\mathcal{G}^{(2)}
, as in Example
5.6
.

Note that the (regularised)
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
-
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
contains
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
as a sub-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence (the embedding is given by the inclusions
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\subseteq\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
).
Any equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
extends naturally to
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
as follows.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X:=\mathcal{G}^{(0)}
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
An
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
-
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\mathcal{E}
induces an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}:=\{L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
, where for
g\in\mathcal{G}
the map
L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}:\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{s(g)}\to\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{r(g)}
is given by

(6.2)

L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi)(t)=L_{g}(\xi(tg)),\qquad\xi\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{s(g)},\,\,t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}.

Proof.

Given
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
\xi,\zeta\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{s(g)}=\bigoplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{s(g)}}E_{s(g)}
, we have

\begin{split}\langle L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi),L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\zeta)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}&=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi)(t),L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\zeta)(t)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle L_{g}(\xi(tg)),L_{g}(\zeta(tg))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}\\
&=\beta_{g}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle\xi(tg),\zeta(tg)\rangle_{B_{s(g)}}\right)=\beta_{g}\left(\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B_{s(g)}}\right).\end{split}

This implies that
L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}:\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{s(g)}\to\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{r(g)}
is a well-defined isometry.
Furthermore, for
b\in B_{s(g)}
and
t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}
, we have

L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi b)(t)=L_{g}((\xi b)(tg))=L_{g}(\xi(tg)b)=L_{g}(\xi(tg))\beta_{g}(b)=L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi)(t)\beta_{g}(b)=(L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi)\beta_{g}(b))(t),

so that
L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi b)=L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi)\beta_{g}(b)
.
Similarly, we get
L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(a\xi)=\alpha_{g}(a)L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(\xi)
for
a\in A_{s(g)}
.
If
g,h,t\in\mathcal{G}
with
s(g)=r(h),s(t)=r(g)
and
\xi\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{s(h)}
then we have

\begin{split}L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(L^{\mathcal{G}}_{h}\xi)(t)&=L_{g}((L^{\mathcal{G}}_{h}\xi)(tg))=L_{g}\big(L_{h}(\xi(tgh))\big)=u_{\alpha}(g,h)L_{gh}(\xi(tgh))u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*}\\
&=u_{\alpha}(g,h)L^{\mathcal{G}}_{gh}(\xi)(t)u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*}.\end{split}

This shows conditions
(1)
,
(2)
in Definition
5.1
.
To check condition
(3)
, we take sections
\xi_{0},\zeta_{0}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
and consider the corresponding continuous sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
given by
x\mapsto\xi_{0}|_{\mathcal{G}_{x}},\zeta_{0}|_{\mathcal{G}_{x}}
as in the definition of the topology of
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
.
Then for
g\in\mathcal{G}
we get

\langle\xi(r(g)),L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle\xi_{0}(t),L_{g}(\zeta_{0}(tg))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}.

This is a continuous section of the pullback
r^{*}\mathcal{B}
because
L
is continuous and
\xi_{0},\zeta_{0}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
, so in particular the sum above is finite.
∎

Definition 6.8
.

We call equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
of the form
L^{\mathcal{G}}

regular
(or, if we want to stress the original equivariant representation
L
,
regularised
).

Example 6.9
.

Given a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}
we can view the collection of maps
(\alpha_{g})_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
as an equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{A}
, see Example
5.9
.
Its regularisation
\alpha^{\mathcal{G}}
acts on
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})=\{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})_{x}\}_{x\in X}
by the formula
(\alpha^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(a))_{t}=\alpha_{g}(a_{tg})
,
a\xi\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})_{s(g)}
,
t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}
.

Example 6.10
.

For a twisted group action
(A,G,\alpha,u)
, i.e. when
\mathcal{G}=G
is a discrete group, the regular
(\alpha,u)
-
(\alpha,u)
equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
coincide with induced equivariant representations introduced in
[
6
, Example 4.8]
, and
\alpha^{G}
is called the regular equivariant representation of
\alpha
in
[
6
, Example 4.7]
.

Definition 6.11
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Two
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
,
L
and
L^{\prime}
, defined respectively on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundles
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{F}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
, are
unitarily equivalent
if there is a
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-unitary
\Gamma:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{F})
such that
\Gamma_{r(g)}L_{g}=L^{\prime}_{g}\Gamma_{s(g)}
for all
g\in\Gamma
, where
\{\Gamma_{x}\}_{x\in X}
is the associated continuous family of unitaries
\Gamma_{x}:E_{x}\to F_{x}
,
x\in X
, see Lemma
3.12
.

Example 6.12

(Regularised equivariant representations revisited)
.

Instead of using the source map
s
in the construction of a regular equivariant representation, we could also use the range map
r
.
More specifically, let
(\mathcal{E},L)
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant correspondence.
We have an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant correspondence
(\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E}),{{}^{\mathcal{G}}L})
where the fibres of
\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})=\{\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}\}_{x\in X}
are direct sums
\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}=\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}^{x}}E_{x}
and for each
g\in\mathcal{G}
the map
{{}^{\mathcal{G}}L}_{g}:\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})_{s(g)}\to\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})_{r(g)}
is given by

{}^{\mathcal{G}}L_{g}(\xi)(t)=L_{g}(\xi(g^{-1}t)),\qquad\xi\in\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})_{s(g)},\,\,t\in\mathcal{G}^{s(g)}.

Then the regularised equivariant representations
(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}})
and
(\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E}),{{}^{\mathcal{G}}L})
are unitarily equivalent, with equivalence given by the bundle of unitaries
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}\xrightarrow{\sim}\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}
, where
\delta_{h}\mapsto\delta_{h^{-1}}
for
h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
.
Also unitary equivalence of Hilbert bundles mentioned in Example
5.6
is a special case of the above notion of unitary equivalence, and tensoring
(\mathcal{E},L)
with the left and right regular Hilbert bundles
(\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}^{x})\}_{x\in X},\lambda)
and
(\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},\rho)
, see Examples
5.6
,
5.10
, we get equivariant correspondences
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}^{x})\}_{x\in X},L\otimes\lambda)
and
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},L\otimes\rho)
where, for each
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
\xi\in E_{s(g)}
, we have

\displaystyle L_{g}\otimes\lambda_{g}(\xi\otimes\delta_{h})

\displaystyle=L_{g}(\xi)\otimes\delta_{gh},\qquad\quad h\in\mathcal{G}^{s(g)},

\displaystyle L_{g}\otimes\rho_{g}(\xi\otimes\delta_{h})

\displaystyle=L_{g}(\xi)\otimes\delta_{hg^{-1}},\qquad h\in\mathcal{G}_{s(g)}.

Indeed, for instance the unitaries
E_{x}\otimes\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\ni\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi_{h}\otimes\delta_{h}\longmapsto\oplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi_{h}\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}=\bigoplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}E_{x}
, for
x\in X
, establish the equivalence
(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}})\cong(\mathcal{E}\otimes\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},L\otimes\rho)
.
Concluding, we have the following unitary equivalences

(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}})\cong(\mathcal{E}\otimes\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},L\otimes\rho)\cong(\mathcal{E}\otimes\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}^{x})\}_{x\in X},L\otimes\lambda)\cong(\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{E}),{{}^{\mathcal{G}}L}),

yielding equivalent pictures of the regularisation of the equivariant correspondence
(\mathcal{E},L)
.

Proposition 6.13

(Absorption for equivariant representations)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
L
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
.
The tensor product equivariant representations
L\otimes\beta^{\mathcal{G}}
and
\alpha^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes L
, acting on
\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})
and
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}
, respectively, are both unitarily equivalent to the induced regular equivariant representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}
on
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

We construct the desired
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-unitary
\Gamma^{\beta}:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
.
Given
x\in X
, there is a unique unitary
\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}:E_{x}\otimes_{B_{x}}\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}\to\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}
satisfying

(6.3)

\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}(\xi\otimes b)(t)=\xi\cdot b(t),\qquad\xi\in E_{x},\ b\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x},\ t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}.

Indeed, for
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in E_{x}
,
b,b^{\prime}\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
we have
\xi\otimes b,\xi^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime}\in E_{x}\otimes_{B_{x}}\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
and

\begin{split}\langle\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}(\xi\otimes b),\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime})\rangle_{B_{x}}&=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\langle\xi b(t),\xi^{\prime}b^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{B_{x}}=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}b(t)^{*}\langle\xi,\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{B_{x}}b^{\prime}(t)\\
&=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\langle\xi\otimes b(t),\xi^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime}(t)\rangle_{B_{x}}=\langle\xi\otimes b,\xi^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime}\rangle_{B_{x}}.\end{split}

This implies that (
6.3
) extends uniquely to an adjointable isometry, which is invertible as its range clearly contains the dense subspace
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{G}_{x}})\subseteq\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x}
.
To see that the bundle
\{\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}\}_{x\in X}
of unitaries is continuous take
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
and
b\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
of the form
b(x)=\oplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}b_{0}(g)
for some
b_{0}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})
.
Then the formula
\zeta(g):=\xi(s(g))\cdot b_{0}(g)
defines an element
\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
and so

\Gamma^{\beta}(\xi\otimes b)(x):=\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}(\xi(x)\otimes b(x))=\bigoplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi(x)\cdot b_{0}(g),\qquad x\in X,

defines an element of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
.
Hence
\{\Gamma_{x}^{\beta}\}_{x\in X}
defines the
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-unitary
\Gamma^{\beta}
.
It intertwines the equivariant representations as for
\xi\in E_{x},b\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
,
t,g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
, we have

\begin{split}(\Gamma_{r(g)}^{\beta}\circ(L\otimes\beta^{\mathcal{G}})_{g})(\xi\otimes b)(t)&=\Gamma_{r(g)}(L_{g}\xi\otimes\beta^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(b))(t)=L_{g}(\xi)\cdot\beta^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}(b)(t)=L_{g}(\xi)\beta_{g}(b(tg))\\
&=L_{g}(\xi b(tg))=L_{g}(\Gamma_{s(g)}^{\beta}(\xi\otimes b(tg)))=(L^{\mathcal{G}}_{g}\circ\Gamma_{s(g)}^{\beta})(\xi\otimes b)(t).\end{split}

This proves the unitary equivalence between
L\otimes\beta^{\mathcal{G}}
and
L^{\mathcal{G}}
.
The equivalence between
\alpha^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes L
and
L^{\mathcal{G}}
can be proved analogously, and it is implemented by the
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-unitary
\Gamma^{\alpha}:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
, where for
x\in X
we set

\Gamma_{x}^{\alpha}(a\otimes\xi)(t)=a(t)\xi,\qquad\xi\in E_{x},\ a\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})_{x},\ t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}.

We leave the details to the reader.
∎

Corollary 6.14
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{C}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
,
(\beta,u_{\beta})
and
(\gamma,u_{\gamma})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
L
and
K
be two composable equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
, so
L
is
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant and
K
is
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-
(\gamma,u_{\gamma})
equivariant, see Example
5.12
.
Then

L^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes K\cong L\otimes K^{\mathcal{G}}\cong(L\otimes K)^{\mathcal{G}}.

Proof.

Using the ‘associativity’ of the tensor product and applying Proposition
6.13
four times we get
L^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes K\cong L\otimes\beta^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes K\cong L\otimes K^{\mathcal{G}}\cong L\otimes K\otimes\gamma^{\mathcal{G}}\cong(L\otimes K)^{\mathcal{G}}
.
∎

We will use the above absorption statement to infer that our Fourier multipliers form an ideal in the category of Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers (to be defined in the next section).
Also, Proposition
6.13
implies that

\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))\cong\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})).

To show that Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers yield completely bounded maps on reduced crossed products we will need yet another form of absorption, where we change the left multiplication in the regular
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})
from the pointwise to the one given by the regular representation.
The following versions of the Fell absorption principle are what we need; they are related to similar results appearing, for instance, in
[
64
, Lemma 2]
and
[
16
, Theorem 3.20]
.
The first one is a generalisation of
[
7
, Theorem 4.10]
.

Theorem 6.15

(Fell’s absorption I)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
L
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
.
The representation
L\dashind(\pi^{\mathcal{B}})
induced by
L
(as in Proposition
5.14
) from the regular representation
\pi^{\mathcal{B}}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})))
(see Example
6.5
) is unitarily equivalent to the regular representation
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\cong\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}}
induced by
\mathcal{E}
.
This equivalence is implemented by the isomorphism of right Hilbert
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
-modules

W:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi^{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))

where
W(\xi\otimes b)(x):=W_{x}(\xi\otimes b(x))
,
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
,
b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
,
x\in X
, and

W_{x}:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi^{\mathcal{B}}_{x}}\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})_{x},\quad W_{x}\left(\xi\otimes b_{x}\right)(g):=L_{g}^{-1}\left(\xi(r(g))\right)b_{x}(g),

for
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
,
b_{x}\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
.

Proof.

Let
x\in X
.
For any
\xi,\ \zeta\in E_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
, and
a,b\in B_{x}
, using Remark
5.2
, we have

\begin{split}\langle L_{g}^{-1}(\xi)b,L_{g}^{-1}(\zeta)a\rangle_{B_{x}}&=\beta_{g}^{-1}(\langle\xi\beta_{g}(b),\zeta\beta_{g}(a)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}})=b^{*}\beta_{g}^{-1}(\langle\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B_{r(g)}})a.\end{split}

Using this, for any
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
and
a
,
b\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
we get

\begin{split}\left\langle W_{x}\left(\xi\otimes b\right),W_{x}\left(\zeta\otimes a\right)\right\rangle_{B_{x}}&=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}b(g)^{*}\beta_{g}^{-1}(\langle\xi(r(g)),\zeta(r(g))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}})a(g)=\left\langle\xi\otimes b,\zeta\otimes a\right\rangle_{B_{x}}.\end{split}

This readily implies that
W_{x}
is a well-defined isometry.
It clearly has dense range, hence in fact
W_{x}
is an adjointable unitary.
Now it suffices to see that
W
is well defined, i.e. that for
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
,
b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
the formula
W(\xi\otimes b)(x)=W_{x}(\xi\otimes b(x))
defines an element of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
.
We may assume here that
b
is of the form
x\mapsto\oplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}b_{0}(g)
for some
b_{0}\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})
, as such elements form a dense subset of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
.
The section
\mathcal{G}\ni g\mapsto L_{g^{-1}}\left(\xi(r(g))\right)
is in
\mathrm{C_{b}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
by continuity and boundedness of
\xi
and
L
, see Lemma
5.3
.
Therefore the section
\mathcal{G}\ni g\mapsto L_{g^{-1}}\left(\xi(r(g)\right))u_{\beta}(g^{-1},g)b_{0}(g)
is in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
.
Since
W(\xi\otimes b)(x)=\oplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}L_{g^{-1}}\left(\xi(r(g))\right)u_{\beta}(g^{-1},g)b_{0}(g)
we conclude that
W(\xi\otimes b)\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
.

To prove that
W
intertwines
L\dashind(\pi^{\mathcal{B}})
and
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}
we need to show that for every
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
,
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
,
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E}),\ b\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})
, we have

(6.4)

W^{*}\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(a)W(\xi\otimes b)=aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(\delta_{U})(b),

where
L_{U}(\xi)(r(g)):=L_{g}(\xi(s(g))
for
g\in U
and

\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(\delta_{U})(b)(x):=\oplus_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}\cap U}\overline{\alpha}_{g}^{-1}\big(u(h,h^{-1}g)\big)b(h^{-1}g),\qquad x\in X.

Moreover, by linearity and continuity, it suffices to consider
b
such that for every
x\in X
,
b(x)\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
, is supported on single element of
\mathcal{G}_{x}
.
Let us then fix
x\in X
and assume that
b_{x}:=b(x)\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})_{x}
, is supported on
t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
.
There is at most one element in
U\cap\mathcal{G}_{r(t)}
, that we denote by
h
if it exists.
We have

\begin{split}\big(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(a)W_{x}(\xi\otimes&b_{x})\big)(g)=[g=ht]\alpha_{g}^{-1}\left(a(h)u_{\alpha}(h,t)\right)L_{t}^{-1}\left(\xi(r(t))\right)b_{x}(t)\\
&=[g=ht]\alpha_{g}^{-1}\left(a(h)u_{\alpha}(h,t)\right)L_{g}^{-1}L_{h^{-1}}^{-1}\left(u_{\alpha}(h^{-1},g)\xi(r(t))u_{\beta}(h^{-1},g)^{*}\right)b_{x}(t)\\
&=[g=ht]L_{g}^{-1}\left(a(h)u_{\alpha}(h,t)L_{h^{-1}}^{-1}(u_{\alpha}(h^{-1},g)\xi(r(t))u_{\beta}(h^{-1},g)^{*}\right)b_{x}(t).\end{split}

Consider
c:=u_{\alpha}(h,t)L_{h^{-1}}^{-1}\left(u_{\alpha}(h^{-1},g)\xi(r(t))u_{\beta}(h^{-1},g)^{*}\right)
where
g=ht
.
Since
r(t)=s(h)
and
L_{h^{-1}}^{-1}(\cdot)=u_{\alpha}(h,h^{-1})^{*}L_{h}(\cdot)u_{\beta}(h,h^{-1})
we get

c=u_{\alpha}(h,t)u_{\alpha}(h,h^{-1})^{*}\alpha_{h}(u_{\alpha}(h^{-1},g))L_{h}\left(\xi(s(h))\right)\beta_{h}(u_{\beta}(h^{-1},g))^{*}u_{\beta}(h,h^{-1}).

By the cocycle identity we have
\alpha_{h}(u_{\alpha}(h^{-1},g))=u_{\alpha}(h,h^{-1})u_{\alpha}(h,t)^{*}
(and similarly for
\beta
).
Thus
c=L_{h}(\xi(s(h))u_{\beta}(h,t)
.
Therefore continuing the above calculations we get

\begin{split}\big(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(a)W_{x}(\xi\otimes b_{x})\big)(g)&=[g=ht]L_{g}^{-1}\left(a(h)L_{h}(\xi(s(h))u_{\beta}(h,t)\right)b_{x}(t)\\
&=W_{x}\left(aL_{U}(\xi)\otimes\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(\delta_{U})(b)\right)(g).\end{split}

This implies the equality (
6.4
) and finishes the proof.
∎

The above theorem states that for any equivariant correspondence
(\mathcal{E},L)
the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
L\dashind(\pi^{\mathcal{B}})
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\hskip 14.72241pt\,\,}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{A}))\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi^{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 16.38895pt\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\Big)}

where
\cong
denotes an isomorphism implemented by a unitary between Hilbert modules.
Thus regular representations absorb the inducing functor in the sense that composing
L\dashind
with the regular representation one gets a regular representation.
For groups the representation
L\dashind(\pi^{\mathcal{B}})
is nothing but the tensor product of a representation
L
and the regular representation
\pi^{\mathcal{B}}
and the above theorem reduces to the classical Fell’s absorption.

Next we derive the second version of Fell’s absorption principle that will be needed later, and which in particular generalises
[
6
, Theorem 4.11]
. Recall that regular representations induced by
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundles were introduced in Definition
6.1
, regularisations of equivariant representations in Lemma
6.7
and Definition
6.8
, and representations induced by equivariant actions in Proposition
5.14
.

Theorem 6.16

(Fell’s absorption II)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
L
be an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
, and let
\psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\to\mathcal{L}(H)
be a representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
on a Hilbert space
H
.
The representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi):\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\psi}H)
induced from
\psi
by the regularisation of
L
is unitarily equivalent to the amplification
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\otimes 1_{H}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\psi}H)
of the regular representation
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}
induced by
\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

Disintegrate
\psi
into a covariant representation
(\pi,v)
as in Subsection
4.1
.
So
\pi=\psi|_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)}
and for any
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
the partial isometry
v_{U}
is the strong limit of
\psi(e^{r(U)}_{i}\delta_{U})
, where for an open set
V\subseteq X
, we choose an approximate unit
\{e^{V}_{i}\}_{i}
in
B_{V}=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{V})
.
We will describe the action of the desired unitary on the dense subspace
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi}H
of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\pi}H
.
For
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
and
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}|_{s(U)})
, define
\delta_{U}\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}|_{U})
by

(\delta_{U}\xi)(g):=\xi(s(g)),\qquad g\in U.

For any
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
and any
\eta\in H
put

(6.5)

W(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta):=\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi)\otimes v_{U}\eta,

where
v_{U}
is the partial isometry from the disintegration of
\psi
, see Subsection
4.1
.
We first show that for any
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}|_{s(U^{\prime})})
,
U^{\prime}\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
,
\eta^{\prime}\in H
we have

(6.6)

\langle W(\xi\delta_{U}\otimes\eta),W(\xi^{\prime}\delta_{U^{\prime}}\otimes\eta^{\prime})\rangle=\langle\xi\delta_{U}\otimes\eta,\xi^{\prime}\delta_{U^{\prime}}\otimes\eta^{\prime}\rangle.

Let
U_{0}=U\cap U^{\prime}
.
The section
\langle\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi),\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}
vanishes outside
r(U_{0})
and for
g\in U_{0}
we have
\langle\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi),\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}(r(g))=\langle L_{g}(\xi(s(g))),L_{g}(\xi^{\prime}(s(g)))\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}
.
Thus

\begin{split}\langle\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi),\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}&=\lim_{i,j}e_{i}^{r(U_{0})}\cdot\langle\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi),\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\cdot e_{j}^{r(U_{0})}\\
&=\lim_{i,j}\langle\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi)e_{i}^{r(U_{0})},\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime})e_{j}^{r(U_{0})}\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\\
&=\lim_{i,j}\langle\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U}(\xi e_{i}^{s(U_{0})}),\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime}e_{i}^{s(U_{0})})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\\
&=\lim_{i,j}\langle\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}L_{U_{0}}(\xi e_{i}^{s(U_{0})}),\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}L_{U_{0}}(\xi^{\prime}e_{i}^{s(U_{0})})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}.\end{split}

Similarly,
\langle\delta_{U}\xi,\delta_{U^{\prime}}\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}=\lim_{i,j}\langle\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}\xi e_{i}^{s(U_{0})},\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}\xi^{\prime}e_{j}^{s(U_{0})}\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}
.
We also have
\psi(e_{i}^{r(U_{0})})v_{U}=\psi(e_{i}^{r(U_{0})})v_{U_{0}}
and
\psi(e_{i}^{r(U_{0})})v_{U^{\prime}}=\psi(e_{i}^{r(U_{0})})v_{U_{0}}
.
Using this and the covariance conditions in Subsection
4.1
, we get

\begin{split}\langle W(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta),W(\delta_{U^{\prime}}\xi^{\prime}\otimes&\eta^{\prime})\rangle=\langle v_{U}\eta,\pi(\delta_{U^{*}}\langle L_{U}(\xi),\delta_{U^{\prime*}}L_{U^{\prime}}(\xi^{\prime})\rangle)v_{U^{\prime}}\eta^{\prime}\rangle\\
&=\lim_{i,j}\left\langle\eta,v_{U_{0}}^{*}\pi\left(\langle\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}L_{U_{0}}(\xi e_{i}^{s(U_{0})}),\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}L_{U_{0}}(\xi^{\prime}e_{j}^{s(U_{0})})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\right)v_{U_{0}}\eta^{\prime}\right\rangle\\
&=\lim_{i,j}\left\langle\eta,\pi\left(\beta_{U_{0}}^{-1}\Big(\langle\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}L_{U_{0}}(\xi e_{i}^{s(U_{0})}),\delta_{U_{0}^{*}}L_{U_{0}}(\xi^{\prime}e_{j}^{s(U_{0})})\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\Big)\right)\eta^{\prime}\right\rangle\\
&=\lim_{i,j}\left\langle\eta,\pi\left(\langle\delta_{U_{0}}\xi e_{i}^{s(U_{0})},\delta_{U_{0}}\xi^{\prime}e_{j}^{s(U_{0})}\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\right)\eta^{\prime}\right\rangle\\
&=\left\langle\eta,\pi\left(\langle\xi\delta_{U},\xi^{\prime}\delta_{U^{\prime}}\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})}\right)\eta^{\prime}\right\rangle=\langle\xi\delta_{U}\otimes\eta,\xi^{\prime}\delta_{U^{\prime}}\otimes\eta^{\prime}\rangle.\end{split}

Now by linearity, continuity and (
6.6
), we see that (
6.5
) determines an isometry
W
on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\pi}H
.
