Using that
\omega_{\alpha}(U,U^{*})a=\omega_{\alpha}(U_{0},U_{0}^{*})a
for any
U_{0}\subseteq U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
and
a\in A_{U^{0}}=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{U_{0}})
, one can adopt the above arguments to see that the formula

W^{*}(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta):=\delta_{U^{*}}\omega_{\alpha}(U,U^{*})^{*}L_{U}(\xi)\otimes v_{U}\eta,

determines an isometry on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\pi}H
.
Since
\omega_{\alpha}(U,U^{*})^{*}L_{U}(\cdot)\omega_{\beta}(U,U^{*})=L_{U^{*}}^{-1}
,
\overline{\pi}(\omega_{\beta}(U,U^{*})^{*})v_{U}=v_{U^{*}}^{*}
and the tensor product is balanced over
\overline{\pi}(\mathcal{M}(A))
, we also have

W^{*}(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta):=\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U^{*}}^{-1}(\xi)\otimes v_{U^{*}}^{*}\eta.

This readily implies that
WW^{*}=W^{*}W=1
, as for instance we have

\begin{split}WW^{*}(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta)&=\delta_{U}\xi\otimes v_{U^{*}}v_{U^{*}}^{*}\eta=\text{s-}\lim_{i}\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\pi(e_{i}^{s(U)})\eta\\
&=\text{s-}\lim_{i}\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\pi(e_{i}^{s(U)})\eta=\text{s-}\lim_{i}\delta_{U}\xi e_{i}^{s(U)}\otimes\eta=\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta.\end{split}

Hence
W
is a unitary and
W^{*}
is its adjoint.

For
V\in\Bis(G)
and
a\in A_{r(V)}
we define
a\delta_{V}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V})
by
a\delta_{V}(g)=a(r(g))
for
g\in V
.
One computes that
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(a\delta_{V})\delta_{U^{*}}L_{U^{*}}^{-1}(\xi)=\delta_{VU^{*}}\zeta
where
\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}|_{s(VU^{*})})
for
gh\in VU^{*}
where
g\in V
,
h\in U^{*}
is given by

\begin{split}\zeta(s(g))&=\alpha_{gh}^{-1}\Big(a(r(g))u_{\alpha}(g,h)\Big)L_{h}^{-1}\big(\xi(s(g))\big)\\
&=\alpha_{h}^{-1}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}\big(u_{\alpha}(g,h)a(r(g))\big)\Big)L_{h}^{-1}(\xi(s(g)))\\
&=L_{h}^{-1}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}\big(u_{\alpha}(g,h)a(r(g))\big)\xi(s(g)\Big).\end{split}

Thus on one hand we have

(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\otimes 1_{H})(a\delta_{V})W^{*}(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta)=\delta_{VU^{*}}\zeta\otimes v_{U^{*}}^{*}\eta.

On the other hand, a direct computation shows that
aL_{V}^{\mathcal{G}}(\delta_{U}\xi)=\delta_{UV^{*}}\zeta_{0}
where
\zeta_{0}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}|_{s(UV^{*})})=\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}|_{r(VU^{*})})
is given by
\zeta_{0}(r(g)):=L_{g}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(a(r(g))\xi(s(g)))\Big)
for
g\in V
.
Moreover, for
gh\in VU^{*}
where
g\in V
,
h\in U^{*}
, we have

\begin{split}L_{VU^{*}}^{-1}(\zeta_{0})(s(g))&=L_{gh}^{-1}\Big(L_{g}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(a(r(g)))\xi(s(g)))\Big)\Big)\\
&=L_{h}^{-1}L_{g}^{-1}L_{g}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(u_{\alpha}(g,h)a(r(g)))\xi(s(g))\beta_{g}^{-1}(u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*})\Big)\\
&=L_{h}^{-1}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(u_{\alpha}(g,h)a(r(g)))\xi(s(g)))\Big)\beta_{h}^{-1}(\beta_{g}^{-1}(u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*})\\
&=\zeta(s(gh))\beta_{gh}^{-1}(u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*}).\end{split}

That is
L_{VU^{*}}^{-1}(\zeta_{0})=\zeta\beta_{UV^{*}}^{-1}(\omega_{\beta}(V,U^{*})^{*})
. Using this we get

\begin{split}W^{*}L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{V})(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta)&=W^{*}\big(aL_{V}^{\mathcal{G}}(\delta_{U}\xi)\otimes v_{V}\eta\big)=W^{*}\big(\delta_{UV^{*}}\zeta_{0}\otimes v_{V}\eta\big)\\
&=\delta_{VU^{*}}\Big(\zeta\beta_{UV^{*}}^{-1}(\omega_{\beta}(V,U^{*})^{*})\Big)\otimes v_{VU^{*}}^{*}v_{V}\eta\\
&=\delta_{VU^{*}}\zeta\otimes\pi(\beta_{UV^{*}}^{-1}(\omega_{\beta}(V,U^{*})^{*}))v_{VU^{*}}^{*}v_{V}\eta.\end{split}

Then using the covariance relations and the fact that
v_{V}^{*}v_{V}
is a strong limit of
\{\pi(e_{i}^{s(V)})\}
we obtain that

\begin{split}\pi(\beta_{UV^{*}}^{-1}(\omega_{\beta}(V,U^{*})^{*}))v_{VU^{*}}^{*}v_{V}\eta&=\lim_{i}\pi(e_{i}^{s(VU^{*})})v_{U^{*}}^{*}v_{V}^{*}v_{V}\eta=\lim_{i,j}v_{U^{*}}^{*}\pi(\beta_{U^{*}}^{-1}(e_{i}^{s(VU^{*})}))\pi(e_{j}^{s(V)})\eta\\
&=\lim_{i}v_{U^{*}}^{*}\pi(\beta_{U^{*}}^{-1}(e_{i}^{s(VU^{*})}))\eta=\lim_{i}\pi(e_{i}^{s(VU^{*})})v_{U^{*}}^{*}\eta.\end{split}

Further exploiting the fact that the tensor product is balanced we get

W^{*}L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)(a\delta_{V})(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta)=\delta_{VU^{*}}\zeta\otimes v_{U^{*}}^{*}\eta=(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\otimes 1_{H})(a\delta_{V})W^{*}(\delta_{U}\xi\otimes\eta).

This finishes the proof.
∎

The above theorem states that for any equivariant correspondence
(\mathcal{E},L)
and for any representation
\psi
of
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
on a Hilbert space
H
the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{H}\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(r^{*}\mathcal{A}))\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}H\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\psi}H\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))\otimes_{\psi}H\Big)}

where the isomorphisms
\cong
are implemented by unitaries between Hilbert modules.
Thus the regular equivariant representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}
absorbs all representations in the sense that the induced representation is an amplification of a regular representation, and hence descends to the reduced crossed product.
For groups the representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)
is nothing but the tensor product of a regular representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}
and the representation
\psi
, and the above theorem recovers the classical Fell absorption principle.

6.1.
Absorption principles for groupoids

Let us specialise the above results to the case of trivial one-dimensional
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{B}=\mathbb{C}\times X
over
X
.
In this case, we are simply considering a twisted groupoid
(\mathcal{G},u)
, and equivariant representations correspond to continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundles
(\mathcal{H},L)
as in Example
5.6
.
Recall the identifications and notation introduced in Proposition
6.4
.
The regular induced representation
\pi^{\mathcal{H}}\colon C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H})))
acts on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H}))
, which is the Hilbert module over
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
obtained as the completion of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{H})
with respect to the obvious right action and inner product

\langle\xi\,|\,\mathopen{}\eta\rangle(x):=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\langle\xi(g)\,|\,\mathopen{}\eta(g)\rangle,\qquad x\in X,\,\xi,\,\eta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{H}).

The representation
\pi^{\mathcal{H}}\colon\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H})))
is given by the formula

\pi^{\mathcal{H}}(f)\xi(g)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}}f(h)\xi(h^{-1}g)u(h,h^{-1}g),

where
f\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G}),\xi\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{H})
and
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

The left regular representation
\lambda^{u}
of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
is given by a similar formula, as it can be seen as the regular representation induced from the trivial Hilbert bundle
\mathbb{C}\times X
, which acts on the
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-Hilbert module which we will simply denote
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})
: the completion of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
in the
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-valued inner product
\langle\xi\,|\,\mathopen{}\eta\rangle(x):=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi(g)\overline{\eta(g)}
,
x\in X,\,\xi,\,\eta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
. The induced representation
L\dashind(\lambda^{u})
acts thus on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)}\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})
by the formula

L\dashind(\lambda^{u})(f\delta_{U})(\xi\otimes\eta)=f\cdot L_{U}(\xi)\otimes\lambda^{u}(\delta_{U})\eta

for an open bisection
U\subseteq\mathcal{G}
,
f\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(r(U))\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(X)
,
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H}|_{r(U)})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})
,
\eta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})\subseteq\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})
,
where
f\delta_{U}(g)=[g\in U]f(r(g))
,
(f\cdot L_{U})(\xi)(r(g))=f(r(g))L_{g}(\xi(s(g)))
, and
(\lambda^{u}(\delta_{U})\eta)(g)=[g\in\mathcal{G}^{r(h)},h\in U]\eta(h^{-1}g)
for
g\in\mathcal{G}
.
With the above notation, Theorem
6.15
gives the following result.

Corollary 6.17
.

The unitary
W\colon\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)}\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H}))
given by
W(\xi\otimes\eta)(g):=L_{g}^{-1}(\xi(r(g)))\eta(g)
, for
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H}),\eta\in\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, yields a unitary equivalence between the
L
-induced representation
L\dashind(\lambda^{u})\colon C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}\big(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)}\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})\big)
and the regular induced representation
\pi^{\mathcal{H}}\colon C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H})))
.

Remark 6.18
.

By Proposition
6.4
,
\pi^{\mathcal{H}}
is naturally unitarily equivalent to the representation
\Lambda^{\mathcal{H}}:=\lambda^{u}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})}
of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}))\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})
. Hence the above corollary amounts to saying that
\lambda^{u}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})}\cong L\dashind(\lambda^{u})
, and so the regular representation induced by
\mathcal{H}
from
\lambda^{u}
is unitarily equivalent to the representation induced by
L
from
\lambda^{u}
.
Pictorially, we can tensor
\mathcal{H}
by
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})
on both sides and equivariance allows us to move regular representations from one side to another.

We recall, see Example
6.12
, that the regularisation
(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H}),L^{\mathcal{G}})
of the Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundle
(\mathcal{H},L)
is unitarily isomorphic to the continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundle
(\mathcal{H}\otimes\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},L\otimes\rho)
, where
(\{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{x})\}_{x\in X},\rho)
is the right regular continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundle, cf. Examples
4.12
,
5.6
,
6.6
.
Thus for any representation
\psi\colon C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to B(H)
on a Hilbert space
H
, the induced representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)
acts on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})\otimes_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)}\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})\otimes_{\psi}H
by the formula

L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)(f\delta_{U})(\xi\otimes\eta\otimes\zeta)=\lim_{i}f\cdot L_{U}(\xi)\otimes\rho_{U}(\eta)\otimes\psi(\delta_{U})\zeta

for an open bisection
U\subseteq\mathcal{G}
,
f\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(r(U))\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(X)
,
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H}|_{r(U)})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{H})
,
\eta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})\subseteq\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G})
,
where
\rho_{U}(\eta)(g)=[g\in\mathcal{G}_{r(h)},h\in U]\eta(gh)
and
\psi(\delta_{U})\zeta
is the strong limit of
\psi(e_{i}\delta_{U})\zeta
where
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is an approximate unit of the ideal
\mathrm{C_{0}}(r(U))\subseteq\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
.
Theorem
6.16
specialises in this context to the following corollary.

Corollary 6.19
.

Consider a continuous Hilbert
\mathcal{G}
-bundle
(\mathcal{H},L)
over a twisted groupoid
(\mathcal{G},u)
and a representation
\psi\colon C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to B(H)
of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
on a Hilbert space
H
.
Then the representation
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi):C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H}))\otimes_{\psi}H)
induced from
\psi
by the regularisation of
L
is unitarily equivalent to the amplification
\Lambda^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1_{H}:C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{H}))\otimes_{\psi}H)
of the regular representation
\Lambda^{\mathcal{H}}
induced by
\mathcal{H}
.

7.
Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers for twisted groupoid actions

This section is central for the work, as we are ready to exploit the results established earlier in the paper to construct the Banach category of Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers for twisted groupoid crossed products.
Recall that
\mathcal{G}
is a fixed locally compact Hausdorff étale groupoid with unit space
X=\mathcal{G}^{(0)}
.
As in the last section we begin the discussion in the general context of Fell bundles.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be Fell bundles over
\mathcal{G}
.
A
multiplier
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
is a continuous bundle of fibrewise bounded operators
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
, i.e. a family of maps
T_{g}\in\mathbb{B}(A_{g},B_{g})
such that for each
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
the map

(7.1)

\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto(m_{T}a)(g):=T_{g}(a(g))\in B_{g},

is in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})
.
If
A
and
B
are completions of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
and
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})
respectively, we say that the multiplier
T
is
A
-
B

bounded
(resp.
completely bounded
or
completely positive
) if the map
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})
extends to a bounded (resp. completely bounded, completely positive) map
m_{T}:A\to B
(note that we still denote it in the same way).
Usually, we will be interested in the case when
A
and
B
are reduced or full section
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras, in which case we will call
T
a
reduced
or
full multiplier
, respectively.

Remark 7.2
.

There is a somewhat unfortunate long tradition of calling both the family
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
and the resulting map
m_{T}
a multiplier.
As it is difficult to fight the tradition, we will rather follow it and hope that it does not cause confusion to the readers.

Example 7.3

(Herz–Schur multipliers)
.

If
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{B}
, then any bounded strictly continuous section
\varphi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(r^{*}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}|_{X}))
defines a multiplier
T^{\varphi}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{A}
where
T^{\varphi}_{g}a:=\varphi(g)a
for all
a\in A_{g}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.
One may then call
T^{\varphi}
a
Herz–Schur multiplier
for
\mathcal{A}
and write
m_{\varphi}:=m_{T^{\varphi}}
, so

(7.2)

(m_{\varphi}a)(g):=\varphi(g)a(g)\in A_{g},\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}),\ g\in\mathcal{G}.

Specialising to the case of the Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}
of a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
, we have
\varphi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(r^{*}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
and the corresponding multipliers were studied in
[
42
]
(in the context of actions of twisted groupoids).
Note that in the case of trivial bundles, every multiplier is a Herz–Schur multiplier (as fibres are one-dimensional).
The Herz–Schur multipliers studied in
[
48
]
,
[
47
]
for group actions, are what we would call completely bounded reduced multipliers.

Example 7.4

(Exel multipliers)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a Fell bundle over
\mathcal{G}
and let
\mathcal{A}^{0}:=\mathcal{A}|_{X}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{A}
to
X
.
Consider two sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}\mathcal{A}^{0})

and define

T_{g}(a)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}}\xi(h)^{*}\cdot a\cdot\zeta(g^{-1}h),\quad a\in A_{g},\ g\in\mathcal{G}.

Here we use the natural
A_{r(g)}
-
A_{s(g)}
-bimodule structure of
A_{g}
.
The sum above is finite because
\xi
has compact support and
\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}
is discrete.
It is clear that
T_{g}
is a bounded linear map
A_{g}\to A_{g}
.
Moreover, if
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
, then
g\mapsto T_{g}(a(g))
is again a continuous section of
\mathcal{A}
because
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
is a right module over
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{0}))
in the canonical way.

For groups, multipliers of the above form first appeared in Exel’s work
[
28
]
and are used to define a notion of
approximation property
for Fell bundles over groups.
More recently, the last notion has been generalised to étale groupoids or inverse semigroups in
[
39
,
16
]
.

As mentioned in Remark
7.2
, in the sequel we will often identify a multiplier
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
with the associated linear map
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})
given by (
7.1
).

The set of all multipliers

m(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
, when viewed as maps on continuous sections, is naturally an involutive
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule.
Namely, for
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}},S=\{S_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\in m(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
and
f\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
these structures are given by
(\lambda T)_{g}:=\lambda T_{g}
,
(T+S)_{g}:=T_{g}+S_{g}
,
(f\cdot T)_{g}:=f(r(g))T_{g}
,
(T\cdot f)_{g}:=f(s(g))T_{g}
, and

(7.3)

T^{\dagger}_{g}(a):=(T_{g^{-1}}(a^{*}))^{*},\qquad g\in\mathcal{G},\ a\in A_{g}.

The involution
\dagger
is conjugate-linear and for
f,T
as above we have
(f\cdot T)^{\dagger}=T^{\dagger}\cdot\overline{f}
,
(T\cdot f)^{\dagger}=\overline{f}\cdot T^{\dagger}
.
In terms of the map
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})
it is given by
m_{T^{\dagger}}(a):=m_{T}(a^{*})^{*}
,
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
.
We can also compose multipliers if the domain and codomain agree:
for
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\in m(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
,
S=\{S_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\in m(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
we put

S\circ T:=\{S_{g}\circ T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}.

In particular, all Fell bundles over
\mathcal{G}
with multipliers as morphisms form a category.
Also
m(\mathcal{A}):=m(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
is an algebra and an involutive
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule.
The involution (
7.3
) is multiplicative on the algebra
m(\mathcal{A})
, so it is not an ‘involution’ in the ordinary sense unless
m(\mathcal{A})
is commutative.

When restricting to Fell bundles coming from twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
we have a distinguished subcategory with morphisms defined as follows.
Let
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
be twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
over
X
, respectively, and consider the associated Fell bundles
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}=r^{*}\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}=r^{*}\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{G}
.
For any
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
and any bounded strictly continuous sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
the formula

T_{g}(a):=\langle\xi(r(g)),a\overline{L}_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}\in B_{r(g)},\qquad a\in A_{r(g)},\ g\in\mathcal{G},

defines a multiplier
T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}:=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
, cf. Proposition
5.4
(3’)
.
We can alternatively express
T_{g}
without using the extended action
\overline{L}
, as the following formula holds

T_{g}(a)=\lim_{i}\left\langle e_{i}\xi(r(g)),L_{g}\Big(\alpha_{g^{-1}}(a)\zeta(s(g))\Big)\right\rangle_{B_{r(g)}},

where
\{e_{i}\}_{i}
is an approximate unit in
A_{r(g)}
,
a\in A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

Definition 7.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
We call
T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}
defined above the
Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
associated to the
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
and sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
.
We denote by
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
the set of all Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers from
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
to
(\beta,u_{\beta})
.
We also write
FS(\alpha,u_{\alpha}):=FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]
.

Example 7.6
.

If
\mathcal{G}=G
is a discrete group then
r^{*}\mathcal{A}\equiv G\times A
and
A_{r(g)}=A
for every
g\in G
.
Thus if
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
are twisted group actions of
\mathcal{G}
respectively on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
, we may identify the Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers in
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
with maps
T_{E,L,\xi,\zeta}:G\times A\to B
given by
T_{E,L,\xi,\zeta}(g,a)=\langle\xi,a\overline{L}_{g}\zeta\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B)}
, for
a\in A
,
\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{M}(E)
, where
L
is an equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on an
A
-
B
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
E
.
When
A=B
is unital, these maps were called Fourier–Stieltjes coefficients in
[
7
]
.
In contrast to
[
7
]
we do not require the unitality of the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras in question and also allow different ‘source’ and ‘target’ algebras.

Example 7.7
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is the trivial one-dimensional bundle over
X
, so that
A=\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
and
r^{*}\mathcal{A}
is the trivial one-dimensional
\mathcal{G}
-bundle, we may identify the Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers with Fourier–Stieltjes coefficients
T_{\mathcal{H},\ell,\xi,\zeta}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathbb{C}
given by
T_{\mathcal{H},\ell,\xi,\zeta}(g)=\langle\xi(r(g)),\ell_{g}\zeta(s(g))\rangle
, where
(\mathcal{H},\ell)
is a
\mathcal{G}
-Hilbert bundle and
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{H})
, see for instance
[
52
]
(in the untwisted case) and compare to Examples
4.12
and
5.6
.

Example 7.8
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle over
X
, carrying a twisted action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of
\mathcal{G}
, then the identity map on
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
arises as
a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier.
Namely, the unit section
1
belongs to
\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
, for the multiplier
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})=\{\mathcal{M}(A_{x})\}_{x\in X}
, and
T_{\mathcal{A},\alpha,1,1}\in FS(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
is a multiplier consisting of identities on each fibre.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Every Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T:=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is
\mathrm{C_{0}}
-completely bounded, meaning that the operator
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
is completely bounded, where the spaces are equipped with the supremum norm.
In fact,
\|m_{T}\|_{cb}\leq\|\xi\|\cdot\|\zeta\|
.
The same statement holds if we view
m_{T}
as an operator mapping
\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
to
\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
.

Proof.

Fix
T:=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
.
The Schwarz inequality in the
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
-Hilbert module
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
implies that
\|m_{T}(a)\|\leq\|a\|\|\xi\|\cdot\|\zeta\|
for
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
Hence we have a bounded operator
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
with
\|m_{T}\|\leq\|\xi\|\cdot\|\zeta\|
.
This generalises to amplifications.
Indeed, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have a natural identification of matrix amplifications
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(r^{*}\mathcal{A}))\cong\mathrm{C_{0}}(r^{*}(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A}))
, where
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A})
is the Fell bundle over
X
with fibres given by
n
by
n
matrices over fibres of
\mathcal{A}
(and the twisted action of
\mathcal{G}
extended to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{A})
in the obvious way), see Example
4.13
.
Thus to estimate the norm of
m_{T}^{(n)}:\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}))\to\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}))
it suffices to fix
g\in\mathcal{G}
and estimate the norm of
T_{g}^{(n)}:\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{r(g)})\to\mathbb{M}_{n}(B_{r(g)})
.
A straightforward computation shows that given a matrix
a=[a_{ij}]_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{r(g)})
we have

T_{g}^{(n)}(a)=\langle\tilde{\xi},a\cdot\tilde{\zeta}\rangle,

where
\tilde{\xi}=[\xi(r(g))\delta_{ij}]_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{E}_{r(g)})
,
\tilde{\zeta}=[\overline{L}_{g}\zeta(s(g))\delta_{ij}]_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{E}_{r(g)})
.
As before, the Schwarz inequality shows that

\|T_{g}^{(n)}(a)\|\leq\|a\|\|\tilde{\xi}\|\|\tilde{\zeta}\|,

and it remains to note that by
[
10
, Section 3, Formula (1’)]
we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\xi}\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\langle\tilde{\xi}_{ki},\tilde{\xi}_{kj}\rangle\Big]_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}=\Big\|\big[\langle\xi(r(g)),\xi(r(g))\rangle\delta_{i,j}\big]_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\|\langle\xi(r(g)),\xi(r(g))\rangle\|^{\frac{1}{2}}=\|\xi(r(g))\|\leq\|\xi\|

and similarly
\|\tilde{\zeta}\|=\|\zeta(s(g))\|\leq\|\zeta\|
.
Hence
\|m_{T}^{(n)}\|=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|T_{g}^{(n)}\|\leq\|\xi\|\cdot\|\zeta\|
.
∎

For fixed
\mathcal{G}
, the class of twisted actions with (positive-definite) Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers as morphisms forms a Banach
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule category.

Proposition 7.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
The Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
form an involutive Banach
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule with the algebraic structure inherited from
m(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
and the norm

(7.4)

\|T\|_{FS}:=\inf\{\|\xi\|\|\zeta\|:T=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}\text{ for some }(\mathcal{E},L)\text{ and }\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))\}.

Moreover, the composition of Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers is a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier, the norm
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
is submultiplicative and the identities are Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers.
In particular,
FS(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
is a unital Banach algebra and involutive Banach
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule with a multiplicative, conjugate-linear involution.

Proof.

The fact that the collection of Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is closed under linear combinations, and in fact forms a
\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{G}^{(0)})
-bimodule follows from the following straightforward relations

f\cdot T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\overline{f}\xi,\zeta},\quad T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}\cdot f=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,f\zeta},\quad T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi_{1},\zeta_{1}}+T_{\mathcal{F},K,\xi_{2},\zeta_{2}}=T_{\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{F},L\oplus K,\xi_{1}\oplus\xi_{2},\zeta_{1}\oplus\zeta_{2}}.

These also imply that
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
is a seminorm.
Since
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
majorises the operator norm in
\mathbb{B}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(r^{*}\mathcal{A}),\mathrm{C_{0}}(r^{*}\mathcal{B}))
, see Lemma
7.9
, and clearly
m_{T}=0
if and only if
T=0
, the expression
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
is in fact a norm.
That
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is closed
under the involution, and that the involution is isometric, follows from the relation

(T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta})^{\dagger}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\zeta,\xi}.

Indeed, set
T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
and let
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
a\in A_{r(g)}
.
Using relations in Remarks
4.7
,
5.2
and the fact that
\alpha_{g}(u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*})u_{\alpha}(g,g^{-1})=1_{\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})}
we get:

\begin{split}T_{g^{-1}}(a^{*})^{*}&=\left(\langle\xi(r(g^{-1})),u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*}\alpha_{g^{-1}}(a^{*})\overline{L}_{g^{-1}}\zeta(s(g^{-1}))\rangle\right)^{*}\\
&=u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1})^{*}\beta_{g}\left(\langle u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*}\alpha_{g^{-1}}(a^{*})\overline{L}_{g^{-1}}\zeta(r(g)),\xi(s(g))\rangle\right)\\
&=u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1})^{*}\langle L_{g}u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*}L_{g^{-1}}a^{*}\zeta(r(g)),L_{g}\xi(s(g))\rangle\\
&=u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1})^{*}\langle\alpha_{g}(u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*})L_{g}L_{g^{-1}}a^{*}\zeta(r(g)),L_{g}\xi(s(g))\rangle\\
&=u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1})^{*}\langle\alpha_{g}(u_{\alpha}(g^{-1},g)^{*})u_{\alpha}(g,g^{-1})a^{*}\zeta(r(g))u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1})^{*},L_{g}\xi(s(g))\rangle\\
&=\langle a^{*}\zeta(r(g)),L_{g}\xi(s(g))\rangle=\langle\zeta(r(g)),aL_{g}\xi(s(g))\rangle=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\zeta,\xi}(a)(g).\end{split}

That Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers are closed under composition follows from the relation

(7.5)

T_{\mathcal{F},K,\xi_{2},\zeta_{2}}\circ T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi_{1},\zeta_{1}}=T_{\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F},L\otimes K,\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2},\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2}},

where we use the notation from Examples
3.10
and
5.12
.
The displayed relation holds because

\begin{split}T_{\mathcal{F},K,\xi_{2},\zeta_{2}}(T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi_{1},\zeta_{1}}(a))(g)&=\langle\xi_{2}(r(g)),T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi_{1},\zeta_{1}}(a)\overline{K}_{g}\zeta_{2}(s(g))\rangle\\
&=\langle\xi_{2}(r(g)),\langle\xi_{1}(r(g)),a(g)\overline{L}_{g}\zeta_{1}(s(g))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})}\overline{K}_{g}\zeta_{2}(s(g))\rangle\\
&=\langle(\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2})(r(g)),a(g)\overline{L}_{g}\zeta_{1}(s(g))\otimes\overline{K}_{g}\zeta_{2}(s(g))\rangle\\
&=T_{\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F},L\otimes K,\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2},\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2}}(a)(g).\end{split}

Using this relation one sees that the norm
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
is submultiplicative.
To prove that
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is complete in this norm we take
\{T_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|T_{i}\|_{FS}<\infty
and we need to show that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}T_{i}
is convergent in
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
.
We may pick
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondences
\{(\mathcal{E}_{i},L_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
and non-zero sections
\{\xi_{i},\zeta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}_{i}))
such that
T_{i}=T_{\mathcal{E}_{i},L_{i},\xi_{i},\zeta_{i}}
and
\|\xi_{i}\|\|\zeta_{i}\|\leq\|T_{i}\|+1/2^{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
In addition (by passing to
\sqrt{\frac{|\zeta_{i}\|}{\|\xi_{i}\|}}\xi_{i}
and
\sqrt{\frac{|\xi_{i}\|}{\|\zeta_{i}\|}}\zeta_{i}
) we may assume that
\|\xi_{i}\|=\|\zeta_{i}\|
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then it follows that
\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}\xi_{i}
and
\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}\zeta_{i}
belong to
\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}_{i}))
because
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|\zeta_{i}\|^{2}<\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|T_{i}\|_{FS}+1
.
Let
T:=T_{\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}_{i},\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}L_{i},\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}\xi_{i},\oplus_{i=1}^{\infty}\zeta_{i}}
.
Then

\begin{split}\|T-\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}\|_{FS}&\leq\|\oplus_{i=n+1}^{\infty}\xi_{i}\|\cdot\|\oplus_{i=n+1}^{\infty}\zeta_{i}\|\leq\sum_{i=n+1}^{\infty}\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}\sum_{i=n+1}\|\zeta_{i}\|^{2}\\
&<\left(\sum_{i=n+1}^{\infty}\|T_{i}\|_{FS}+1/2^{i}\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.\end{split}

By Example
7.8
,
T_{\mathcal{A},\alpha,1,1}
is the unit in
FS(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and clearly its FS-norm equals
1
.
∎

Example 7.11
.

Applying the above construction to actions on a trivial line bundle
\{\mathbb{C}\}_{x\in X}
, cf. Example
5.6
, we get the
Fourier–Stieltjes algebra
FS(\mathcal{G})
of the groupoid
\mathcal{G}
, similar to what was considered in
[
55
]
or
[
50
]
.
In our setting,
FS(\mathcal{G})
is the space of all (continuous, bounded) functions
\varphi\colon\mathcal{G}\to\mathbb{C}
of the form
\varphi(g)=\langle\xi(r(g)),\ell_{g}(\zeta(s(g)))\rangle
, for
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{H})
of a (continuous)
\mathcal{G}
-Hilbert bundle
(\mathcal{H},\ell)
.
By the above proposition,
FS(\mathcal{G})
is an involutive Banach algebra with respect to the pointwise product of functions
\mathcal{G}\to\mathbb{C}
, the conjugation as involution, and the norm given as in (
7.4
).
By Remark
5.7
, we get the same algebra
FS(\mathcal{G},u)=FS(\mathcal{G})
for any (continuous)
2
-cocycle
u
on
\mathcal{G}
.

Proposition 7.12
.

For a twisted action
(\alpha,u)
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{A}
, we have an injective contractive involutive algebra homomorphism
FS(\mathcal{G})\ni\varphi\mapsto T^{\varphi}\in FS(\alpha,u)
where
T^{\varphi}_{g}(a):=\varphi(g)a
, for
a\in A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.

Proof.

Take any continuous
\mathcal{G}
-Hilbert bundle
(\mathcal{H},\ell)
and sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{H})
that define
\varphi
, i.e.
\varphi(g)=\langle\xi(r(g)),\ell_{g}(\zeta(s(g)))\rangle
, for
g\in\mathcal{G}
.
Tensor
(\mathcal{H},\ell)
with the trivial equivariant representation
(\mathcal{A},\alpha)
of
(\alpha,u)
, see Examples
5.9
,
5.10
.
For
a\in A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
we have

\begin{split}T_{\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{H},\alpha\otimes\ell,1\otimes\xi,1\otimes\zeta}(g)(a)&=\langle(1\otimes\xi)\big(r(g)\big),a\cdot\overline{\alpha\otimes\ell}_{g}(1\otimes\zeta)\big(s(g)\big)\rangle=a\langle\xi\big(r(g)),\ell_{g}\big(\zeta(s(g))\big)\rangle\\
&=\varphi(g)a=T^{\varphi}_{g}(a).\end{split}

Hence
T^{\varphi}\in FS(\alpha,u)
and
\|T^{\varphi}\|_{FS}\leq\|1\otimes\xi\|\|1\otimes\zeta\|\leq\|\xi\|\|\zeta\|
, which implies
\|T^{\varphi}\|_{FS}\leq\|\varphi\|_{FS}
.
The map
\varphi\mapsto T^{\varphi}
is clearly injective, and it is easily checked that it is an involutive algebra homomorphism.
∎

The next theorem is one of the central results in this paper.
Its proof uses the absorption principles established in Sections
5
and
6
and the inverse semigroup picture introduced in Section
4
.

Theorem 7.13
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Every Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
yields strict completely bounded maps
m_{T}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
and
m_{T}^{\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
between reduced and full crossed products, and
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb},\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{cb}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
.
If
T=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
for some equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
and section
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
, then
m_{T}^{r}
and
m_{T}^{f}
are completely positive and
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}=\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{cb}=\|T\|_{FS}=\|\xi\|^{2}
.

Proof.

Let us fix an equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
, sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
and the corresponding Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T:=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
.
To deal with reduced crossed products we will use our first version of the Fell absorption principle.
Using the notation from Example
3.10
and Theorem
6.15
, we define the (creation) operator
\theta_{\xi}:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi^{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
by the formula
\theta_{\xi}(b):=\xi\otimes b
,
b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
; for an approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
we have
\xi\otimes b=\lim_{i}\xi\otimes\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(e_{i})b=\lim_{i}\xi e_{i}\otimes b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi^{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
.
The operator
\theta_{\xi}
is adjointable with norm dominated by
\|\xi\|
and adjoint given by the formula

(7.6)

\theta_{\xi}^{*}\big(a\zeta\otimes b)=\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\langle\xi,a\zeta\rangle_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})})b,

for
a\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
and
b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))
(then
a\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
, so the inner product takes values in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
and the formula makes sense).
Using the unitary
W:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi^{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B}))\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
from Theorem
6.15
we define a map
\Psi:\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})))\to\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})))
by the formula

\Psi(S):=\theta_{\xi}^{*}W^{*}SW\theta_{\zeta},\qquad S\in\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))).

By its form,
\Psi
is completely bounded and strict, with
\|\Psi\|_{cb}\leq\|\xi\|\|\zeta\|
.
We claim that
\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(m_{T}(a))=\Psi(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(a))
for
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
Indeed, by (
6.4
) it suffices to check that for every
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
,
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
,
b\in\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{B})
, we have
\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(m_{T}(a))b=\theta_{\xi}^{*}(aL_{U}(\zeta)\otimes\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(\delta_{U})(b))
.
But this readily follows from (
7.6
).
Since
\pi^{\mathcal{B}}
extends to a faithful representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
, and similarly for
\pi^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
(see the last part of Proposition
6.4
),
we conclude that
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
extends to a map
m_{T}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
with

\pi^{\mathcal{B}}(m_{T}^{\rd}(a))=\Psi(\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(a))=\theta_{\xi}^{*}W^{*}\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(a)W\theta_{\zeta},\qquad a\in\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}).

This implies that
m_{T}^{\rd}
is strict, completely bounded and

\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}=\|\Psi\circ\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\|_{cb}\leq\|\Psi\|_{cb}\leq\|\xi\|\|\zeta\|.

In particular,
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
(since the presentation of
T
was arbitrary).
If
\xi=\zeta
, then
\Psi
and hence also
m_{T}^{\rd}
is completely positive and for any approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
we get

\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}=\|\Psi\circ\pi^{\mathcal{E}}\|_{cb}=\lim_{i}\|\Psi\circ\pi^{\mathcal{E}}(e_{i})\|=\|\theta_{\xi}^{*}W^{*}W\theta_{\xi}\|=\|\xi\|^{2}.

Thus we have
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}=\|T\|_{FS}=\|\xi\|^{2}
.

To deal with full crossed products let
\psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\to\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})
be a faithful nondegenerate representation of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
on a Hilbert space
H
.
We view
\psi=\pi\rtimes v
as the integrated form of a covariant representation
(\pi,v)
of the associated inverse semigroup action, see Subsection
4.1
.
Consider the induced representation
L\dashind(\psi):\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathbb{B}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi}H)
as in Proposition
5.14
.
Similarly to above we define the (creation) operator
\theta_{\xi}:H\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\pi}H
by
\theta_{\xi}(\pi(b)h):=(\xi\cdot b)\otimes h
for
b\in B=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
and
h\in H
.
In other words,
\theta_{\xi}(h)=\lim_{i}\xi e_{i}^{B}\otimes h
where
\{e_{i}^{B}\}
is an approximate unit in
B
.
Its adjoint is determined by
\theta_{\xi}^{*}\big(a\zeta\otimes h)=\pi(\langle a^{*}\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B})h
, where
\zeta\in E
,
a\in A
, and
h\in H
. So
\theta_{\xi}^{*}(\zeta\otimes h)=\lim_{i}\pi(\langle e_{i}^{A}\xi,\zeta\rangle_{B})h
where
\{e_{i}^{A}\}
is an approximate unit in
A
.
We define a completely bounded map
\Psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to B(H)
by the formula

\Psi(b):=\theta_{\xi}^{*}(L\dashind(\psi))(b)\theta_{\zeta}.

We claim that
\Psi(b)=\psi(T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}(b))
for all
b\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
It suffices to check that for
b=a\delta_{U}
where
a\in A_{r(U)}
,
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
.
Then for any
g\in U
we have

T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}(b)(g)=\langle\xi(r(g)),L_{g}(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(a(r(g))\zeta(s(g))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}=\lim_{i}\langle e_{i}^{A}\xi,L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\zeta)\rangle_{B}(r(g)).

That is
T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}(b)\circ r|_{U}^{-1}=\lim_{i}\langle e_{i}^{A}\xi,L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\zeta)\rangle_{B}
.
Using this we get

\begin{split}\Psi(b)h&=\lim_{i}\theta_{\xi}^{*}(L\dashind(\psi))(b)(\zeta\cdot e_{i}^{B})\otimes h=\lim_{i}\theta_{\xi}^{*}\big(L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\zeta)\otimes v_{U}h\big)\\
&=\lim_{i}\pi(\langle e_{i}^{A}\xi,L_{U}(\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\zeta)\rangle_{B})v_{U}h=\psi(T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}(b))h.\end{split}

This proves our claim and implies that the completely bounded map
\Psi
maps
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
to
\psi(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}))
.
Thus, putting
m_{T}^{\f}:=\psi^{-1}\circ\Psi
, we get the desired multiplier map
m_{T}^{\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
.
The assertions concerning norms follow as in the first part.
∎

Note that the above theorem will be later enriched by the discussion of the
decomposable
norms of the maps
m_{T}^{r}
and
m_{T}^{s}
, see Proposition
11.13
and Theorem
11.17
.

The set
CB[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
of completely bounded
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-bimodule maps
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
has a similar structure to that of Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers described in Proposition
7.10
.
Namely, it is a Banach space with pointwise operations and the norm
\|\cdot\|_{cb}
.
It is also an involutive Banach
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule, where the bimodule structure is determined by

f\cdot\Psi(a):=\Psi((f\circ r)\cdot a),\qquad\Psi\cdot f(a):=\Psi(a\cdot(f\circ s)),\qquad f\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(X),\,a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}),

with
\Psi\in CB[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
, and the involution is given by

\Psi^{\dagger}(a):=\Psi(a^{*})^{*},\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}),

cf.
[
68
, Proposition 1]
.
This involution is called
conjugation
in
[
68
]
,
[
7
]
.
In the same way, the set
CB^{\red}[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
of completely bounded
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-bimodule maps
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
forms an involutive Banach
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule.

Remark 7.14
.

Viewing the class of all twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
as objects, denote by
FS_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
the category where morphisms are Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers, and by
CB_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
(resp.
CB_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
) the category where morphisms are completely bounded, strict
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-bimodule maps between full (resp. reduced) crossed products, equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{cb}
.
The extensions in Theorem
7.13
give contractive involutive
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule functors
m^{\f}:FS_{TA}(\mathcal{G})\to CB_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
and
m^{\rd}:FS_{TA}(\mathcal{G})\to CB_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
.

8.
Positive-definiteness and multipliers on section C*-algebras

In this section we will specify our considerations to the completely positive multipliers.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be Fell bundles over
\mathcal{G}
.
We say that a multiplier
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
is
positive-definite
if for any
x\in X
, any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, any
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
and any collections
\{a_{g_{i}}\in A_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
and
\{b_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
we have

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}b_{g_{i}}^{*}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*})\right)b_{g_{j}}\geq 0

(note that the last sum takes values in
B_{x}
).
Then each of the maps
T_{x}\in\mathbb{B}(A_{x},B_{x})
,
x\in X
, is completely positive, and we say that
T
is
strict
if each map
T_{x}:A_{x}\to B_{x}
,
x\in X
, is strict, and we call
T

bounded
if
\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}\|<\infty
.

Remark 8.2
.

By passing to adjoints and inverses (replacing
g_{i}
by
g_{i}^{-1}
and putting
\widetilde{a}_{g_{i}}:=a_{g_{i}^{-1}}^{*}
,
\widetilde{b}_{g_{i}}:=b_{g_{i}^{-1}}^{*}
) one sees that a multiplier
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
is positive-definite if and only if for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, any
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}^{x}
and any collections
\{a_{g_{i}}\in A_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
and
\{b_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
we have

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}b_{g_{i}}\left(T_{g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}}(a_{g_{i}}^{*}a_{g_{j}})\right)b_{g_{j}}^{*}\geq 0.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{A_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be Fell bundles over
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be a reduced or full multiplier from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
.
If the corresponding map
m_{T}
between cross-sectional
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras is completely positive, then
T
is positive-definite and bounded (in fact we have
\|m_{T}\|=\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}\|
), and
T
is strict if
m_{T}
is strict.

Proof.

Fix
x\in X
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, a family
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
and collections
\{a_{g_{i}}\in A_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
,
\{b_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
.
We may find sections
\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
supported on open bisections and such that
f_{i}(g_{i})=a_{g_{i}}
,
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then
(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})(g_{i}{g_{j}}^{-1})=a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}
for
1\leq i,j\leq n
.
Since the matrix
(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})_{i,j=1}^{n}
is positive both as an element of
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}))
and of
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}))
, and
m_{T}^{(n)}
is positive by assumption, we conclude that the operator

b:=\Lambda_{x}^{(n)}\circ m_{T}^{(n)}\big((f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})_{i,j=1}^{n}\big)\in\mathcal{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{B})_{x}^{\oplus n})

is positive.
Choose
\xi_{i}\in\ell^{2}(\mathcal{B})_{x}=\bigoplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}B_{h}
such that
\xi_{i}(g_{j})=[i=j]b_{g_{i}}
for
1\leq i,j\leq n
, and let
\xi=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\in\ell^{2}(\mathcal{B})_{x}^{\oplus n}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\bigoplus_{h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}B_{h}
.
Then

0\leq\langle\xi,b\xi\rangle_{B_{x}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\langle\xi_{i},\Lambda_{x}(m_{T}(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*}))\xi_{j}\rangle_{B_{x}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}b_{g_{i}}^{*}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*})\right)b_{g_{j}}.

Hence
T
is a positive-definite multiplier.
Since any approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{X})
is also an approximate unit in
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A})
and in
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A})
, and
m_{T}
is a completely positive map, we get

\|m_{T}\|=\lim_{i\in I}\|m_{T}(e_{i})\|=\lim_{i\in I}\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}(e_{i}(x))\|.

Similarly, since, for any
x\in X
, the net
\{e_{i}(x)\}_{i\in I}
is an approximate unit in
A_{x}
, and
T_{x}:A_{x}\to B_{x}
is a completely positive map, we also get
\|T_{x}\|=\lim_{i\in I}\|T_{x}(e_{i}(x))\|
.
Combining these equalities one gets
\|m_{T}\|=\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}\|
.
Finally, if
m_{T}
is strict, then the net
\{T(e_{i})\}_{i\in I}
converges strictly to a positive element
T(1)\in\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{X}))
.
For any
x\in X
, the net
\{e_{i}(x)\}_{i\in I}
is an approximate unit in
A_{x}
.
For any
a\in B_{x}
we may find
b\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{G},\alpha)
with
b(x)=a
, and then

\lim_{i\in I}T_{x}(e_{i}(x))a=\lim_{i\in I}T_{x}(e_{i}(x))b(x)=\lim_{i\in I}\big(T(e_{i})b\big)(x)=\big(T(1)b\big)(x).

Thus the map
T_{x}:A_{x}\to B_{x}
is strict.
∎

We will now pass to the context of twisted groupoid actions.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
For a multiplier
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
the following are equivalent:

(1)

T
is positive-definite;

(2)

for any
x\in X
,
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and any collection
\{a_{g_{i}}\in A_{x}:1\leq i\leq n\}
the following matrix is positive:

\left(\beta_{g_{i}}^{-1}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}\big(\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*})u_{\alpha}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})^{*}\big)u_{\beta}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})\right)\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(B_{x});

(3)

for any
x\in X
,
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}^{x}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and any collection
\{a_{g_{i}}\in A_{x}:1\leq i\leq n\}
the following matrix is positive:

\left(\beta_{g_{i}}\left(T_{g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}}\big(\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(a_{g_{i}}^{*}a_{g_{j}}u_{\alpha}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})^{*})\big)\right)u_{\beta}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(B_{x}).

Proof.

(1)
\iff
(2)
Fix
x\in X
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and a
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
.
Choosing
\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}\in A_{g_{i}}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}=A_{r(g_{i})}
and
\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}=B_{r(g_{i})}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
, is equivalent to choosing
a_{g_{i}}\in A_{x}
and
b_{g_{i}}\in B_{x}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
, by putting
\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}=\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{g_{i}})\delta_{g_{i}}
and
\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}=\beta_{g_{i}}(b_{g_{i}})\delta_{g_{i}}
.
Using the fact that for all
1\leq i,j\leq n

\alpha_{g_{i}}(u_{\alpha}(g_{j}^{-1},g_{j}))=\alpha_{g_{i}}(u_{\alpha}(g_{j}^{-1},g_{j}))u_{\alpha}(g_{i},x)=u_{\alpha}(g_{i},g_{j}^{-1})u_{\alpha}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j}),

one gets
\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}\cdot(\tilde{a}_{g_{j}})^{*}=\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*})u_{\alpha}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})^{*}\delta_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}
.
Similarly, for
1\leq i,j\leq n
,

\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}^{*}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}(\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}\tilde{a}_{g_{j}}^{*})\right)\tilde{b}_{g_{j}}=b_{g_{i}}^{*}\cdot\beta_{g_{i}}^{-1}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}\big(\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*})u_{\alpha}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})^{*}\big)u_{\beta}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})\right)\cdot b_{g_{j}}.

Hence positivity of the elements
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}^{*}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}(\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}\tilde{a}_{g_{j}}^{*})\right)\tilde{b}_{g_{j}}
for all choices of elements
\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
is equivalent to positivity of the matrix in the assertion.

(1)
\iff
(3)
We will use the version of
(1)
described in Remark
8.2
.
Fix
x\in X
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}^{x}
.
Choosing
a_{g_{i}}\in A_{x}
and
b_{g_{i}}\in B_{x}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
, is equivalent to choosing
\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}\in A_{g_{i}}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
and
\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
, by putting
\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}=a_{g_{i}}\delta_{g_{i}}
and
\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}=\beta_{g_{i}}(b_{g_{i}})\delta_{g_{i}}
.
Fix then
i,j
.
The equality
\alpha_{g_{i}}(u_{\alpha}(g_{i}^{-1},g_{j}))u(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})=u_{\alpha}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1})=\alpha_{g}(u_{\alpha}(g_{i}^{-1},g_{i}))
is equivalent to

u_{\alpha}(g_{i}^{-1},g_{i})^{*}u_{\alpha}(g_{i}^{-1},g_{j})=\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(u_{\alpha}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})^{*}).

Using the last equality one gets
\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}^{*}\cdot(\tilde{a}_{g_{j}})=\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}\big(a_{g_{i}}^{*}a_{g_{j}}u_{\alpha}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})^{*}\big)\delta_{g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}}
.
Similarly,

\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}\left(T_{g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}}(\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}^{*}\tilde{a}_{g_{j}})\right)\tilde{b}_{g_{j}}^{*}=b_{g_{i}}\beta_{g_{i}}\left(T_{g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}}\big(\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(a_{g_{i}}^{*}a_{g_{j}}u_{\alpha}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})^{*})\big)\right)u_{\beta}(g_{i},g_{i}^{-1}g_{j})\cdot b_{g_{j}}^{*}.

Hence positivity of the elements
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}\left(T_{g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}}(\tilde{a}_{g_{i}}^{*}\tilde{a}_{g_{j}})\right)\tilde{b}_{g_{j}}^{*}
for all choices of elements
\tilde{b}_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
is equivalent to positivity of the matrix in the assertion.
∎

We are ready to formulate and prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
, and set
A:=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}),

B:=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B})
.
For any multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

T
is strict, bounded and positive-definite;

(2)

m_{T}
extends to a strict completely positive map
m_{T}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
;

(3)

m_{T}
extends to a strict completely positive map
m_{T}^{\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
;

(4)

T=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
for some equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
and section
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
.

If the above equivalent conditions hold, then
\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{cb}=\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}=\|m_{T}^{\f}\|=\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|=\|T\|_{FS}=\|\xi\|^{2}
and the triple
(\mathcal{E},L,\xi)
in
(4)
can be chosen to be cyclic, in the sense that the set

\{L_{U}(a\xi b):a\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{s(U)}),\ b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{s(U)}),\ U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})\}

is linearly dense in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
.
Moreover, a cyclic triple
(\mathcal{E},L,\xi)
in
(4)
is unique up to a unitary equivalence in the sense that if
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},L^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
is another cyclic triple with
T=T_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime},L^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}
, then there is an
A
-
B
-bimodule
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-unitary
W:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
establishing equivalence between
L
and
L^{\prime}
, and sending
\xi
to
\xi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The implications
(4)
\implies
(2)
,
(3)
follow from Theorem
7.13
and the implications
(2)
,
(3)
\implies
(1)
are a consequence of Lemma
8.3
.

Thus we only need to prove that
(1)
implies
(4)
.
Fix a strict, bounded and positive-definite multiplier
T
.
For each
x\in X
define
k_{x}:\mathcal{G}^{x}\times\mathcal{G}^{x}\to\mathcal{B}(A_{x},B_{x})
via

k_{x}(g,h)(a):=\beta_{g}\left(T_{g^{-1}h}\big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(au_{\alpha}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*})\big)\right)u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\qquad g,h\in\mathcal{G}_{x}.

Then by Lemma
8.4
,
k_{x}
is positive-definite and strict in the sense of Corollary
2.4
.
Thus, by this latter result, there is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
E_{x}
from
A_{x}
to
B_{x}
and a map
\xi_{x}:\mathcal{G}^{x}\to\mathcal{M}(E_{x})
such that the set
E_{x}^{0}:=\{a\xi_{x}(g)b:g\in\mathcal{G}^{x},\ a\in A_{x},b\in B_{x}\}
is linearly dense in
E_{x}
, and for
g,h\in\mathcal{G}^{x}
,
a\in A_{x}
, we have

(8.1)

\langle\xi_{x}(g),a\cdot\xi_{x}(h)\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{x})}=\beta_{g}\left(T_{g^{-1}h}\big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(au_{\alpha}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*})\big)\right)u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\in B_{x}.

In this way, we get a family of
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondences
\mathcal{E}:=\{E_{x}\}_{x\in X}
.
Define the section
\xi:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E})=\{\mathcal{M}(E_{x})\}_{x\in X}
by setting
\xi(g):=\xi_{r(g)}(g)
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.
To topologise
\mathcal{E}
we let
\Gamma
be the linear span of sections of the form
a\xi_{U}b
, where
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
,
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
,
b\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}|_{r(U)})
and

(a\xi_{U}b)(x):=a(x)\xi(r|_{U}^{-1}(x))b(x),\qquad x\in X.

If
c\xi_{V}d\in\Gamma
is another element of this form, with
V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
,
c\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(V})
,
d\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}|_{r(V)})
, then
\langle a\xi_{U}b,c\xi_{V}d\rangle_{B}(x):=\langle a\xi_{U}b(x),c\xi_{V}d(x)\rangle_{B_{x}}
is zero outside
r(U)\cap r(V)
and writing
g:=r|_{U}^{-1}(x)
and
h:=r|_{V}^{-1}(x)
for
x\in r(U)\cap r(V)
, by (
8.1
) we get

\langle a\xi_{U}b,c\xi_{V}d\rangle_{B}(x)=b(x)^{*}\Big(\beta_{g}\Big(T_{g^{-1}h}\big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}(a(x)^{*}c(x)u_{\alpha}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*})\big)\Big)u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\Big)d(x).

By continuity of all the operations involved, we see that the map
X\ni x\mapsto\langle a\xi_{U}b,c\xi_{V}d\rangle_{B}(x)\in B_{x}
belongs to
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})
.
It extends uniquely to the (positive-definite) conjugate-linear map
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{B}:\Gamma\times\Gamma\to\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B})\subseteq B
.
In particular, for any
a\in\Gamma
the map
X\mapsto\|a(x)\|^{2}=\|\langle a,a\rangle_{B}(x)\|
is upper semicontinuous.
The fact that for each
x\in X
the values of the prescribed sections are dense in the fibre
E_{x}
follows immediately from the minimality assumption on each
E_{x}
and the above construction of
\Gamma
.
Hence
\mathcal{E}
admits a unique topology making it a Banach bundle such that
\Gamma\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E})
.
The left
\mathcal{A}
and right
\mathcal{B}
-module actions on
\mathcal{E}
are continuous by the very definition of sections, and the
B
-valued inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{B}
extends uniquely to
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
so that
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
becomes a
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
B
.

We claim that there is an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
determined by

(8.2)

L_{g}(a\xi(h)b):=\alpha_{g}(a)u_{\alpha}{(g,h)}\xi(gh)u_{\beta}(g,h)^{*}\beta_{g}(b),

where
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
h\in\mathcal{G}^{s(g)},a\in A_{s(g)},b\in B_{s(g)}
.
The most difficult (technically involved) part of the proof is showing that for
h,f\in\mathcal{G}^{s(g)}
,
a,c\in A_{s(g)}
,
b,d\in B_{s(g)}
, we have

(8.3)

\langle L_{g}(a\xi(h)b),L_{g}(c\xi(f)d)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}=\beta_{g}(\langle a\xi(h)b,c\xi(f)d\rangle_{B_{s(g)}}).

We denote by
L
and
R
the left and right hand side of (
8.3
).
To make the calculations easier to read and write we abbreviate
u_{g,h}:=u_{\alpha}{(g,h)}
and
u_{g,h}:=u_{\beta}{(g,h)}
as it is clear from the context whether we twist
\alpha
or
\beta
.
Then

\begin{split}R&=\beta_{g}\Big(b^{*}\beta_{h}\Big(T_{h^{-1}f}\big(\alpha_{h}^{-1}(a^{*}c\cdot u^{*}_{h,h^{-1}f}\big)\Big)u_{h,h^{-1}f}\,d\Big)\\
&=\beta_{g}(b^{*})u_{g,h}\,\beta_{gh}\Big(T_{h^{-1}f}\big(\alpha_{h}^{-1}(a^{*}c\cdot u^{*}_{h,h^{-1}f})\big)\Big)u^{*}_{g,h}\beta_{g}(u^{\beta}_{h,h^{-1}f})\beta_{g}(d)\\
&=\beta_{g}(b^{*})u_{g,h}\beta_{gh}\Big(T_{h^{-1}f}\big(\alpha_{h}^{-1}(a^{*}c\cdot u^{*}_{h,h^{-1}f})\big)\Big)u_{gh,h^{-1}f}u^{*}_{g,h}\beta_{g}(d),\end{split}

where in the last equality we used the cocycle identity.
The left hand side is equal to

\begin{split}L&=\Big\langle\alpha_{g}(a)u_{g,h}\xi(gh)u^{*}_{g,h}\beta_{g}(b),\alpha_{g}(c)u_{g,f}\xi(gf)u^{*}_{g,f}\beta_{g}(d)\Big\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}\\
&=\beta_{g}(b^{*})u_{g,h}\beta_{gh}\Big(T_{h^{-1}f}\big(\alpha_{gh}^{-1}(u^{*}_{g,h}\alpha_{g}(a^{*}c)u_{g,f}u^{*}_{gh,h^{-1}f})\big)\Big)u_{gh,h^{-1}f}u^{*}_{g,h}\beta_{g}(d).\end{split}

Thus we need to check that the terms that
T_{h^{-1}f}
acts on are equal.
We compute

\begin{split}\alpha_{gh}^{-1}\big(u^{*}_{g,h}\alpha_{g}(a^{*}c)u_{g,f}u^{*}_{gh,h^{-1}f}\big)&=\alpha_{h}^{-1}\alpha_{g}^{-1}\Ad_{u_{g,h}}\big(u^{*}_{g,h}\alpha_{g}(a^{*}c)u_{g,f}u^{*}_{gh,h^{-1}f}\big)\\
&=\alpha_{h}^{-1}\Big(\alpha_{g}^{-1}\big(\alpha_{g}(a^{*}c)u_{g,f}u^{*}_{gh,h^{-1}f}u^{*}_{g,h}\big)\big)\\
&=\alpha_{h}^{-1}\Big(a^{*}c\cdot\alpha_{g}^{-1}\big(u_{g,f}u^{*}_{gh,h^{-1}f}u^{*}_{g,h}\big)\Big)\\
&=\alpha_{h}^{-1}(a^{*}c\cdot u^{*}_{h,h^{-1}f}),\end{split}

where the equality
\alpha_{g}^{-1}(u_{g,f}u^{*}_{gh,h^{-1}f}u^{*}_{g,h})=u^{*}_{h,h^{-1}f}
at the last step follows from the cocycle identity, as we have
\alpha_{g}(u_{h,h^{-1}f})u_{g,f}=u_{g,h}u_{gh,h^{-1}f}
.
This proves that
L=R
, that is (
8.3
) holds.
This last equality implies that the linear extension of the prescription in (
8.2
) defines consistently a linear isometric map
L_{g}:E_{s(g)}^{0}\to E_{r(g)}^{0}
such that, for all
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in E_{s(g)}^{0}
,

(8.4)

\langle L_{h}(\xi_{1}),L_{h}(\xi_{2})\rangle_{B_{r(h)}}=\beta_{h}(\langle\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\rangle_{B_{s(h)}}).

By continuity we get an isometry
L_{g}\in\mathcal{B}(E_{s(g)},E_{r(g)})
where (
8.4
) holds for all
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in E_{s(g)}
.
Next note that (
8.2
) readily implies that

L_{g}(a\cdot a_{0}\xi(h)b_{0}\cdot b)=\alpha_{g}(a)\cdot L_{g}(a_{0}\xi(g)b_{0})\cdot\beta_{g}(b),

for all
a,a_{0}\in A_{s(g)}
,
b,b_{0}\in B_{s(g)}
,
h\in\mathcal{G}^{s(g)}
.
Thus by linearity and continuity

L_{g}(a\cdot\xi\cdot b)=\alpha_{g}(a)\cdot L_{g}(\xi)\cdot\beta_{g}(b),\qquad\xi\in E_{s(g)},\ a\in A_{s(g)},\ b\in B_{s(g)}.

Similarly, to see that for
(f,g)\in\mathcal{G}^{(2)}
we have
L_{f}\circ L_{g}(\cdot)=u_{\alpha}(f,g)L_{fg}(\cdot)u_{\beta}(f,g)^{*}
on
E_{s(g)}
, we only need to check it on
a\xi(h)b
where
f\in\mathcal{G}^{s(g)}
,
a\in A_{s(g)}
,
b\in B_{s(g)}
.
We compute using our shorthand notation for twists:

\begin{split}L_{f}(L_{g}(a\xi(h)b))&=L_{f}\Big(\alpha_{g}(a)\,u_{g,h}\cdot\xi(gh)\cdot u_{g,h}^{*}\,\beta_{g}(b)\Big)\\
&=\alpha_{f}\big(\alpha_{g}(a)u_{g,h}\big)u_{f,gh}\cdot\xi(fgh)\cdot u_{f,gh}^{*}\,\beta_{f}\big(u_{g,h}^{*}\beta_{g}(b)\big)\\
&=\alpha_{f}\big(\alpha_{g}(a)\big)\big(\alpha_{f}(u_{g,h})u_{f,gh}\big)\cdot\xi(fgh)\cdot\big(u_{f,gh}^{*}\,\beta_{f}(u_{g,h}^{*})\big)\beta_{f}\big(\beta_{g}(b)\big)\\
&=\alpha_{f}\big(\alpha_{g}(a)\big)(u_{f,g}u_{fg,h})\cdot\xi(fgh)\cdot(u_{fg,h}^{*}u_{f,g}^{*})\beta_{f}\big(\beta_{g}(b)\big)\\
&=u_{f,g}\alpha_{fg}(a)u_{fg,h}\cdot\xi(fgh)\cdot u_{fg,h}^{*}\,\beta_{fg}(b)u_{f,g}^{*}\\
&=u_{f,g}\,L_{fg}(a\xi(h)b)\,u_{f,g}^{*}.\end{split}

Hence
L=\{L_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
is an
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
-
(\beta,u_{\beta})
-equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
.
Moreover, for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
a\in A_{r(g)}
, (
8.1
) implies that
T_{g}(a)=\langle\xi(r(g)),a\xi(g)\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}\in B_{r(g)}
and (
8.2
) implies that
\overline{L}_{g}(\xi(s(g))=\xi(g)
.
Thus

T_{g}(a)=\langle\xi(r(g)),a\overline{L}_{g}\big(\xi(s(g))\big)\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})},

so that
T=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi|_{X},\xi|_{X}}
where
\xi|_{X}\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
is bounded because
T
is bounded, and in view of the above we have
\|\xi(x)\|^{2}=\|T_{x}\|
for every
x\in X
.
This finishes the proof of
(1)
\implies
(4)
.
Moreover, in view of (
8.2
) we have
L_{U}(a\xi|_{X}b)=\alpha_{U}(a)\xi_{U}\beta_{U}(b)
for every
a\in A_{s(U)}
,
b\in A_{s(U)}
,
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
.
The linear span of such elements is equal to
\Gamma
, which by construction is dense in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
.
Hence the triple
(\mathcal{E},L,\xi|_{X})
is cyclic as in the second part of the assertion.

Finally, let
(\mathcal{E},L,\xi)
,
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},L^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
, be two cyclic triples such that
T_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime},L^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
.
We claim that for
a\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{s(U)})
,
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, and
c\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{s(V)})
,
V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})

(8.5)

\langle L_{U}(a\xi),L_{V}(b\xi)\rangle=\langle L_{U}^{\prime}(a\xi^{\prime}),L_{V}^{\prime}(c\xi^{\prime})\rangle.

To prove this we express the left hand side only in terms of
a,c
,
U,V
and
T
.
If
x\notin r(U)\cap r(V)
, then
\langle L_{U}(a\xi),L_{V}(b\xi)\rangle(x)=0
.
Assume then that
x\in r(U)\cap r(V)
.
There are unique
g\in U
and
h\in V
such that
r(h)=r(g)=x
.
Put
a_{0}=a(s(g))^{*}\alpha_{g}^{-1}(u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*})
and
c_{0}=\alpha_{g^{-1}{h}}(c(s(h)))
and compute

\begin{split}\langle L_{U}(a\xi),L_{V}(c\xi)\rangle(x)&=\langle L_{g}(a\xi(s(g))),L_{h}(c\xi(s(h))\rangle\\
&=\langle L_{g}(a\xi(s(g))),L_{h}(c\xi(s(h)))u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*}\rangle u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\\
&=\langle L_{g}(a\xi(s(g))),u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*}L_{g}L_{g^{-1}h}(c\xi(s(h)))\rangle u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\\
&=\beta_{g}\left(\langle\xi(s(g)),a(s(g))^{*}\alpha_{g}^{-1}(u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)^{*})L_{g^{-1}h}(c\xi(s(h)))\rangle\right)u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\\
&=\beta_{g}\left(\langle\xi(s(g)),a_{0}c_{0}L_{g^{-1}h}(\xi(s(h)))\rangle\right)u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h)\\
&=\beta_{g}\left(T_{gh^{-1}}(a_{0}c_{0})\right)u_{\beta}(g,g^{-1}h).\end{split}

This proves (
8.5
), which implies that for
a\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{s(U)})
,
b\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{s(U)})
,
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, and
c\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{s(V)})
,
d\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{s(V)})
,
V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
we have

\langle L_{U}(a\xi b),L_{V}(c\xi d)\rangle=\langle L_{U}^{\prime}(a\xi^{\prime}b),L_{V}^{\prime}(c\xi^{\prime}d)\rangle.

Thus if the triples
(\mathcal{E},L,\xi)
,
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},L^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
are cyclic, then by linearity and continuity
the formula
W(L_{U}(a\xi b)):=L_{U}^{\prime}(a\xi^{\prime}b)
determines a unitary
W:\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
with the properties described in the last part of the assertion.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

Extending Remark
7.14
, let
FS_{TA}^{+}(\mathcal{G})
,
CP_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
,
CP_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
be the subcategories of
FS_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
,
CB_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
,
CB_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
consisting of positive-definite multipliers, and completely positive maps, respectively.
By Theorem
8.5
, the functors from Remark
7.14
restrict to isometric functors
m^{\f}:FS_{TA}^{+}(\mathcal{G})\to CP_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
and
m^{\rd}:FS_{TA}^{+}(\mathcal{G})\to CP_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
, and the ranges of these functors consist of fibre-preserving maps in
CP_{TA}(\mathcal{G})

and
CP_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
, respectively.

The next corollary extends the characterisation obtained in the above theorem further in the case of Herz–Schur multipliers.

Corollary 8.7
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle over
X
, carrying a twisted action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of
\mathcal{G}
and let
\varphi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(r^{*}\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
.
Denote by
T^{\varphi}
the Herz–Schur multiplier associated with
\varphi
and by
m_{\varphi}
the associated multiplier map defined as in (
7.2
).
The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

T^{\varphi}
is a positive-definite multiplier for
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
;

(2)

\varphi
takes values in central elements, i.e.
\varphi(g)\in Z\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, and for every
x\in X
and every
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the matrix
\left(\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(\varphi(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}))\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{M}(A_{x}))
is positive;

(3)

\varphi
takes values in central elements, i.e.
\varphi(g)\in Z\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, and for every
x\in X
and every
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}^{x}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the matrix
\left(\alpha_{g_{i}}(\varphi(g_{i}^{-1}g_{j}))\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{M}(A_{x}))
is positive;

(4)

m_{\varphi}
extends to a completely positive map
m_{\varphi}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
;

(5)

m_{\varphi}
extends to a completely positive map
m_{\varphi}^{\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
;

(6)

T^{\varphi}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
for some
\mathcal{E}
,
L
and
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
, which is central in the sense that
a\xi=\xi a
for all
a\in A=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
.

If the above equivalent conditions hold, then
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}=\|\varphi|_{X}\|_{\infty}=\|m_{\varphi}^{\rd}\|=\|m_{\varphi}^{\f}\|=\|\xi\|
.

Proof.

By its form,
T^{\varphi}
is strict and bounded (we have
\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}^{\varphi}\|=\|\varphi|_{X}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}<\infty
).
By Lemma
8.4
(2)
,
T^{\varphi}
is positive-definite if and only if for every
x\in X
and every
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
and
\{a_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}\subseteq A_{x}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the matrix
\left(\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(\varphi(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}))a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{x})
is positive.
This condition implies that for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
, and any
a_{g}\in A_{s(g)}
, the matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\varphi(s(g))&\varphi(g^{-1})a_{g}^{*}\\
\alpha_{g}^{-1}(\varphi(g))a_{g}&\alpha_{g}^{-1}(\varphi(s(g)))a_{g}a_{g}^{*}\end{array}\right)

is positive and, in particular, self-adjoint. This implies
\alpha_{g}^{-1}(\varphi(g))=\varphi(g^{-1})^{*}\in Z\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}_{s(g)})
, and so
\varphi
is central.
When
\varphi
is central, positivity of
\left(\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(\varphi(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}))a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(A_{x})
for any set
\{a_{g_{1}},\ldots,a_{g_{n}}\}\subseteq A_{x}
is clearly equivalent to positivity of
\left(\alpha_{g_{i}}^{-1}(\varphi(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}))\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{M}(A_{x}))
.
This proves the equivalence
(1)
\iff
(2)
.
The equivalence
(1)
\iff
(3)
can be checked analogously, cf. also the considerations before
[
8
, Proposition 4.3]
.

Finally, Theorem
8.5
gives the implications
(6)
\implies
(1)
\iff
(4)
\iff
(5)
and further shows that
(1)
implies that
T^{\varphi}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
for some equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{A}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
and section
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
, which is
(6)
except for the centrality of
\xi
.
To show the last fact assume
(1)
and invoke the construction from the proof of Theorem
8.5
.
By (
8.1
) for any
a\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
we have

\langle\xi,a\xi\rangle_{A}(x)=\langle\xi,\xi\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(A)}(x)a(x),

where
\langle\xi,\xi\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(A)}(x)=\sum_{h,g\in\mathcal{G}^{x}}\alpha_{g}(\varphi(g^{-1}h))
is central in
\mathcal{M}(A_{x})
because
\varphi
is central.
This implies that

\langle\xi,a^{*}a\xi\rangle_{A}=a^{*}\langle\xi,a\xi\rangle_{A}=\langle\xi,a^{*}\xi\rangle_{A}a=a^{*}\langle\xi,\xi\rangle_{A}a=a^{*}a\langle\xi,\xi\rangle_{A},

and consequently
\langle a\xi-\xi a,a\xi-\xi a\rangle_{A}=0
.
Hence
a\xi=\xi a
.
∎

Remark 8.8
.

The positivity condition in
(2)
implies the one in
[
42
, Definition 3.4]
.
In the context of group actions, the positivity condition in
(3)
appears in
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
and
[
8
]
, where the corresponding
\varphi
is called of
positive-type
or
AD-positive-definite
, respectively.

9.
Fourier multipliers

In this section we introduce Fourier multipliers as those Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers which arise from regular equivariant representations.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
We say that a multiplier
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is a
Fourier multiplier
if it can be represented by a regular equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
, i.e. if there exists an equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
and sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})))
such that for any
a\in A_{r(g)}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}
,

(9.1)

T_{g}(a)=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle\xi(t),a\overline{L}_{g}(\zeta(tg))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(B_{r(g)})}.

We denote by
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
the set of all such Fourier multipliers and by
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
the set of Fourier multipliers that can represented in the form (
9.1
) with
\xi=\zeta
.

Note that the last part of the definition is related to a certain subtlety regarding various possible notions of positivity — see Question
12.2
.

Example 9.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle over
X
, carrying a twisted action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
The multipliers described in Example
7.4
, given by sections in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}\mathcal{A})
applied to a Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
, give rise to Fourier multipliers in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]
.
More concretely, given
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}(\mathcal{A}))
, we get the Fourier multiplier

T_{g}(a)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}}\xi(h)^{*}a\alpha_{g}(\zeta(g^{-1}h)),\quad a\in A_{r(g)}.

Specialising further to the case of the trivial bundle
\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{C}\times X
, that is, the case where we have just a twisted groupoid
(\mathcal{G},u)
(see Example
4.12
), given functions
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
, we get from the above formula a multiplier
T
of
(\mathcal{G},u)
, that we view again as the function in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
given by

T_{g}=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}}\overline{\xi(h)}\zeta(g^{-1}h),\quad g\in\mathcal{G}.

Notice that the above function equals the (untwisted) convolution product
\tilde{\xi}^{*}*\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
, where
\tilde{\xi}(g):=\xi(g^{-1})
.
In particular, if
\xi=\zeta
, this is a prototype example of a positive-type function, see
[
11
, Definition 5.6.15]
.

The next proposition shows that the collection of Fourier multipliers has a natural ‘ideal’ property.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{D}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
,
(\beta,u_{\beta})
and
(\gamma,u_{\gamma})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
The Fourier multipliers
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
form an involutive
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-submodule of
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
.
Moreover,
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
equipped with the norm

\|T\|_{F}:=\inf\big\{\|\xi\|\|\zeta\|:T=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}\ \text{for some $(\mathcal{E},L)$ and $\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})))$}\big\},

is a Banach space. Further

\displaystyle FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]\cdot F[(\beta,u_{\beta}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})]\subseteq F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})],

\displaystyle F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]\cdot FS[(\beta,u_{\beta}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})]\subseteq F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})].

In particular,
F(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
is a two-sided ideal of the Banach algebra
FS(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
.

Proof.

The same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
7.10
show that
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is a Banach
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule with norm
\|\cdot\|_{F}
.
For instance, the direct sum of regular equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
is a regular equivariant representation, i.e.
(L\oplus K)^{\mathcal{G}}=L^{\mathcal{G}}\oplus K^{\mathcal{G}}
, or more generally
(\oplus_{i\in I}L_{i})^{\mathcal{G}}=\oplus_{i\in I}L_{i}^{\mathcal{G}}
.
Also, by Corollary
6.14
, we have
L^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes K\cong L\otimes K^{\mathcal{G}}\cong(L\otimes K)^{\mathcal{G}}
, which implies the inclusions in the assertion.
Indeed, if
T_{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi_{1},\zeta_{1}}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
,
T_{\mathcal{F},K,\xi_{2},\zeta_{2}}\in F[(\beta,u_{\beta}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})]
, and

\Gamma:L^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes K\to(L\otimes K)^{\mathcal{G}}
is the unitary from Corollary
6.14
, then using (
7.5
) we get

\begin{split}T_{\mathcal{F},K,\xi_{2},\zeta_{2}}\circ T_{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi_{1},\zeta_{1}}&=T_{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E})\otimes\mathcal{F},L^{\mathcal{G}}\otimes K,\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2},\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2}}\\
&=T_{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F}),(L\otimes K)^{\mathcal{G}},\Gamma(\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}),\Gamma(\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2})}\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})].\end{split}

Hence
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]\cdot F[(\beta,u_{\beta}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})]\subseteq F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\gamma,u_{\gamma})]
, and the other inclusion is proved analogously.
∎

The equality of the norms
\|\cdot\|_{F}
and
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
on the set of Fourier multipliers remains unclear.
See Question
12.1
for further discussion.

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Every Fourier multiplier
T\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
extends to a strict completely bounded map
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
from reduced to full crossed products, and
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}\leq\|T\|_{F}
.
If
T=T_{s^{*}\mathcal{E},L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\xi}\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
, then
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}
is completely positive and
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}=\|T\|_{FS}=\|T\|_{F}=\|\xi\|^{2}
.

Proof.

Write
T:=T_{s^{*}\mathcal{E},L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
where
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
.
Let
\psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\to\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{H})
be a faithful nondegenerate representation on a Hilbert space
H
.
As in the second part of the proof of Theorem
7.13
, we have

(9.2)

m_{T}(a):=\theta_{\xi}^{*}(L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi))(a)\theta_{\zeta},\qquad a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}),

where
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi):\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathbb{B}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}H)
is the induced representation and
\theta_{\xi},\theta_{\zeta}:H\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}H
are creation operators.
By Fell absorption (Theorem
6.16
)
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi)
descends to a representation of the reduced crossed product, and by abuse of notation we may write
L^{\mathcal{G}}\dashind(\psi):\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathbb{B}(\mathrm{C_{0}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\psi}H)
.
Then (
9.2
) implies that
m_{T}
extends to a strict completely bounded map
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
and
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}\leq\|\xi\|\cdot\|\zeta\|
.
In particular,
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}\leq\|T\|_{F}
.
Also if
\xi=\zeta
, then (
9.2
) implies that
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}
is completely positive and
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}=\|\xi\|^{2}
, which also implies
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}=\|T\|_{F}
.
∎

Remark 9.5
.

To use the categorical language, as in Remarks
7.14
and
8.6
, denote by
F_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
and
CB_{TA}^{\rd,\f}(\mathcal{G})
the non-unital categories of all twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
where morphisms in
F_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
are Fourier multipliers, and morphisms in
CB_{TA}^{\rd,\f}(\mathcal{G})
are completely bounded strict
\mathrm{C_{0}}(X)
-bimodule maps from reduced to full crossed products whose composition is defined by
TS:=T\circ\Lambda\circ S
where
\Lambda
is the relevant regular representation.
The extension in Theorem
7.13
gives a contractive involutive
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule functor
m^{\rd,\f}:F_{TA}(\mathcal{G})\to CB_{TA}^{\rd,\f}(\mathcal{G})
.
It restricts to an isometric functor
m^{\rd,\f}:F_{TA}^{+}(\mathcal{G})\to CP_{TA}^{\rd,\f}(\mathcal{G})
, where morphisms in
F_{TA}^{+}(\mathcal{G})\subseteq F_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
are elements of
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
and morphisms in
CP_{TA}^{\rd,\f}(\mathcal{G})\subseteq CB_{TA}^{\rd,\f}(\mathcal{G})
are completely positive maps.

Definition 9.6
.

Given two Fell bundles
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{G}
we define the
support
of a multiplier
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
as
\supp(T):=\overline{\{g\in\mathcal{G}:T_{g}\neq 0\}}
.

Proposition 9.7
.

Compactly supported Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier are Fourier multipliers, i.e. if
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
are
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
, and
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
has compact support, then
