T\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
.

Proof.

Let
K
be a compact subset of
\mathcal{G}
.
We claim that there exist
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})))
such that
T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}),\alpha^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}(g)(a)=a
for all
a\in A_{r(g)}
and
g\in K
.
Indeed, pick
\xi_{0}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
such that
\xi_{0}(x)=1
for all
x\in r(K)
, and
\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
such that
\zeta(g)=1
for all
g\in K
.
Consider the corresponding strictly continuous sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{M}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A})))
given by
x\mapsto\xi_{0}|_{\mathcal{G}_{x}},\zeta_{0}|_{\mathcal{G}_{x}}
, cf. Proposition
7.12
.
Then for
a\in A_{r(g)}
and
g\in K
we have

\begin{split}T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}),\alpha^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}(g)(a)&=\langle\xi(r(g)),a\overline{\alpha^{\mathcal{G}}}_{g}(\zeta(s(g)))\rangle_{\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})}\\
&=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\xi_{0}(t)^{*}a\alpha_{g}\big(\zeta_{0}(tg)\big)=\xi_{0}(r(g))^{*}a\zeta_{0}(g)=a.\end{split}

This proves our claim.

Suppose now that
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
has compact support.
The claim above yields
T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}),\alpha^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
such that
T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{A}),\alpha^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}\circ T=T
.
Thus
T\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
, by Proposition
9.3
.
∎

In terms of maps on crossed products “compactness of the support” means that the range of the map is supported on a precompact open set.
We formalise this as follows.

Lemma 9.8
.

For any Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
\mathcal{G}
and every precompact open set
V\subseteq\mathcal{G}
the subspace
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{V})
of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
is closed both in the reduced and (hence) also in the universal (maximal) norm.

Proof.

Since the reduced norm is dominated by the universal norm it suffices to show that
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{V})
is closed in the reduced norm.
We use the known fact that the inclusion
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
extends to a contractive linear map
j:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, where the codomain is equipped with the supremum norm, see
[
43
, Proposition 7.10]
.
This implies the assertion because
j
is the identity on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V})
and
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V})
is closed in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
∎

Corollary 9.9
.

Let
T=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be a reduced (or full) multiplier from a Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}
to a Fell bundle
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{G}
.
Then
T
has compact support if and only if the range of
m_{T}^{\rd}
(resp.
m_{T}^{\f}
) is contained in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{V})
for some precompact open
V\subseteq\mathcal{G}
.

Proof.

If the range of
m_{T}^{\rd}
(resp.
m_{T}^{\f}
) is contained in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{V})
, then clearly
\supp(T)\subseteq V
.
Hence precompactness of
V
implies compactness of
\supp(T)
.
Conversely, if
\supp(T)
is compact then there is a precompact open set
V\subseteq\mathcal{G}
containing
\supp(T)
.
Since
m_{t}(\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}))\subseteq\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{V})
and
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{V})
is closed in both
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B})
and
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B})
one concludes that the range of the continuous extension of
m_{T}
is also in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{B}|_{V})
.
∎

10.
Approximation properties for multipliers

In this section we turn to the discussion of approximation properties.
We begin in the general context of abstract Fell bundle multipliers, proving a lemma that shows one can always approximately ‘strictify’ a given multiplier.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
T
be a multiplier from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
such that
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|T_{g}\|<\infty
.
Choose an approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
.
Putting

T_{i,g}(a):=T_{g}\big(e_{i}(r(g))a\alpha_{g}(e_{i}(s(g)))\big)

for all
a\in A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
i\in I
,
we obtain a net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of strict multipliers from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
such that
m_{T_{i}}(f)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(f)
for every
f\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

By definition, for
f\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
and
i\in I
we have
m_{T_{i}}(f)=m_{T}(e_{i}fe_{i})
and therefore

\|m_{T_{i}}(f)-m_{T}(f)\|_{\infty}=\|m_{T}(e_{i}fe_{i}-f)\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|T_{g}\|\cdot\|e_{i}fe_{i}-f\|_{\infty}\to 0.

This proves the assertion, as each
T_{i}
is a strict multiplier by construction.
∎

We now specify the context to twisted groupoid actions.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
T
be a multiplier from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
such that
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|T_{g}\|<\infty
.
Choose an approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
. As before put

T_{i,g}(a):=T_{g}\big(e_{i}(r(g))a\alpha_{g}(e_{i}(s(g)))\big)

for all
a\in A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
i\in I
.
Then

(1)

if
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
, then for every
i\in I
we have
T_{i}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
,
\|T_{i}\|_{FS}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
and
\|T_{i}\|_{FS}\to\|T\|_{FS}
(similar statement holds with
FS
replaced by
F
);

(2)

if
T
is positive-definite, then each
T_{i}
is a positive-definite Fourier-Stieltjes multiplier. Moreover we have the estimate
\sup_{i\in I}\|T_{i}\|_{FS}\leq\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}\|
.

Proof.

If
T=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}
, for an equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
and sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}))
then for each
i\in I
we have
T_{i}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,e_{i}\xi_{i},e_{i}\zeta}
.
Clearly
\|e_{i}\xi\|\leq\|\xi\|
, and
\|e_{i}\xi\|\to\|\xi\|
because
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
, as we assume
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondences are non-degenerate.
We have similar relations for
\zeta
and this readily implies the assertion in
(1)
.

Further one can easily check that if
T
is positive-definite, so is
T_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Thus the assertion in
(2)
follows by Theorem
8.5
.
∎

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
and let
L
be an equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}
.
If
T=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi,\zeta}
for sections
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})))
, then there are nets
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\zeta_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
such that
\|\xi_{i}\|\leq\|\xi\|
,
\|\zeta_{i}\|\leq\|\zeta\|
, for every
i\in I
, and putting
T_{i}:=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi_{i},\zeta_{i}}
it follows that
T_{i}
is compactly supported and

m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Proof.

Using Lemmas
10.1
and
10.2
, and by passing to
\xi_{i}:=e_{i}\xi\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
and
\zeta_{i}:=e_{i}\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
for an approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
, we may reduce the proof to the case when
\xi,\zeta\in\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
.
Since
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
is dense in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
we may find nets
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\zeta_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}))
with
\|\xi_{i}\|\leq\|\xi\|
,
\|\zeta_{i}\|\leq\|\zeta\|
for each
i\in I
and
\|\xi-\xi_{i}\|\to 0
,
\|\zeta-\zeta_{i}\|\to 0
.
Then putting
T_{i}:=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\xi_{i},\zeta_{i}}
, we have

\|m_{T_{i}}(a)-m_{T}(a)\|_{\infty}\leq\|\xi-\xi_{i}\|\cdot\|a\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\zeta\|+\|\zeta-\zeta_{i}\|\cdot\|a\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\zeta_{i}\|\to 0

for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Finally, if
\xi
and
\zeta
are supported on precompact bisections
U
and
V\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, respectively, then (
9.1
) implies that
T
is supported on a precompact bisection
U^{*}V
.
Since elements in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
are finite sums of elements with supports in precompact bisections, this proves the compact support assertion, as whenever
\tilde{\xi},\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E})
, the multiplier
T=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}),L^{\mathcal{G}},\tilde{\xi},\tilde{\zeta}}
is compactly supported.
∎

The last lemma can be used to approximate Fourier multipliers by compactly supported ones, but a priori only in the sense of uniform convergence on compact sets.

Proposition 10.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
If
T\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
, then there is a net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of compactly supported multipliers in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
such that
\|T_{i}\|_{F}\leq\|T\|_{F}
for every
i\in I
and
m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Proof.

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
we can find an equivariant representation
L_{k}
of
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle
\mathcal{E}_{k}
and non-zero sections
\xi_{k},\zeta_{k}\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}_{k})))
such that
T=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}_{k}),L_{k}^{\mathcal{G}},\xi_{k},\zeta_{k}}
and
\|\xi_{k}\|\cdot\|\zeta_{k}\|\leq\|T\|_{F}+{1/k}
, so that
\lambda_{k}:=\frac{\|T\|_{F}}{\|\xi_{k}\|\cdot\|\zeta_{k}\|}\leq 1
converges to
1
as
k
tends to infinity.
Thus setting
T_{k}:=\lambda_{k}T=T_{\ell^{2}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}_{k}),L_{k}^{\mathcal{G}},\lambda_{k}\xi_{k},\zeta_{k}}\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
we get
\|T_{k}\|_{F}\leq\|\lambda_{k}\xi_{k}\|\cdot\|\zeta_{k}\|=\|T\|_{F}
and
m_{T_{k}}\to m_{T}
.
Applying Lemma
10.3
to each
T_{k}
one gets the desired net.
∎

We are ready to define an analogue of the property above for arbitrary multipliers.

Definition 10.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
A multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
has the
(positive) Fourier approximation property
if there is a
\|\cdot\|_{F}
-bounded net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
(resp. in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})],(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
) such that
m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for all
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Later we will often use the acronym
AP
for the approximation property.
The next proposition gathers several equivalent conditions for a given multiplier to have the Fourier AP.

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
For a multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
the following are equivalent:

(1)

T
has the (positive) Fourier approximation property;

(2)

there is a
\|\cdot\|_{F}
-bounded net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
(resp. in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})],(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
) of compactly supported multipliers such that
m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
;

(3)

there is a net
\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
acting respectively on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundles
\mathcal{E}_{i}
and sections
\xi_{i}
,
\zeta_{i}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(s^{*}\mathcal{E}_{i})
,
i\in I
, (resp.
\xi_{i}=\zeta_{i}
) such that

(a)

\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{x\in X}\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\langle\xi_{i}(t),\xi_{i}(t)\rangle_{B_{x}}\|\cdot\sup_{x\in X}\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\langle\zeta_{i}(t),\zeta_{i}(t)\rangle_{B_{x}}\|<\infty
,

(b)

for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
the functions

\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle\xi_{i}(t),a(g)L_{g}\zeta_{i}(tg)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}

converge uniformly on compact sets to the function
\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto T_{g}(a(g))\in B_{r(g)}
.

Proof.

Combine Proposition
10.4
and Lemma
10.3
.
∎

Definition 10.7
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
A multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
has the
Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property
if there is a
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
-bounded net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
of compactly supported multipliers such that
m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
If one can find
T_{i}
’s as above which are positive-definite, we speak of the
positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property
.

Remark 10.8
.

By Propositions
9.7
and
10.6
(2)
, the Fourier AP and Fourier–Stieltjes AP differ only in the boundedness condition, formulated respectively in terms of the Fourier and the Fourier–Stieltjes norm.
Since
\|\cdot\|_{FS}\leq\|\cdot\|_{F}
, the
FS
-boundedness is formally weaker than
F
-boundedness.
Hence the Fourier AP implies the Fourier–Stieltjes AP.
It seems reasonable to conjecture that the two notions coincide at least for identity multipliers and positive approximation that we discuss below (see Question
12.1
).
Note also that for the (twisted) actions of discrete groups on unital
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras the Fourier AP was introduced in
[
6
]
under the name
weak approximation property
.

Theorem
8.5
implies the following characterisation of the positive Fourier–Stieltjes AP.

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
For a multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
the following are equivalent:

(1)

T
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property;

(2)

there is a net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of compactly supported and positive-definite (not necessarily strict) multipliers from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
such that
\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{x\in X}\|(T_{i})_{x}\|<\infty
and
m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
;

(3)

there is a net
\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of equivariant representations of
\mathcal{G}
, acting respectively on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundles
\mathcal{E}_{i}
, and sections
\xi_{i}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}_{i})
,
i\in I
, such that
\sup_{i\in I}\|\xi_{i}\|<\infty
, and for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
the functions

\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{r(g)}}\langle\xi_{i}(t),a(g)L_{i,g}\big(\xi_{i}(tg)\big)\rangle_{B_{r(g)}}

converge uniformly on compact sets to the function
\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto T_{g}(a(g))\in B_{r(g)}
.

Proof.

Theorem
8.5
implies the equivalence
(1)
\iff

(2)
modulo strictness of the
T_{i}
s, which can be ignored thanks to Lemma
10.1
.
The same theorem also shows that both conditions are equivalent to a version of
(3)
where sections
\xi_{i}
are in
\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{E}_{i}))
rather than in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}_{i})
, but then as in the proof of Lemma
10.3
we may replace
\xi_{i}
by
e_{j}\xi_{i}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{E}_{i})
for an approximate unit
\{e_{j}\}_{j\in J}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
.
∎

One of the main consequences of the approximation property for a given multiplier is the following result which shows that the multipliers enjoying this property yield (completely) bounded maps between the appropriate
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Let
T
and
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be multipliers from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
such that
m_{T_{i}}(a)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

(1)

If
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
and
M:=\sup_{i\in I}\|T_{i}\|_{F}<\infty
, then
m_{T}
extends to a completely bounded map
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
such that
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}\leq M
and
m_{T_{i}}^{\rd,\f}\to m_{T}^{\rd,\f}
pointwise.

(2)

If
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
and
M:=\sup_{i\in I}\|T_{i}\|_{FS}<\infty
, then
m_{T}
extends to completely bounded maps
m_{T}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
and
m_{T}^{f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
, where
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}
,
\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{cb}\leq M
and
m_{T_{i}}^{\rd}\to m_{T}^{\rd}
,
m_{T_{i}}^{\f}\to m_{T}^{\f}
pointwise.

Proof.

Note first that the uniform convergence on compact sets in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
implies convergence in the maximal
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-norm.
Indeed, for
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
we have
a=\sum_{U\in F}a_{U}
for some
a_{U}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{U})
,
U\in F
, where
F\subseteq\Bis(\mathcal{G})
is finite.
The norm of
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
restricted to
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}|_{U})
,
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
, coincides with the supremum norm.
Therefore
\|m_{T}(a)-m_{T_{i}}(a)\|_{\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})}\leq\sum_{U\in F}\|m_{T}(a_{U})-m_{T_{i}}(a_{U})\|_{\infty}\to 0
.
Assume now that we are given a net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
and
M:=\sup_{i}\|T_{i\in I}\|_{F}<\infty
.
Then
\|m_{T_{i}}(a)\|_{\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})}\leq M\cdot\|a\|_{\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})}
, by Theorem
9.4
, and thus
\|m_{T}(a)\|_{\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})}\leq M\cdot\|a\|_{\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})}
.
Hence
m_{T}
yields a bounded operator
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
with norm not greater than
M
.
Applying this reasoning to matrices, we deduce that
m_{T}^{\rd,\f}
is in fact completely bounded and
\|m_{T}^{\rd,\f}\|_{cb}\leq M
.
This proves
(1)
.
The proof of
(2)
is the same (instead of Theorem
9.4
use Theorem
7.13
).
∎

The next two corollaries are immediate consequences of the above result and appropriate definitions (in the second we also use Lemmas
10.1
and
10.2
).

Corollary 10.11
.

Every multiplier with the Fourier approximation property is reduced to full.
Every multiplier with the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property is both reduced and full.

Corollary 10.12
.

Every bounded positive-definite (not necessarily strict) multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
is both reduced and full (and we have
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{cb}=\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{cb}=\sup_{x\in X}\|T_{x}\|
).

Fourier and Fourier–Stieltjes approximation properties for actions

We will now study the definitions considered above in the case of the identity multiplier and view them as approximation properties of a given twisted groupoid action.

Definition 10.13
.

We say that an action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of a groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
has the
(positive) Fourier
or
Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property
if the identity multiplier of
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
has this property.

Corollary 10.14
.

A twisted groupoid action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property if and only if there is a net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of compactly supported and positive-definite multipliers of
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
such that
\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{x\in X}\|(T_{i})_{x}\|<\infty
and
m_{T_{i}}(a)\to a
uniformly on compact sets for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
10.9
.
∎

Remark 10.15
.

We will focus on the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property as it is (at least formally) weaker than the Fourier approximation property.
Exel’s approximation property
[
28
, Definition 4.4]
, recently generalised to Fell bundles over étale groupoids in
[
39
, Definition 3.1]
and
[
16
, Definition 4.10]
, when formulated for the bundle
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
associated with a twisted groupoid action, means that the identity multiplier has the positive Fourier AP with the approximating net given by coefficients of the regular equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
, see Examples
6.9
,
6.5
.
More precisely, we say that the action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of a groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
has
Exel’s approximation property
if there is a net
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}\mathcal{A})
such that
\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{x\in X}\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{x}}\xi_{i}(g)^{*}\xi_{i}(g)\|<\infty
and for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})=\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}\mathcal{A})
, the net of sections (compare to Example
9.2
)

\mathcal{G}\ni g\longmapsto\sum_{t\in\mathcal{G}^{r(g)}}\xi_{i}(h)^{*}a(g)\alpha_{g}(\xi_{i}(g^{-1}h))\in A_{r(g)}=\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}_{g}\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}

converges uniformly on compact sets to
a
.
Following
[
13
, Definition 3.5]
,
[
67
, Definition 2.2]
,
[
11
, Theorem 5.6.18]
, one could say that the twisted action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
on
\mathcal{A}
is
strongly amenable
if there is a bounded net
\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}Z\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
of central sections of positive-type (see Remark
8.8
) such that
\sup_{g\in G}\|\varphi_{i}(g)a(g)-a(g)\|_{\infty}\longrightarrow 0
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
By Corollary
8.7
, strong amenability implies the positive Fourier–Stieltjes AP.
The diagram

strong amenability
Exel’s AP
pos. Fourier AP
pos. Fourier–Stieltjes AP
Fourier AP
Fourier–Stieltjes AP
weak containment

summarises the general relationships between the discussed approximation properties.
The last implication from the Fourier–Stieltjes AP to the weak containment property will be proved below (Theorem
10.16
).
For a brief discussion of the converse we refer to Question
12.5
.

The next result should be compared to
[
6
, Theorem 5.8]
.

Theorem 10.16
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle over
X
, carrying a twisted action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
Assume
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
has the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property.
Then it has the weak containment property, i.e.
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})=\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, and
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is nuclear if and only if
A=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
is nuclear.

Proof.

For the first part we cannot use Corollary
10.11
, as the Fourier–Stieltjes AP is (at least formally) weaker than the Fourier AP.
We adapt the proof of Bédos–Conti
[
8
, Theorem 4.6]
for crossed products, which was in turn inspired by
[
11
, Theorem 2.6]
for groups.

Recall from Lemma
9.8
that if
V\subseteq\mathcal{G}
is a precompact open set then the subspace
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V})
of
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is closed in the reduced and hence also in the universal (maximal) norm.
Now let
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]
be a net witnessing the approximation property.
For every
i\in I
the support of each
T_{i}
is contained in a precompact open set
V_{i}
, and so
m_{T}:\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V_{i}})
.
By the above claim the extended maps
m_{T_{i}}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V_{i}})
and
m_{T_{i}}^{\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V_{i}})
(that exist by Theorem
7.13
) take values in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
The regular representation
\Lambda:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is the identity on
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, and therefore for every
a\in\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})

m_{T_{i}}^{\rd}(\Lambda(a))=\Lambda(m_{T_{i}}^{\f}(a))=m_{T_{i}}^{\f}(a).

Hence if
\Lambda(a)=0
, then
m_{T_{i}}^{\f}(a)=0
for all
i\in I
, which implies
a=0
because
m_{T_{i}}^{\f}(a)\to a
, see Lemma
10.10
(2)
.
Thus
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})=\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

If
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is nuclear, then so is
A=\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
because we have a conditional expectation
E:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to A
.
To prove the converse implication we adapt Takeishi’s proof of
[
67
, Theorem 4.1]
for amenable groupoids, which in turn was based on
[
11
, Theorem 5.6.18]
.
Assume that
A
is nuclear.
Let
D
be any
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra.
We need to show that the canonical quotient map
Q:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\max}D\rightarrow\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\min}D
is injective.
The first part of the proof of
[
67
, Theorem 4.1]
shows (using nuclearity of
A
) that for any precompact open set
V\subseteq\mathcal{G}
, the quotient map
Q
is injective on
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V})\otimes_{\max}D\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\max}D
.
For the approximating net
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]
, as above, the range of each
m_{T_{i}}^{\f}
is contained in the closed subspace
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V_{i}})
, and therefore
{m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\left({\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\max}D\right)
is contained in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}|_{V_{i}})\otimes_{\max}D
.
Let
a\in{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\max}D
with
Q(a)=0
.
Using that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\max}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\scriptstyle{{m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\max}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\min}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\otimes_{\min}D}
,

we get
Q\circ({m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})(a)=({m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{id})\circ Q(a)=0
.
Since
Q
is injective on the range of
{m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}
this means that
{m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}(a)=0
for all
i\in I
.
Hence
a=\lim_{i}{m}_{T_{i}}^{\f}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}(a)=0
.
This proves that
Q
is injective.
∎

We will now specify the above results to the context of (twisted) groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras.

Corollary 10.17
.

Let
u
be a continuous
2
-cocycle on a locally compact Hausdorff étale groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.
Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

the trivial twisted action
(\id,u)
on the trivial one-dimensional
\mathrm{\mathrm{C}^{*}}
-bundle
\mathbb{C}\times X
has the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property;

(2)

(\id,u)
or, equivalently, the trivial (untwisted) action
\id
on
\mathbb{C}\times X
has the positive Fourier approximation property.

(3)

C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
or, equivalently,
C^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{G},u)
is nuclear;

(4)

C^{*}(\mathcal{G})
or, equivalently,
C^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{G})
is nuclear;

(5)

\mathcal{G}
is amenable.

Proof.

If
(\id,u)
has the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property, then by the previous theorem
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)=C^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{G},u)
is nuclear, and this is known to be equivalent to amenability of
\mathcal{G}
, see
[
67
]
.
But amenability of
\mathcal{G}
is, in turn, equivalent to the existence of a net
\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of positive-type functions in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
that converge pointwise to
1
uniformly on compacts, see
[
2
, Proposition 2.2.13]
or
[
11
, Theorem 5.6.18]
.
This is another way to say that
(\id,u)
or just
\id
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes AP as
FS(\mathcal{G},u)=FS(\mathcal{G})
, see Example
7.11
.
∎

Remark 10.18
.

Most of the statements in the above corollary were already known to be equivalent before, as already indicated in the references throughout the proof, except for the implication from
(1)
to the other statements
(2)
–
(5)
.

11.
Some further applications: Haagerup trick and decomposable norms

The Haagerup trick is a way of producing multipliers of a dynamical system from maps on the associated reduced algebra, applied first for groups by Uffe Haagerup
[
36
, Lemma 2.5]
, later used in many different contexts and in particular generalised to group actions in
[
8
, Proposition 4.12]
and
[
47
, Proposition 3.4]
.
A version of this trick for twisted groupoid actions and bimodule maps was a main tool in
[
42
]
.
In general, for étale groupoids and their actions it is not clear what the Haagerup trick should be.
However when either the groupoid
\mathcal{G}
is discrete or when we consider only bimodule maps the situation becomes much more transparent.
We discuss here these two cases and some of the applications, notably to identifying the Fourier–Stieltjes norm of the associated multiplier.
For the last purpose we will also use the fact that maps coming from a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}
are
decomposable
, that is they are finite linear combinations of completely positive maps, which follows from the usual polarisation formula

T=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{3}i^{k}T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi+i^{k}\zeta,\xi+i^{k}\zeta}

and the multipliers of the form
T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
induce completely positive maps, cf. Theorem
8.5
.
Finally we will use the well-known fact that for any Fell bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
\mathcal{G}
,
the inclusion
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
extends to a contractive injective linear embedding
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
, cf.
[
43
, Proposition 7.10]
.

11.1.
Twisted actions of discrete groupoids

In this subsection we will assume that the groupoid
\mathcal{G}
is discrete.

Lemma 11.1

(Haagerup Trick I)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be Fell bundles over a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.
For any bounded linear map
\Phi:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B})

the formula

T_{g}^{\Phi}(a)=\Phi(a\delta_{g})(g),\qquad a\in A_{g},\,\,g\in\mathcal{G},

where
a\delta_{g}
is a section in
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{\{g\}})
with
a\delta_{g}(g)=a
, defines a multiplier
T^{\Phi}=\{T_{g}^{\Phi}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
with
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|T_{g}^{\Phi}\|\leq\|\Phi\|
.
If
\Phi
is completely positive, then
T^{\Phi}=\{T_{g}^{\Phi}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
is positive-definite.

Proof.

It is clear that the formula above defines a multiplier.
Moreover,
\|T_{g}^{\Phi}(a)\|=\|\Phi(a\delta_{g})(g)\|\leq\|\Phi(a\delta_{g})\|\leq\|\Phi\|\cdot\|a\|
for any
a\in A_{g}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
.
Hence
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|T_{g}^{\Phi}\|\leq\|\Phi\|
.
Assume that
\Phi
is completely positive.
Fix
x\in X
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, elements
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
, and collections
\{a_{g_{i}}\in A_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
,
\{b_{g_{i}}\in B_{g_{i}}:1\leq i\leq n\}
.
The matrix
(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}\delta_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})_{i,j=1}^{n}=(a_{g_{i}}\delta_{g_{i}}*(a_{g_{j}}\delta_{g_{j}})^{*})_{i,j=1}^{n}
is positive in
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}))
and so
b:=\Phi^{(n)}((a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}\delta_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})_{i,j=1}^{n})
is positive in
\mathcal{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathcal{B})_{x}^{\oplus n})
.
Let
\xi=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{g_{i}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathcal{B})_{x}^{\oplus n}
be given by
\xi_{g}(h)=[h=g]b_{g}
, for all
h,g\in\mathcal{G}
.
Then

0\leq\langle\xi,b\xi\rangle_{B_{x}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\langle\xi_{g_{i}},\Phi(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}\delta_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})\xi_{g_{j}}\rangle_{B_{x}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}b_{g_{i}}^{*}\left(T_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}}^{\Phi}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*})\right)b_{g_{j}}.

Hence
T^{\Phi}
is positive definite.
∎

Proposition 11.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.
A multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property if and only if there is a bounded net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of completely positive maps
\Phi_{i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to C(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}|_{V_{i}})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
with ranges supported in finite sets
V_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
,
i\in I
, and such that
\Phi_{i}(a)\to m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Proof.

If
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]
is a net witnessing the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property for
T
then the associated maps
\{m_{T_{i}}^{\rd}\}_{i\in I}
, given by Theorem
7.13
, have the desired properties by Theorem
8.5
and Corollary
9.9
.
Conversely, assume that
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a bounded net of completely positive maps
\Phi_{i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to C(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}|_{V_{i}})
where
V_{i}
is finite.
Then the associated net
\{T^{\Phi_{i}}\}_{i\in I}
given by Lemma
11.1
consists of positive-definite multipliers such that
\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{x\in X}\|(T_{i})_{x}\|\leq\sup_{i\in I}\|\Phi_{i}\|<\infty
.
Moreover,
\Phi_{i}(a\delta_{g})\to m_{T}(a\delta_{g})=T_{g}(a)\delta_{g}
for
a\in A_{r(g)}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, implies

m_{T^{\Phi_{i}}}(a\delta_{g})=T^{\Phi_{i}}(a)\delta_{g}=\Phi_{i}(a\delta_{g})(g)\delta_{g}\to m_{T}(a\delta_{g})(g)\delta_{g}=T_{g}(a)\delta_{g}=m_{T}(a\delta_{g}).

This implies that
m_{T^{\Phi_{i}}}(a)\to m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
∎

Corollary 11.3
.

A twisted groupoid action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a bundle
\mathcal{A}
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property if and only if there is a bounded net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of completely positive maps on
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
that converge pointwise to the identity and that have ranges supported on finite subsets of
\mathcal{G}
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
11.2
to the identity multiplier.
∎

The above results make it more evident that the positive Fourier–Stieltjes AP is a ‘nuclearity condition’ for multipliers.
In particular, if in the characterisation in Corollary
11.3
one replaces ‘ranges are supported on finite subsets of
\mathcal{G}
’ with ‘ranges are finite-dimensional’ one gets a characterisation of nuclearity of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, see
[
19
, Theorem 3.1]
.
We show that in this context ‘finite rank’ implies ‘supported on finite sets’ using the following lemma, which is probably well known to experts, cf. the proof of

[
47
, Theorem 4.3]
.

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
A
and
B
be
C^{*}
-algebras.
Assume
A
is finite dimensional and let
B_{0}
be a dense
*
-subalgebra of
B
.
For every completely positive map
\Phi:A\to B
there is a net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of completely positive maps
\Phi_{i}:A\to B_{0}\subseteq B
such that
\|\Phi_{i}\|=\|\Phi\|
and
\Phi_{i}(a)\to\Phi(a)
for
a\in A
(equivalently
\Phi_{i}\to\Phi
in norm).

Proof.

Since
A
is finite dimensional we have
A=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{M}_{n_{k}}(\mathbb{C})
.
Then each map
\Phi_{k}:=\Phi|_{\mathbb{M}_{n_{k}}(\mathbb{C})}
(with
k=1,\ldots,N
) is contractive and completely positive, and
\Phi(\oplus_{k=1}^{N}a_{k})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Phi_{k}(a_{k})
for
\oplus_{k=1}^{n}a_{k}\in A
.
Thus it suffices to prove the assertion for each
\Phi_{k}
.
Hence we may simply assume that
A=\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
for some
n
.
We may also assume that
\|\Phi\|=1
.

Let
e_{p,q}
,
p,q=1,\ldots,n
, be the canonical matrix units in
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
Since the matrix
(e_{p,q})^{n}_{p,q=1}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C}))
is positive and
\Phi
is completely positive, we get that
(\Phi(e_{p,q}))^{n}_{p,q=1}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)^{+}
.
As
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B_{0})^{+}
is dense in
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)^{+}
, given
\varepsilon_{i}>0
,
i\in I
, with
\varepsilon_{i}\to 0
, there are matrices
(b^{i})_{p,q=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(B_{0})^{+}
such that
\|(\Phi(e_{p,q}))^{n}_{p,q=1}-(b^{i})_{p,q=1}^{n}\|<\varepsilon_{i}/n
.
Define
\Phi_{i}:A\to B
by

\Phi_{i}\big((\lambda_{p,q})^{n}_{p,q=1}\big)=\sum^{n}_{p,q=1}\lambda_{p,q}b^{i}_{p,q}.

By Choi’s Theorem
[
53
, Theorem 3.14]
,
\Phi_{i}
is completely positive.
Since
\|\Phi(e_{p,q})-\Phi_{i}(e_{p,q})\|=\|\Phi(e_{p,q})-b^{i}_{p,q}\|<\varepsilon_{i}/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\to}}0
and
\{e_{p,g}\}_{p,q=1}^{n}
is a basis in
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, we conclude that
\Psi_{i}
converges to
\Psi
pointwise (and hence also in norm as
\dim(A)<\infty
).
Moreover,

|\|\Phi_{i}\|-1|=|\|\Phi_{i}(1)\|-\|\Phi(1)\||\leq\|\Phi_{i}(1)-\Phi(1)\|\leq\sum_{p=1}^{n}\|\Phi(e_{p,p})-b^{i}_{p,p}\|\leq\varepsilon_{i}/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\to}}0.

Thus
\|\Phi_{i}\|\to 1
and therefore replacing
\Phi_{i}
by
\frac{\Phi_{i}}{\|\Phi_{i}\|}
we get the desired net.
∎

Corollary 11.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.
If a map
\Phi:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
is a pointwise limit of a bounded net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of finite rank completely positive maps
\Phi_{i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
,
i\in I
, then the multiplier
T^{\Phi}
from Lemma
11.1
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property.

Proof.

Since
\Phi_{i}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is finite dimensional, by Lemma
11.4
there is a net
\{\phi^{i}_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of norm one completely positive maps
\phi_{j}^{i}:\Phi_{i}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
that converge pointwise to the identity on
\Phi_{i}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
Thus replacing the net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
with
\{\phi_{j}^{i}\circ\Phi_{i}\}_{(i,j)\in I\times J}
we may assume that each map
\Phi_{i}
takes values in the dense
*
-subalgebra
\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
.
Then the finite rank for
\Phi_{i}
implies that the range of
\Phi_{i}
is supported on a finite subset of
\mathcal{G}
.
Hence the assertion follows by Proposition
11.2
.
∎

Remark 11.6
.

The above results (i.e. Lemma
11.1
, Proposition
11.2
, and Corollaries
11.3
and
11.5
, remain valid (with essentially the same proofs) when the reduced crossed products are replaced with full crossed products.
If, in addition, the crossed product for
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
is separable, then nets
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
may be replaced by sequences, and then their boundedness follow from their pointwise convergence.

Theorem 11.7
.

Let
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
be a twisted groupoid action of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a bundle
\mathcal{A}
.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
has the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property and
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
is nuclear;

(2)

(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
has the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property and
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
is nuclear;

(3)

the reduced crossed product
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is nuclear;

(4)

the full crossed product
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is nuclear;

(5)

there is a net of finitely supported positive-definite multipliers
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that for each
i\in I
,
\sup_{x\in X}\|(T_{i})_{x}\|\leq 1
, and for every for every
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
(T_{i})_{g}
is of finite rank and
(T_{i})_{g}(a)\to a
for any
a\in A_{r(g)}
.

If the above equivalent conditions are satisfied, then
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})=\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Proof.

Clearly,
(1)
implies
(2)
, and
(2)
implies
(3)
and
(4)
, as well as
\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})=\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, by Theorem
10.16
.
Suppose now that
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is nuclear.
Equivalently, by
[
19
, Theorem 3.1]
, the identity on
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
is the pointwise limit of finite-rank completely positive contractions.
Modifying this net as in the proof of Corollary
11.5
one gets a net as in
(5)
.
The same argument works for the full crossed product.
Hence either
(3)
or
(4)
implies
(5)
.
Now if
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a net as in
(5)
, it witnesses the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property of
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and the maps
\{m_{T_{i}}|_{\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})}
witness nuclearity of
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
.
Hence
(5)
implies
(1)
.
∎

Remark 11.8
.

The problem of characterising nuclearity of the crossed product for actions on nuclear
C^{*}
-algebras in terms of an amenability condition has received a lot of attention, usually using von Neumann algebra techniques, see
[
3
, Théorème 4.5]
,
[
13
, Corollary 4.11]
,
[
39
, Theorem A]
.
In particular, assuming that
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
is nuclear, the aforementioned results combined with Theorem
11.7
give that:

(1)

If
\mathcal{G}=G
is a discrete group and the twist is trivial, then the Fourier–Stieltjes AP is equivalent to amenability of the action.

(2)

If
\mathcal{G}=G
is a discrete group, then the Fourier–Stieltjes AP is equivalent to Exel’s AP for the twisted action.

(3)

If
\mathcal{G}
is countable,
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
is separable and the twist is trivial, then the Fourier–Stieltjes AP is equivalent to measurewise amenability of the action.

The characterisation of nuclearity of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
in terms of condition
(5)
in Theorem
11.7
is a generalisation of
[
47
, Theorem 4.3]
, stated for discrete group actions on unital
C^{*}
-algebras.

11.2.
Bimodule maps, Herz–Schur multipliers and strong amenability

In this section we consider again a general étale locally compact Haudorff groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.
We will consider however only bimodule maps on a crossed product.

Proposition 11.9

(Haagerup Trick II)
.

Let
(\alpha,u)
be a twisted action of an étale groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
.
To every bounded linear
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
-bimodule map
\Phi:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
there is an associated bounded strictly continuous central section
\varphi_{\Phi}\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(r^{*}Z\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
which is uniquely determined by the recipe

(11.1)

\varphi_{\Phi}(g)a(g)=\Phi(a)(g)\qquad\text{ for any $a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})$ supported on a bisection}.

In particular,
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|\varphi_{\Phi}(g)\|\leq\|\Phi\|
.
If
\Phi
is completely positive then the Herz–Schur multiplier
\varphi_{\Phi}:\mathcal{G}\to r^{*}Z\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})
is of positive type.

Proof.

Fix
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
U\in\Bis(\mathcal{G})
containing
g
.
Let
a_{0}\in A_{r(g)}
and take any
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
and
b\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{s(U)})
such that
a_{0}=a(r(g))=\alpha_{g}(b(s(g))
.
Choose an approximate unit
\{e_{i}^{U}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}|_{r(U)})
.
We will use the notation from Lemma
4.8
.
Thus let
a\delta_{U}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
be given by
(a\delta_{U})(g)=a(r(g))
,
g\in U
.
Clearly
a*(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\to}}a\delta_{U}
and
(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})*\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\to}}a\delta_{U}
uniformly.
Using that both
\Phi
and the embedding
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
(with convolution multiplication) are
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
-bimodule maps we get

\lim_{i}a_{0}\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})(g)=\lim_{i}\big(a*\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})\big)(g)=\lim_{i}\Phi(a*(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})(g)=\Phi(a\delta_{U})(g),

and similarly

\lim_{i}\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})(g)a_{0}=\lim_{i}\big(\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})*\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\big)(g)=\lim_{i}\Phi\big(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U}*\alpha_{U}^{-1}(a)\big)(g)=\Phi(a\delta_{U})(g).

Hence
the following strict limit in
\mathcal{M}(A_{r(g)})

\varphi_{\Phi}(g):=\text{s-}\lim_{i}\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})(g)

exists, is central, and satisfies (
11.1
) (which clearly determines
\varphi_{\Phi}(g)
uniquely).
We have
\|\varphi_{\Phi}(g)\|=\|\text{s-}\lim_{i}\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})(g)\|\leq\sup_{i}\|\Phi(e_{i}^{U}\delta_{U})\|\leq\|\Phi\|
.

Assume now that
\Phi
is completely positive.
Fix
x\in X
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and elements
g_{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{x}
and
a_{i}\in A_{x}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Find sections
\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A})
supported on bisections and such that
f_{i}(g_{i})=\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then
f_{i}*f_{j}^{*}
is supported on a bisection and

(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})(g_{i}{g_{j}}^{-1})=\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{i}a_{j}^{*})u(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})^{*}

for every
1\leq i,j\leq n
, cf. the calculations in the proof of Lemma
8.4
.
Hence by (
11.1
) we get

\Phi(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1})u(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})=\varphi_{\Phi}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1})\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}).

Since the matrix
(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}))
is positive, complete positivity of
\Phi
implies that

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\alpha^{-1}_{g_{i}}\Big(\Phi(f_{i}*f_{j}^{*})(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1})u(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1},g_{j})\Big)\geq 0

(to see this replace
m_{T}
in the proof of Lemma
8.3
by
\Phi
, see also the proof of Lemma
8.4
).
Combining the last two displayed formulas we obtain

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\alpha^{-1}_{g_{i}}\Big(\varphi_{\Phi}(g_{i}g_{j}^{-1})\alpha_{g_{i}}(a_{g_{i}}a_{g_{j}}^{*}))\Big)\geq 0,

which means that the Herz–Schur multiplier
\varphi_{\Phi}
is of positive type, see Lemma
8.4
.
∎

Remark 11.10
.

A similar result for actions of twisted groupoids was proved in
[
42
, Proposition 4.1]
under the assumption that
\mathcal{A}
has a continuous unital section.
Note that the proposition mentioned above should have been stated for bimodule maps (every
*
-preserving, so in particular every completely positive, right
A
-module map is automatically an
A
-bimodule map), and then the proof of Proposition
11.9
can be adapted to get a ‘non-unital version’ of
[
42
, Proposition 4.1]
.
For group actions on unital algebras a version of the above Haagerup trick appears also in the proof of
[
24
, Theorem 3.6]
.

Proposition 11.11
.

Let
(\alpha,u)
be a twisted action of an étale groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
.
The following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

the action
(\alpha,u)
is
strongly amenable
, i.e. there is a bounded net of positive-type sections
\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}Z\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
such that
\sup_{g\in G}\|\varphi_{i}(g)a(g)-a(g)\|_{\infty}\longrightarrow 0
for
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
;

(2)

the identity on
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
is the pointwise limit of a bounded net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of completely positive
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
-bimodule maps
\Phi_{i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})\to C(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)}|_{V_{i}})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
with ranges supported on precompact sets
V_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
,
i\in I
.

Proof.

If
\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
are as in
(1)
then the maps
\{m_{\varphi_{i}}^{\rd}\}_{i\in I}
given by Corollary
8.7
satisfy the properties described in
(2)
.
Conversely, having a net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
as in
(2)
, the corresponding maps given by Proposition
11.9
form a bounded net
\{\varphi_{\Phi_{i}}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(r^{*}Z\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A}))
of Herz–Schur multipliers of positive type.
Moreover, if
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
is supported on a bisection, then
\sup_{g\in G}\|\varphi_{\Phi_{i}}(g)a(g)-a(g)\|_{\infty}=\sup_{g\in G}\lim_{i}\|\Phi(a)(g)-a(g)\|_{\infty}=0
.
Hence this holds for any
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u)})
.
∎

Remark 11.12
.

For the trivial bundle
\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{C}\times X
, that is, the case of just a twisted groupoid
(\mathcal{G},u)
(see Example
4.12
), the above result reduces to amenability of the underlying groupoid
\mathcal{G}
, that is, the existence of a net of positive-type functions
\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{G})
with
\varphi_{i}(g)\to 1
uniformly on compacts.
This is just a re-statement of item
(1)
in the above proposition.
We know that amenability of
\mathcal{G}
is equivalent to nuclearity of
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)=C^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{G},u)
(see Corollary
10.17
), which means that the identity map
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
can be approximated (in the point-norm topology) by a composition of (contractive) completely positive linear maps
C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)\to\mathbb{M}_{n_{i}}(\mathbb{C})\to C^{*}(\mathcal{G},u)
.
This is, in turn, equivalent the approximation property appearing in item
(2)
of Proposition
11.11
.
We are not sure whether this has already appeared in the literature in the way it is presented here.

11.3.
Decomposable norm vs Fourier–Stieltjes norm

As we noted in the introduction to this section, every map given by a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier, whether on the reduced or universal crossed product, can be expressed as a linear combination of completely positive maps.
More interestingly, we can also relate the Fourier–Stieltjes norm of the multiplier to the
decomposable
norm of the associated map, as introduced in
[
35
]
.

We first recall some basic relevant facts.
Suppose that
A,B
are
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras and
\Phi:A\to B
is a linear map.
Haagerup in
[
35
]
proved that following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

\Phi
is
decomposable
, i.e. it can be expressed as a linear combination of completely positive maps from
A
to
B
;

(2)

\Phi=\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}+i(\Phi_{3}-\Phi_{4})
where
\Phi_{k}:A\to B
are completely positive for
k=1,\ldots,4
;

(3)

there exists a completely positive map
\Psi:A\to\mathbb{M}_{2}(B)
of the form

\Psi=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{1}&\Phi\\
\Phi^{\dagger}&\Psi_{2}\end{pmatrix}

Then the maps
\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}:A\to B
are necessarily completely positive.

Moreover, the linear space
DC(A,B)
of decomposable maps from
A
to
B
with the norm

\|\Phi\|_{\mathrm{dec}}:=\sup\left\{\max\{\|\Phi_{1}\|,\|\Phi_{2}\|\}:\Psi=\begin{pmatrix}\Phi_{1}&\Phi\\
\Phi^{\dagger}&\Phi_{2}\end{pmatrix}\text{ is completely positive}\right\}

is a Banach space.
The norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec}}
dominates the completely bounded norm, coincides with the latter on completely positive maps and makes the set of decomposable maps a Banach space.
It is also submultiplicative with respect to composition, see
[
35
]
for more details.
Thus
C^{*}
-algebras with decomposable maps as morphisms, equipped with
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec}}
-norm, form a Banach category which contains the category of
C^{*}
-algebras with morphisms being completely positive maps as a Banach subcategory.

The next result can be viewed as an extension or continuation of Theorem
7.13
.

Proposition 11.13
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
.
For every Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
the corresponding completely bounded maps
m_{T}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
and
m_{T}^{\f}:\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
are decomposable, and we have
\|m_{T}\|_{cb}\leq\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
,
\|m_{T}\|_{cb}\leq\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
.

If
T
is positive-definite, then
\|m_{T}\|_{cb}=\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}=\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}=\|T\|_{FS}
.

Proof.

Let
T:=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\zeta}=\{T_{g}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}
be a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier.
By rescaling the respective sections (passing to
\sqrt{\frac{|\zeta\|}{\|\xi\|}}\xi
and
\sqrt{\frac{|\xi\|}{\|\zeta\|}}\zeta
) we can assume that
\|\xi\|=\|\zeta\|
.
Using Examples
3.6
,
4.13
and
5.11
consider the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence
\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{E})
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha^{(2)},u_{\alpha}^{(2)})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta^{(2)},u_{\beta}^{(2)})}
.
Define the section
\eta:=\xi\oplus\zeta\in\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{E})
in the obvious ‘diagonal’ way and consider the associated Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
\tilde{T}:=T_{\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{E}),L^{(2)},\eta,\eta}
.
The operator
m_{\tilde{T}}^{\rd}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha^{(2)},u_{\alpha}^{(2)})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta^{(2)},u_{\beta}^{(2)})})
is completely positive by Theorem
8.5
.
Recall that we have a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha^{(2)},u_{\alpha}^{(2)})})\cong\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}))

(and its analogue for
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
).
Define the map
\Psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}))
via the formula

\Psi(x)=m_{\tilde{T}}^{\rd}\left(\begin{bmatrix}x&x\\
x&x\end{bmatrix}\right),\qquad x\in\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}).

As the map
x\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}x&x\\
x&x\end{bmatrix}
is completely positive so is
\Psi
.
It is now elementary to check that in fact

\Psi=\begin{bmatrix}m_{T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}}^{\rd}&m_{T}^{\rd}\\
({m_{T}^{\rd}})^{\dagger}&m_{T_{\mathcal{E},L,\zeta,\zeta}}^{\rd}\end{bmatrix}.

Thus, since
m_{T_{\mathcal{E},L,\zeta,\zeta}}^{\rd}
and
m_{T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}}^{\rd}
are completely positive,
m_{T}^{\rd}
is decomposable.
Moreover, as we rescaled to
\|\xi\|=\|\zeta\|
, by Theorem
8.5
we have
\|m_{T_{\mathcal{E},L,\zeta,\zeta}}^{\rd}\|=\|m_{T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}}^{\rd}\|=\|\xi\|\|\zeta\|
.
Thus
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq\|\xi\|\cdot\|\zeta\|
, which shows
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
.
The same argument gives
\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq\|T\|_{FS}
.
The other statements follow easily from Theorem
7.13
and the discussion before the proposition.
∎

Remark 11.14
.

One could similarly show that Fourier multipliers yield decomposable ‘reduced-to-full’ maps, as studied in Theorem
9.4
.

The above result naturally raises the question whether we can identify the decomposable norm of
m_{T}
(say on the reduced crossed product) with the norm
\|T\|_{FS}
.
In the context of the usual Fourier–Stieltjes algebras of discrete groups such an identification was noted only very recently in
[
5
]
.
Below we will show that it remains true in the context of twisted crossed products of
discrete
groupoids.
Before we do that we need a simple (and likely well-known) lemma.

Lemma 11.15
.

Let
A
,
B
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras,
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
If
F
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
B
, then the direct sum
E:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}F
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
, when equipped with the diagonal left action of
A
and the scalar product and the action of
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
given by

\begin{gathered}\big\langle(\xi_{i})_{i=1}^{n},(\zeta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big\rangle_{\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)}:=\left(\langle\xi_{i},\zeta_{j}\rangle\right)_{i,j=1}^{n},\\
(\xi_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\cdot\left(b_{ij}\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}:=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi_{j}b_{ji}\right)_{i=1}^{n},\end{gathered}

where
(\xi_{i})_{i=1}^{n},(\zeta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\in\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}F
,
\left(b_{ij}\right)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
.

Conversely, every
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
(up to unitary equivalence) arises in the way described above from a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
B
.

Proof.

Assume first that
A
and
B
are unital. The proof amounts to a careful check of algebraic formulas, so we will only indicate the main steps needed to establish the second statement.

Suppose that
E
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
.
Denote by
e_{ij}
(
i,j=1,\ldots,n
) matrix units in
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
.
Define
F=Ee_{11}
, and let
\phi:B\to e_{11}\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)e_{11}
be the obvious isomorphism.
It is easy to check that
F
becomes a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
B
, when equipped with the scalar product

\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle_{B}=\phi^{-1}(\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle),

and the action

f_{1}\cdot b:=f_{1}\phi(b),

where
f_{1},f_{2}\in F,b\in B
; note that the left action of
A
is unchanged.
When convenient, we will write the corresponding identification map from
Ee_{11}
to
F
as
\psi_{1}
.
Now given
j\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
we define a map
\psi_{j}:Ee_{jj}\to F
simply by putting

\psi_{j}(e)=ee_{j1},\qquad e\in Ee_{jj}.

Finally we define the map
\Psi:E\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}F
, with the right hand side understood now as a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
defined in accordance with the first statement in the lemma, setting

\Psi(e)=\big(\psi_{i}(ee_{ii})\big)_{i=1}^{n}.

We leave it to the diligent reader to verify that
\Psi
is indeed a unitary equivalence.

Consider now the case where
A
and
B
need not be unital.
Let
E
be again a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
A
to
\mathbb{M}_{n}(B)
.
Then
\mathcal{M}(E)
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
\mathcal{M}(A)
to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{M}_{n}(B))\cong\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{M}(B))
.
By the unital case (and its proof) we have
\mathcal{M}(E)\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{F}
, where
\tilde{F}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence from
\mathcal{M}(A)
to
\mathcal{M}(B)
.
Set
F=\tilde{F}B\subset\tilde{F}
.
It is then a matter of carefully checking that the explicit isomorphism from
\mathcal{M}(E)
to
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{F}
restricts to an isomorphism from
E
to
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}F
.
∎

We will now formulate a version of the above result for
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-correspondence bundles.

Corollary 11.16
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of
\mathcal{G}
, let
\mathcal{E}
be a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathbb{M}_{n}(\mathcal{B})
-correspondence bundle and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then
\mathcal{E}
is unitarily equivalent to the direct sum
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{F}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-correspondence bundle, with pointwise operations described in Lemma
11.15
.
If moreover
\tilde{L}
is an equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{E}
, then we may assume that we also have an equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{F}
and in the equivalence above
\tilde{L}_{g}
corresponds to a direct sum of
L_{g}
.

Proof.

For the first part it suffices to note that the isomorphisms introduced in Lemma
11.15
are clearly continuous with respect to the relevant ‘bundle’ operations.

For the second we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
11.15
, using the second condition in Definition
5.1
and the form of matrix liftings described in Example
4.13
(it essentially suffices to observe that in the unital case for every
g\in\mathcal{G}
the operator
\tilde{L}_{g}
maps
E_{s(g)}e_{11}
into
E_{r(g)}e_{11}
).
Once again we leave the check to the reader.
∎

We are now ready to formulate the main decomposability result.

Theorem 11.17
.

Let
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
be twisted actions of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
respectively on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
.
Then for every Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
we have

\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}=\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}=\|T\|_{FS}.

Proof.

In view of Proposition
11.13
it suffices to prove the inequalities
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}},\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\geq\|T\|_{FS}
,
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\geq\|T\|_{FS}
.
We will prove that
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\geq\|T\|_{FS}
, as the second inequality follows in the same way.
Write then simply
m_{T}
for
m_{T}^{\rd}
and assume that

\|m_{T}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq 1.

We will show that
\|T\|_{FS}\leq 1
.

By the definition of the decomposable norm the displayed inequality means that there exists a completely positive map
\Psi:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathbb{M}_{2}\left(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})\right)
such that

\Psi=\begin{pmatrix}\Phi_{1}&m_{T}\\
m_{T}^{\dagger}&\Phi_{2}\ \end{pmatrix},

with
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
completely positive and such that
\|\Phi_{1}\|\leq 1
,
\|\Phi_{2}\|\leq 1
.
Let us now apply Lemma
11.1
to the map
\Psi
, viewed now as a map from
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
to
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{B})^{(\beta^{(2)},u_{\beta}^{(2)})})
.
This produces a new completely positive map
\tilde{\Psi}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{B})^{(\beta^{(2)},u_{\beta}^{(2)})})
, which comes from a multiplier.
In fact, as the Haagerup trick acts on matrices ‘entrywise’, and does not change maps which are already multipliers, we have

\tilde{\Psi}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\Phi}_{1}&m_{T}\\
m_{T}^{\dagger}&\tilde{\Phi}_{2}\end{pmatrix},

with
\tilde{\Phi}_{1},\tilde{\Phi}_{2}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
contractive completely positive maps.
Applying Lemma
10.1
we may approximate
\tilde{\Psi}
by strict multipliers.
Namely, for an approximate unit
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
, Lemmas
10.1
and
10.2
give a net
\{\tilde{T}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta^{(2)},u_{\beta}^{(2)})]
such that for every
i\in I
we have
\tilde{\Psi}(e_{i}ae_{i})=m_{\tilde{T}_{i}}^{\rd}(a)
,
a\in\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.
Then

m_{\tilde{T}_{i}}^{\rd}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\Phi}_{1,i}&m_{T_{i}}\\
m_{T_{i}}^{\dagger}&\tilde{\Phi}_{2,i}\end{pmatrix},

where
\tilde{\Phi}_{1,i},\tilde{\Phi}_{2,i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
are contractive completely positive maps (coming from Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers) and
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
are such that
\|T_{i}\|_{FS}\to\|T\|_{FS}
(again by Lemma
10.2
).

Fix for the moment
i\in I
.
By Theorem
8.5
the map
\tilde{\Phi}_{i}
is given by a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
\tilde{T}_{i}\in FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta^{(2)},u_{\beta}^{(2)})]
,
\tilde{T}=T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
for some
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{B})
-correspondence
\mathcal{E}
equipped with an equivariant representation
\tilde{L}
of
\mathcal{G}
and section
\xi\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{M}_{2}(\mathcal{E})))
.
Applying Theorem
11.15
to
\mathcal{E}
,
\tilde{L}
and
\xi
we may identify
\mathcal{E}
with
\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{F}
,
\tilde{L}
with a direct sum of two copies of an equivariant representation
L
of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{F}
, and
\xi:=(\zeta,\eta)
for two sections
\zeta,\eta\in\mathrm{C_{b}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{F})
.
Using this identification we obtain

T_{i}=T_{L,F,\zeta,\eta},\;\;\tilde{\Phi}_{1,i}=m_{T_{L,F,\zeta,\zeta}},\qquad\tilde{\Phi}_{2,i}=m_{T_{L,F,\eta,\eta}}.

Thus finally applying the last part of Theorem
8.5
respectively to
\Phi_{1,i}
and
\Phi_{2,i}
we deduce that
\|\zeta\|,\|\eta\|\leq 1
.
But this means that
\|T_{i}\|_{FS}\leq 1
, which ends the proof, as
\|T_{i}\|_{FS}\to\|T\|_{FS}
.
∎

Remark 11.18
.

In the context of the last theorem we could define a version of Haagerup’s decomposable norm for
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
-bimodule maps, say
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec},\mathrm{bim}}
, requesting that all the entries of the requested 2 by 2 matrix decomposition are bimodule maps.
Then the arguments of the above theorem together with the Haagerup trick from Proposition
11.9
would show that even for not necessarily discrete
\mathcal{G}
and arbitrary Herz–Schur type multiplier
T
we have
\|T\|_{FS}=\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec},\mathrm{bim}}=\|m_{T}^{\f}\|_{\mathrm{dec},\mathrm{bim}}
.

Remark 11.19
.

In contrast to Remark
7.14
, where we only had contractive functors, assuming that
\mathcal{G}
is discrete we get isometric involutive
\mathrm{C_{b}}(X)
-bimodule functors
m^{\f}:FS_{TA}(\mathcal{G})\to DC_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
and
m^{\rd}:FS_{TA}(\mathcal{G})\to DC_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
by letting
DC_{TA}(\mathcal{G})
(resp.
DC_{TA}^{\rd}(\mathcal{G})
) to be the category whose objects are twisted actions of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles and morphisms are decomposable maps between full (resp. reduced) crossed products that preserve fibers and are equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec}}
.

As an application (though formally we will use only that the norms
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
are ‘equivalent’) we characterise the Fourier–Stieltjes AP for discrete groupoid actions in a similar fashion as we did for the positive Fourier–Stieltjes AP in Proposition
11.2

and Corollary
11.3
.

Proposition 11.20
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
be
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles over
X
, carrying twisted actions
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.
A multiplier
T
from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
has the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property if and only if there is a
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec}}
-bounded net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of completely bounded maps
\Phi_{i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to C(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}|_{V_{i}})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})})
with ranges supported in finite sets
V_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
,
i\in I
, and such that
\Phi_{i}(a)\to m_{T}(a)
for every
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
.

Proof.

If
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]
is a net witnessing the positive Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property for
T
, then the associated maps
\{m_{T_{i}}^{\rd}\}_{i\in I}
, given by Theorem
7.13
, have the desired properties by Proposition
11.13
and Corollary
9.9
.
Conversely, assume that
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a net of decomposable maps such that
M:=\sup_{i\in I}\|\Phi_{i}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}<\infty
and
\Phi_{i}:\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})\to C(\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}|_{V_{i}})
where
V_{i}
is finite,
i\in I
.
We may write each
\Phi_{i}
as a linear combination
\sum_{k=0}^{3}i^{k}\Phi_{i,k}
(here
i^{k}
is the imaginary unit to the
k
th power, not an index of the set
I
) where each
\Phi_{i,k}
is completely positive and
\|\Phi_{i,k}\|\leq\|\Phi_{i}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}\leq M
, see the formulas in
[
35
, Page 175]
.
Applying Lemma
11.1
to
\Phi_{i}
and
\Phi_{i,k}
we obtain completely positive maps
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i,k}
coming from multipliers and such that
\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{i,k}\|\leq\|\Phi_{i,k}\|\leq M
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}=\sum_{k=0}^{3}i^{k}\widetilde{\Phi}_{i,k}^{(j)}
.
Choosing an approximate unit
\{e_{j}\}_{j\in J}
in
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
and ‘strictifying’ the considered maps by putting
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{(j)}(a):=\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}(e_{j}ae_{j})
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i,k}^{(j)}(a):=\Phi_{i,k}(e_{j}ae_{j})
for
a\in\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, by Lemma
11.1
we get that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i,k}^{(j)}=m_{T_{i,k}^{(j)}}
for
T_{i,k}^{(j)}\in FS((\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta}))^{+}
and
\|T_{i,k}^{(j)}\|_{FS}\leq\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{i,k}\|\leq M
.
Thus putting
T_{i}^{(j)}=\sum_{k=0}^{3}i^{k}T_{i,k}^{(j)}
we obtain a bounded net
\{T_{i}^{(j)}\}_{(i,j)\in I\times J}\subseteq FS((\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta}))
with
\sup_{(i,j)\in I\times J}\|T_{i}^{(j)}\|_{FS}\leq 4M
.
Moreover, for
a\in\mathrm{C_{c}}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
we have
m_{T_{i}^{(j)}}(a)\to m_{T}(a)
because
\Phi_{i}(a)\to m_{T}(a)
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{(j)}(a)\to\Phi_{i}(a)
.
∎

Corollary 11.21
.

A twisted groupoid action
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
of a discrete groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}
has the Fourier–Stieltjes approximation property if and only if there is a
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{dec}}
-bounded net
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of decomposable maps on
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
that converge pointwise to the identity and that have ranges supported on finite subsets of
\mathcal{G}
.

12.
Open questions and further perspectives

In this final section of the paper, we would like to discuss the key questions left open in our work and present some of the perspectives for further research regarding Fourier–Stieltjes algebras and their applications in the context of crossed products by twisted groupoid actions.

Open questions

For the first three questions we work with fixed
(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
and
(\beta,u_{\beta})
, twisted actions of an étale groupoid
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-bundles
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
over
X
.

Question 12.1
.

If
T\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
is a Fourier multiplier, is it true that
\|T\|_{FS}=\|T\|_{F}
?

Note that we always have
\|T\|_{FS}\leq\|T\|_{F}
.
The equality
\|T\|_{FS}=\|T\|_{F}
is known to hold in the classical case of discrete groups, see
[
30
]
; one of possible ways of approaching this question relates to the ‘functional’ picture of Fourier–Stieltjes and Fourier algebras mentioned in the introduction and missing in our context.
In general we do not also know if
F(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
is closed in
FS(\alpha,u_{\alpha})
.
Note that the issue appears already in the context of groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras.
To avoid that, in
[
59
, Definition 1.4]
, Renault defines the Fourier algebra to be a closure in the Fourier–Stieltjes algebra of the coefficients given by the regular representation, and this convention is also adopted by Oty
[
50
]
.
We decided not to do this here.

Question 12.2
.

Is it true that
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]\cap FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha})(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}=F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
?

If
T\in F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
, then
T
is positive-definite, by Theorem
8.5
.
However this theorem does not imply that every positive-definite Fourier multiplier is in
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
, as a priori the associated cyclic equivariant representation of
\mathcal{G}
might not be regular.
On the other hand, if we denote by
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
the set of Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers that can represented in the form
T_{\mathcal{E},L,\xi,\xi}
, then Theorem
8.5
states that
FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]^{+}
consists exactly of positive-definite, bounded, strict multipliers from
\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})}
to
\mathcal{B}^{(\beta,u_{\beta})}
.
Thus

F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]^{+}\subseteq F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]\cap FS[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\alpha,u_{\alpha})]^{+}

and we do not know whether the reverse inclusion holds.

Question 12.3
.

Is it true that
F[(\alpha,u_{\alpha}),(\beta,u_{\beta})]
can be described as the closure of compactly supported Fourier–Stieltjes multipliers?
Either in
\|\cdot\|_{F}
or
\|\cdot\|_{FS}
?

The statement above is true in the context of discrete groups, as shown already in
[
30
]
.
The analogous statement in the context of groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras appears in
[
57
]
, but without proof (only an indication of an argument based on the partitions of unity; however we do not see how to use it to obtain the desired convergence, although in principle one could consider adapting the arguments in the proof of Proposition
10.4
).

Question 12.4
.

Can one find an example of a Fourier–Stieltjes multiplier
T
such that
\|m_{T}^{\rd}\|_{\mathrm{dec}}<\|T\|_{FS}
?

Theorem
11.17
shows that this cannot happen for
discrete

\mathcal{G}
; the proof of that theorem shows that any counterexample would imply a failure of an even weaker form of the Haagerup trick.
However we do not know the answer to the question above even for groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras.

Note that the problems above are clearly interrelated.
For example a positive answer to Question
12.2
would yield, via Remark
11.18
, a positive answer to Question
12.1
for Herz–Schur type multipliers.

The following questions are related to the approximation properties of actions/crossed products, and matters discussed in Section
10
.

Question 12.5
.

In what generality can one invert the arrows in the diagram in Remark
10.15
?

Some of the reverse implications hold in certain special cases, for example for discrete groupoids (see Theorem
11.7
) but are not clear in general.
For instance, strong amenability implies Exel’s AP (which is in turn equivalent to “amenability”) for group actions on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras, see
[
13
, Theorem 4.9]
.
On the other hand, for group actions the implication from weak containment to any of the approximation properties is generally a big mystery although it holds in certain special cases, and there are examples of groupoids that satisfy the weak containment property but are not amenable, see
[
69
]
.

Question 12.6
.

Does nuclearity of the reduced crossed product
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
imply one of the approximation properties in the diagram in Remark
10.15
?

Strong amenability in general is not necessary for nuclearity of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
, which follows for instance from Suzuki’s examples of group actions on unital simple
C^{*}
-algebras in
[
66
]
.
But it might be relevant when the algebra
\mathrm{C_{0}}(\mathcal{A})
is commutative, as for group actions, strong amenability and amenability are equivalent, see
[
3
, Théorème 4.9]
.

By Theorem
11.7
we know that nuclearity of
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\red}(\mathcal{A}^{(\alpha,u_{\alpha})})
implies the positive Fourier–Stieltjes AP when
\mathcal{G}
is discrete.
When
\mathcal{G}=G
is a group nuclearity of the crossed product implies the formally stronger Exel’s AP, see
[
13
, Corollary 4.11]
and Remark
11.8
.
This makes us tend to think that nuclearity may imply Exel’s AP for étale gropouid actions.

Perspectives

One of the deepest modern applications of the Fourier and Fourier–Stieltjes algebras in the classical context of discrete groups is related to using these algebras and their properties as means of distinguishing the corresponding group von Neumann algebras.
A key role is played here by the space of
completely bounded
multipliers of the Fourier algebra of
\Gamma
, denoted
M_{cb}(A(\Gamma))
, which contains the Fourier–Stieltjes algebra
B(\Gamma)
and can be used to define finer, often quantitative approximation properties of
\Gamma
(see for example
[
20
]
,
[
34
]
).
The starting point to study such questions in our context (or even in the context of actions of discrete groups on
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras) one would need to

•

investigate the operator space structure of our Fourier and Fourier–Stieltjes algebras;

•

analyse the corresponding space of (completely) bounded multipliers of the Banach algebra
F[(\alpha,u),(\alpha,u)]
.

The first step is related to Question
12.1
; we would expect the relevant cb-multiplier norm to coincide both with the Fourier norm and Fourier–Stieltjes norm of a given element of the Fourier algebra.
Note also
[
57
, Theorem 3.1]
, which in the groupoid case (trivial action) expresses completely bounded multipliers on the Fourier algebra of
\mathcal{G}
via
invariant
elements of the Fourier–Stieltjes algebra of the product groupoid
\mathcal{G}*\mathcal{G}
.
We expect that in general it might be useful in this context to use the language of
(dynamical) quantum groupoids
, as introduced for example in
[
27
]
.

Another natural direction of study concerns attempting to reconstruct a given groupoid dynamical system from the associated Fourier–Stieltjes or Fourier algebra.
It is well known that from the Fourier algebra
A(\Gamma)
of a discrete group
\Gamma
one can reconstruct
\Gamma
— as a set — via the spectrum (space of characters) of
A(\Gamma)
.
In the articles
[
59
]
or
[
55
]
, dealing with the groupoid operator algebras, analogous questions are often phrased as the study of
duality for étale groupoids
.
This is closely related to the issue of identifying the Fourier or Fourier–Stieltjes algebras with the dual spaces (of some sort) associated with the relevant operator algebras.
As we mentioned in the introduction, in general this is far from clear, and even the case of groupoid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras is not straightforward, as exemplified by
[
57
, Theorem 2.3]
.

Concrete questions one can study are the following:

•

what equivalence relation between (pairs of) twisted groupoid actions leads to the isometric isomorphism of the corresponding Fourier–Stieltjes spaces/algebras?

•

Under what conditions one can for example indeed reconstruct a given twisted groupoid action, up to the equivalence mentioned above, knowing its associated Fourier–Stieltjes algebra?

In the first part one should mention the Morita equivalence of
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-dynamical systems, discussed in
[
9
, Section 4]
and shown to yield — under certain restrictive assumptions — isometric isomorphism of the associated Fourier-Stieltjes algebras.
In our context this can be of course further enriched by looking at different, but equivalent, groupoids, in the spirit of
[
65
, Section 3.4]
.
The second question is naturally far more difficult, and even partial results would be of great interest.

The two broad topics mentioned above clearly do not exhaust the scope of natural questions one can ask regarding our Fourier–Stieltjes algebras.
Just to quote significant body of work in the group case, as presented in
[
37
]
, one could also ask about the general form of (completely) bounded homomorphisms between Fourier–Stieltjes algebras or about the behaviour of these algebras with respect to the restriction to subgroupoids, related to classical spectral synthesis questions.
This brings us to the possibility of dropping the étale assumption, and considering for example the crossed products by actions of arbitrary locally compact groups.
The last step naturally would take us far beyond the scope of current work.
